### 18c LADDER — ROUND 1 FINDINGS (fable-reviewer)

*Cold review, Fable (xhigh), 2026-09-07, over `b3cafb9a..a07addf0` (18c batches 1–3). Read in my own context: CLAUDE.md and the canon it loads; this plan's §1, §3, §4, §5.1, §8–§9, R-0…R-3 with their adjudications; QUESTIONS #91–#93; every file in the diff and the seam/core code it leans on (`BlockRangeIndex.verdict`, `StructuralSeamEdit`, `UndoStack.withCompound`, `revertStructural`/`reapplyStructural`, `BoundarySnapping.seamTrimmedHalves`, `SegmentationRules`, `MarkdownActs`/`MarkdownEditing.returnKey`, `runStructural`/`scheduleCrossBlockCompound`), and the walk captures under `…-screens/18c-2/` (08, 09, 13). Runs on this tree, both green: core `swift test --filter 'NewlineSplittingTests|StructuralSplitAtNewlinesTests|StructuralDivideTests'` → **60 / 3**; Mac `xcodebuild test` over `TypedReturnKeyTests · TypedReturnTests · ReturnKeyTraceTests · SoftNewlineTests · ListContinuationTests · NestedListDoorTests · CapabilityRegistryTests · SplitAtNewlinesDoorTests · FannedDefaultsTests · FannedDefaultsBoardTests` → **126 / 10, zero skipped**. Nothing was edited during the review; this section is the one write. Format: `[severity] · file:line · what · evidence · fix`.*

**Counts: 0 BLOCKER · 2 MAJOR · 2 MINOR · 4 NIT.**

#### Findings, severity-ranked

**[MAJOR] R1-1 · `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Editor/EditorTextView.swift:120–133` (the `.continueList` road of `insertNewline`) with `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Formatting/MarkdownActs.swift:388–401` and `App/Variorum/Shared/EditorBrain/BlockRangeIndex.swift:144–150` · THE LIST DOOR STILL OPENS THE RETIRED TAIL FROM THE KEY. A list typed at a note's END therefore travels the tail road: it crystallizes ONE BLOCK PER ITEM, and ⏎ on its empty item ends the list WITHOUT making a block. §5.1 batch 2's premise *"a tail exists only after a paste/drop now"* and the R-2 record's claim that the `.openTail` arm *"simply becomes unreachable from a typed ⏎ because the intercept acts first"* are both false.**
- **Mechanism (traced):** `insertNewline` computes `list = .continueList` for a texted list line and hands the key to `insertNewlineContinuingLists`; its `.continueList` arm (`MarkdownActs.swift:388–393`) calls the `ordinary` closure, which — seeing `.continueList`, not `.ordinary` — skips `claimTypedReturn()` and runs `super.insertNewline(sender)` (`EditorTextView.swift:130`). That newline reaches `EditorViewModel.shouldChangeText` with `isSoftNewline == false`, so `replacementIsSingleNewline` is true (`EditorViewModel.swift:852–855`), and at the exact document end with no tail `BlockRangeIndex.verdict` answers `.openTail` (`:144–150`) — the Phase 7 gesture U-1 retires. The lead is then inserted at the caret the newline left, i.e. INTO the tail (`MarkdownActs.swift:395–400`). Every later item is typed in the tail, and `SegmentationRules.crystallizedBoundary` (`Sources/VariorumCore/Domain/SegmentationRules.swift:110–128`) mints the previous item the moment the next lead exists (*"a paragraph completes when the NEXT non-blank segment exists"*) — one block per item. ⏎ on the empty item there: `.exitList` deletes the lead, then `claimTypedReturn()` → `TypedReturn.act` sees a tail WITH text → `.content` → nothing. That is R-2's residual 3 — which records the symptom and attributes it to *"the tail road, which this batch does not touch"*; the cause is the key's own list arm.
- **Evidence on this tree:** `ListContinuationTests.theDocumentEndTailGestureStillFiresAndTheLeadLandsInTheTail` (`App/Variorum/VariorumTests/WalkBuildRoundTests.swift:394–409`) is GREEN in my run — it asserts `hasOpenTail == true` and `"- milk\n- "` after a REAL ⏎ at the document end. It is 13k K5's pin of exactly the gesture U-1 retires, and R-2's retired-pins table does not name it. The walk's own captures show the road: `08-list-continues-on-return.png` has the bar reading `open tail` while the list is typed at the end — after step 4 had just proved the plain key opens no tail — and `09`/`13` end with a dangling empty item (`–`) and a bulleted `– alpha` beneath it, not the *"ended the list and made the block"* the record narrates for step 6 (the exact keystroke sequence behind 09 is not recoverable from the captures — SPECULATIVE on that detail only; the tail road is where it happened either way).
- **Degradation:** one gesture, two answers by position — the S4-22 class this letter exists to close: the same three-item list is one block mid-note and three blocks at the end; the empty item's ⏎ makes a block mid-note and nothing at the end. The end of the note is where she types most; U-1's own sentence is *"I want it to work on newlines even when you're in the middle"* — and it now works in the middle but not, for lists, at the end.
- **Fix (the design's own letter — §5.1: *"inside a list, Return continues the list"*, *"no open tail"* — no product call):** in the `.continueList` arm, type the continuation's newline through the soft road — `withSoftNewline { super.insertNewline(sender) }` — at least whenever the verdict would otherwise be `.openTail` (`index.tailLength == nil && caret == documentLength`; the always-soft variant is simpler and also defensible, but inside a PASTED tail it would mint the tail on a list continuation — B2's contract — which changes a K5 behaviour nobody asked to change, so the narrow condition is the honest one). The newline then lands as the last block's content (B1's exact mechanism), the lead follows it inside the block, and K5 reads *"in the same block"* at the end too. Retire `theDocumentEndTailGestureStillFiresAndTheLeadLandsInTheTail` BY NAME under U-1 and rewrite it to the new truth. **Red-on-old pin:** seed `["- milk"]`, real ⏎ at the document end → `!hasOpenTail`, `entries.count == 1`, store `["- milk\n- "]` after flush; type `eggs`, ⏎, ⏎ → the list block whole + the empty block below (`entries.count == 2`, `!hasOpenTail`). Both halves are RED today. The closeout delta for §5.1/R-2 must drop the "unreachable" sentence and say what is true: the plain key never opens the tail; the list door no longer does either.

**[MAJOR] R1-2 · `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Editor/EditorSurfaceController.swift:2385–2420` (`runStructural`; `model.textView?.isEditable = false` at `:2397`, restored in the `defer`) · KEYSTROKES TYPED DURING THE TYPED RETURN'S FLIGHT ARE DROPPED, SILENTLY. R-2 residual 4 asked for this look; the answer is that the window is real, on the hottest key, and unbounded when the writer is busy.**
- **Mechanism (traced):** the key claims the act synchronously (`runTypedReturn`) and the act runs on the NEXT main-actor turn (`scheduleTypedReturn` → `scheduleCrossBlockCompound`, `:1497`); both arms — `insertEmptyBlock` and `invokeGesture(.divideAtCursor)` — enter `runStructural`, which sets `isEditable = false` and then awaits `autosave.flushNow()` (a writer-queue write whenever the buffer is dirty, which it always is right after typing a paragraph), `awaitMintSettle()`, `session.runner.run` (a second writer-queue write; `OperationsRunner` is an actor over the serialized `AppDatabase.write`) and a reader hop, before `applyStructuralResult` and the `defer` re-enable editing. Any `keyDown` in that window → `interpretKeyEvents` → `insertText:` → `NSTextView.shouldChangeText(in:replacementString:)` returns false while the view is not editable → the character is discarded (NSBeep). Before 18c the same ⏎ was a synchronous content insert with no writer hop at the key; now anything else holding the writer — an autosave flush of a large buffer, a sync pull applying its batch in ONE transaction (SyncSpec §5), a purge-janitor pass — stretches the window from milliseconds to that transaction's length. The walk's step 8 measured cliclick's inter-process cadence (tens of ms apart, quiet writer) and saw no drop; it measured neither a burst typist nor a busy writer.
- **Degradation:** *"nothing typed in either app can be permanently lost"* is the repo's own doctrine; a character dropped after a paragraph break, silently, on the key she presses most, is the class the doctrine forbids. Frequency on a quiet machine: unmeasured, bounded by ~three db hops (ms); under a held writer: every keystroke for the transaction's length.
- **Red-on-old pin (deterministic):** in the `TypedReturnKeyTests` harness, hold the writer from a detached task (`Task.detached { try h.database.write { _ in Thread.sleep(forTimeInterval: 0.3) } }` — the writer queue is serialized), press the real ⏎ at the end of `"alpha"`, `await Task.yield()` until `h.model.structural.structuralInFlight`, `h.textView.insertText("x", replacementRange: h.textView.selectedRange())`, then `await h.flush()`; expect the store `["alpha", "x"]` — RED today (`["alpha", ""]`, the `x` gone). The fix's record should also state the measured quiet-machine window.
- **Fix — defer, never drop:** `EditorTextView.keyDown` stashes NSEvents while the surface controller reports a flight in progress (a flag flipped beside `isEditable` in `runStructural`), and the flight's `defer` replays them through `keyDown(with:)` once editing is back — so a replayed character lands at the LANDED caret (the new block, which is where she meant it) and a replayed ⏎ chains through the same deferred seam as today. General to every structural flight (⌘⇧D then typing is the same class), scoped to `keyDown` (key equivalents route through `performKeyEquivalent` and are untouched). If the implementer disputes on frequency, the dispute returns to me; my position is that a silent drop on this key earns the deferral regardless of its rate.

**[MINOR] R1-3 · `App/Variorum/Shared/EditorBrain/TypedReturn.swift:130–141` with `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Formatting/MarkdownActs.swift:376–379, 406–413` (`.exitList` → `deleteWithoutNewline`) and `Sources/VariorumCore/Domain/MarkdownEditing.swift:313` · ⏎⏎ at a list's end leaves the list block with a TRAILING CONTENT NEWLINE (`"- one\n"`), so every list exited by the key carries a permanent blank last line — a visible extra line on the page, and ⏎⏎ after a list differs from ⏎⏎ after prose by exactly that line. Product-undefined; goes to QUESTIONS, not ruled here.**
- **Mechanism:** the first ⏎ (`.continueList`) types `"\n- "`; the second (`.exitList`) deletes ONLY the lead (`returnKey` returns `lead: span.range.lowerBound..<span.content.lowerBound`), leaving `"- one\n"` with the caret after the newline; `TypedReturn.act` then sees `intra == length`, `remainder == ""` → `.emptyBlock(.below)` (`:137–140`) and the newline stays as content. §5.1's *"trailing whitespace ignored"* was written for whitespace SHE typed (⇧⏎); this newline is the list door's own continuation, now retracted. Pinned as the current shape at `ReturnKeyTraceTests.swift:802` (`["- one\n", "", "gamma"]`) and `WalkBuildRoundTests.swift:475` (`["- milk\n", ""]`).
- **Degradation:** the page renders `- one` / (blank line) / paragraph gap / caret — two rendered breaks for a gesture that in prose yields one; the k−1 rule intends k typed newlines → k−1 stored, but here the one stored newline is the retracted item's, not a blank line she asked for. Under K5 alone the empty line was where the caret stayed to continue prose in the same block; under U-1 the caret leaves for the new block and the line is orphaned.
- **Route:** §5.1 (*"a second ⏎ on the empty item ends the list AND makes the block"*) is silent on the retracted item's newline → a QUESTIONS entry with two options: **(a)** the exit arm retracts the whole item it created — the lead AND the newline before it when that line is the block's last — then claims the key, so the block ends at `- one` and ⏎⏎ after a list reads like ⏎⏎ after prose (the spec-defensible default: it retracts the door's own insertion, never hers; red-on-old pin: the two pins above flip to `["- one", "", "gamma"]` / `["- milk", ""]`); **(b)** keep the blank line (it is what a Markdown export wants before a following paragraph) and say so in the field guide. I would build (a) PROVISIONAL if the orchestrator wants a shape landed in this ladder; otherwise the entry alone.

**[MINOR] R1-4 · `App/Variorum/Shared/EditorBrain/TypedReturn.swift:142` with `Sources/VariorumCore/Domain/MarkdownEditing.swift:298` · ⏎ in the MIDDLE of a list item now DIVIDES the list block: `"- one two"` with the caret after `one` → blocks `"- one"` and `"two"`; the second half loses its bullet and the list is no longer one block. §5.1's *"the list door runs FIRST and keeps its behavior: inside a list, Return continues the list"* holds only at the line's END. Product-undefined; goes to QUESTIONS.**
- **Mechanism:** `returnKey(inLines:at:)` claims a list line only when `offset == span.range.upperBound` (`:298`), answering `.ordinary` anywhere else on the line; `insertNewline`'s closure then runs `claimTypedReturn()`; `TypedReturn.act` finds text after the caret → `.divideAtCaret`; the planner's word-edge snap places the cut and `divideRows` mints `"two"` as a fresh block. Under K5 (pre-U-1) the same key was a content newline and the block stayed whole. Not pinned either way today.
- **Route:** U-1's letter (*"inside a block's text, a divide at the caret"*) and §5.1/U-19 (4) (*"lists stay whole"*, *"keeps its behavior"*) pull apart here → a QUESTIONS entry with the options: **(a)** a caret ANYWHERE on a list line is the list door's — `.content` (K5's content newline; the block stays whole); **(b)** a mid-item ⏎ splits the ITEM into two bullets in the same block (Notes/Bear); **(c)** the built shape (divide the block). (a) is the spec-defensible default — it is what "keeps its behavior" says — and one line in `TypedReturn.act` (consult `MarkdownSyntax.isListLead` on the caret's line). Pin whichever she picks: seed `["- one two"]`, caret 5, real ⏎.

**[NIT] R1-5 · `App/Variorum/Shared/EditorBrain/TypedReturn.swift:125–142` · Leading whitespace is not the mirror of trailing whitespace: a caret INSIDE a block's leading run (`"  hello"`, caret 1) answers `.divideAtCaret`; the planner's snap then places a cut whose first half trims to empty and `divideRows` refuses `invalidCut`, spoken — while caret 0 answers `.emptyBlock(.above)` and a caret inside a TRAILING run answers `.emptyBlock(.below)` (`TypedReturnTests.swift:78–88`). Rare (mints trim leading whitespace; list indents are the list door's). Fix: "before any text" = `text[..<intra]` all-whitespace, mirroring the trailing rule; pin it beside `trailingWhitespaceStillCountsAsTheEnd`.

**[NIT] R1-6 · `Sources/VariorumCore/Domain/NewlineSplitting.swift:38–42` and `:101–103` · The header promises the page *"reads byte-identically"* and *"Nothing she typed disappears"*; that is true for pure-newline runs only — a blank line carrying spaces (`"one\n  \ntwo"`) is one break whose seam trim drops the spaces (`seamTrimmedHalves` trims both sides; `bulkSegments`' own normalization, the right rule). Say so in the comment, and add one line that the break rule is `"\n"`-only while the cut brain counts `Character.isNewline` — consistent with the app's `"\n"` separators (Control-Return's U+2028 is content everywhere), stated so the next reader does not "fix" it.

**[NIT] R1-7 · `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Editor/FannedDefaults.swift:39` · `App/Variorum/VariorumMobile/Features/Write/MobileStructural.swift:15` · Both headers still place `KeepOnTopDivide` in `Shared/`; batch 3 moved it to `Sources/VariorumCore/Domain/` (R-3, J9). Two stale pointers to a path that no longer exists.

**[NIT] R1-8 · `App/Variorum/Variorum/Registry/OpsRefusalCopy.swift:34–36, 61–65` rendered at `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Takes/TakesBottomBar.swift:378` · The chip's ON copy (*"Return makes a block wherever the cursor is"*) is Write's truth; on the Takes board's live pane ⏎ is content (`aTakePaneKeepsBothReturnsInTheTake`), and the same help string hovers the Takes bar's chip. Qualify with "in Write" (the `TypingErgonomicsTests` doctrine: prose must not contradict the surface it sits on).

#### Considered and cleared (traced, not findings)
- **Compound nesting around the new one-leg `withCompound(label: "Divide")` legs** (Write `EditorSurfaceController.swift:501`, board `TakesStructureController.swift:562`, phone `MobileStructural.swift:175`) and the "Split at Newlines" compounds: every production `withCompound` site enumerated; none is entered from inside another (the composed Make Block and the cross-block make call `ops.divide`/`ops.divideW` directly, never the keep-on-top door), so `withCompound`'s `precondition` cannot fire.
- **⏎ repeated at a block's START piles up empty blocks above** (`.above` again each time) while ⏎ repeated at its END stops after one (the `.nothing` no-op) — consistent with §5.1's letter (the no-op is for a caret IN an empty block) and with every editor's "push down" gesture; not a finding.
- **A note holding a gap slot** (a placement whose rendered variant is dead — a sync-race transient) makes the note-wide repair refuse through `renderedVariant`'s `noRenderableVariant` — the all-or-nothing grammar #93(c) already carries, spoken.
- **`splitBlockAtNewlines` recomputes `breaks` per leg** (cost ∝ paragraphs × block length): cost ∝ the block being repaired, never the corpus; a summoned act; P1 holds.

#### CLEAN BILL — checked and found solid
1. **`divideW`'s two arms (batch 1) are additive and default-preserving.** Every caller that passes no `displayedVariantID` — the W ceremony (`TakesViewModel.swift:2233`, `EditorSurfaceController.swift:2603`) and the cross-block make (`:1623`, `:1639`) — keeps `context.rendered` byte-for-byte (`anchorID`, `Operations+Structural.swift:477`, falls back for nil and for an id outside `cuts`); the label arm labels only whole-take rides, cut halves stay `nil`; both core pins red-on-old per R-1 and green here.
2. **`KeepOnTopDivide.compose`:** nil unless the displayed take is in the live ACTIVE fan (the F-3 guard); empty other takes are reported, never marked (they would refuse as `invalidCut`); the three app doors and the core op hand it a STORE-read active fan (`Queries.blockFan` filtered), never a projection; the move into core Domain changed no call site (grep) and the phone compiles it as core; the mirror preset parks at 0.
3. **`splitBlockAtNewlines`:** one transaction per block; the fanned arm's W then the in-place chain; ONE undo entry with an exact-row redo built from the same do-time pre-images — `revertStructural` restores C's text to its birth text BEFORE tombstoning so no mint is stamped into the tray, `reapplyStructural` untombstones then restores post-op text (read both bodies); the no-op writes nothing (pinned down to the outbox row and the undo depth); a sealed post refuses through `placedContext`; a locked single take refuses INSIDE the transaction (no half split — pinned); the fanned arm tolerates locks exactly as `divideW` does; comments copy per successor; presentation inherits; every write is an ordinary trigger-outboxed row and `Sources/VariorumCore/Sync/` is untouched (`git diff --stat` empty).
4. **`withCompound`'s all-or-nothing claim (#93(c)) is real:** on a throw the collected inverses run newest-first (`UndoStack.swift:236–246`) — compensation by ordinary tombstones, the recorded compound behaviour, not a transaction rollback; the record's wording ("rolls the whole repair back") is fair.
5. **`NewlineSplitting.breaks`:** UTF-16 offsets from one grapheme pass (no `String.Index` distance loops — P1); breaks exclude leading/trailing runs; the list exception is "exactly one newline between two list leads" and a blank line inside a list breaks (pinned both ways; the S1 revert put it red); every break is an interior, grapheme-aligned cut `seamTrimmedHalves` accepts (pinned).
6. **The two empty-block seam shapes verify against `StructuralSeamEdit`'s guards by reading:** `.below` = `.divide` with `prefixLength == oldRange.length` and the retained-bytes check over the whole old text; `.above` = `.extract(.prefix)` with `remainderLength == oldRange.length`; each inserts exactly one separator and one identity run; `verifyDifferentialOracle` re-checks the landed surface. The empty-tail arm closes the separator through the ordinary `.closeTail` verdict with no store write; the caret-at-last-block-end-under-an-empty-tail case (`theLastBlocksEndUnderAnOpenTailIsStillTheBlock`) leaves a harmless empty tail behind the new block.
7. **The act re-derives on its own turn** (`runTypedReturnAct`), so ⏎⏎ composes (pinned) and a claim that has gone stale degrades to nothing rather than to a divide at a moved offset; `.nothing` in an empty block is consumed and not spoken (U-19 (2)); a selection is not the gesture (#92(a)); marked text and a non-editable surface never claim.
8. **The 13u fence holds:** `BlockRangeIndex.swift`, `CrossBlockEditPlanner.swift` and `Sync/` are untouched (`git diff --stat` empty); the PLAIN key never reaches `.openTail` (pin 4, `neitherKeyOpensTheTailAtTheDocumentEndAnyMore`, `herS1ScenarioMakesABlockAndNeverALooseNewline`) — the list door is the one road left (R1-1).
9. **The text-undo directive** (`removeAllActions()` on every structural rewrite — R-2 residual 1) is necessary as written: an NSUndoManager action registered AFTER the seam would replay at a stale offset after any divide, and the empty-block insert cannot be special-cased without knowing where each action sits. Correctly routed to 13u1.
10. **Registry (P-1):** three declarations, five renderers, availability `noteScoped`/`takeFamily` as the neighbours'; `CapabilityRegistryTests` and `bothBarsRenderTheTwoMenuDoors` green here; no shortcut (not daily chrome, her 13j criterion).
11. **Receipts count from the operations' own results only (J8):** `FannedDefaultsCopy.divided(kept:stayedBehind:)` and `SplitAtNewlinesCopy.receipt` (no-op legs excluded; the empty-take clause names a take that stopped following the block); the copy-truth pins hold both.
12. **Copy:** the tutorial's "blocks & typing" card and the chip's ON / help / global copy describe U-1 (R1-8 qualifies one surface).
13. **R-2 residual 2** (no ⌥⌘Z for a ⏎-made empty block): confirmed — `Operations.newBlock` registers nothing (`Operations+Block.swift:27–43`); a canon extension, correctly hers.
14. **The phone's flip:** `MobileStructural.divide(atSurfaceCaret:)` reads the fan from the store, re-resolves the caret after the flush, composes keep-on-top, lands the caret at the leading successor; its four pins carry the new premise (read). The Takes board's `prepareDivide` hands the same store-read fan to the composition (no second read), and `displayedVariantID` rides from the seed take (#91's built option (a)).
15. **The record's red-on-old claims located:** R-1's two core pins and Revert A's list; R-2's Revert R1 (twelve red, the crash-red honestly named); R-3's S1/S2 reverts — each names pins that exist in the tree and whose assertions match the described reds.

*Dispositions and the fix pass follow the cross-model flow: findings to the implementer verbatim; its agree/dispute back to me; I make the final call and implement my confirmed fixes with their pins, one commit per piece, the suite green afterwards; the implementer's sanity pass over my diff returns to me.*

#### IMPLEMENTER'S RESPONSES (18c round 1)

*Opus, the 18c batches 1–3 implementer (this session built batch 2; batches 1 and 3 are read from their
records and from the tree). Read-only pass — nothing was edited, no suite was run beyond what a trace
needed; every mechanism below was re-traced against `db8b44c4` in my own context before answering.*
**Eight findings: 8 AGREE · 0 DISPUTE.** Four carry a rider — something the trace turned up that the
fix or the entry should carry with it.

**R1-1 (MAJOR — the list door still opens the retired tail at the document end) — AGREE, and the record
of mine it corrects is named.** The mechanism is exactly as traced: `insertNewline` computes the list
verdict FIRST and my claim is gated on `if case .ordinary = list` (`EditorTextView.swift:129`), so a
`.continueList` never reaches `claimTypedReturn()`; `super.insertNewline` then runs with
`isSoftNewline == false`, and `EditorViewModel.shouldChangeText` passes `replacementIsSingleNewline: true`
(`:855`), which at the exact document end with no tail is `.openTail`. **The proof already lives in the
tree and I missed it:** `ListContinuationTests.theDocumentEndTailGestureStillFiresAndTheLeadLandsInTheTail`
(`WalkBuildRoundTests.swift:394`) asserts `hasOpenTail == true` after a REAL plain ⏎ and is green beside my
own `neitherKeyOpensTheTailAtTheDocumentEndAnyMore`, which asserts the opposite for non-list text — two
green pins that disagree about the same key, separated only by whether the caret's line is a bullet. It is
not in my retired-pins table because I never looked for it; the walk SHOWED me the symptom (step 6's bar
reading `open tail` right after step 4 had proved the plain key opens none) and my residual 3 wrote it off
as *"the tail road, which this batch does not touch"* — a true sentence about WHERE it happens that hides
the fact that a TYPED ⏎ is what opens it. §5.1's *"a tail exists only after a paste/drop now"* is therefore
false as shipped, and the honest closeout sentence is the reviewer's: the plain key never opens the tail,
and after this fix the list door does not either. **Rider for the fix pass:** the narrow condition is the
right one for the reason given (the always-soft variant would mint a PASTED tail on a list continuation —
B2's contract, which nobody asked to change), and `aTextedChecklistItemMintsAnUncheckedBox`
(`WalkBuildRoundTests.swift:340`) should stay GREEN through it: it asserts `documentText ==
"- [x] milk\n- [ ] "`, and the soft road produces those same bytes with the newline landing as the last
block's content instead of opening a tail — a useful discriminator that the fix changed the ROUTE and not
the typing.

**R1-2 (MAJOR — keystrokes dropped during the typed Return's flight) — AGREE, with a rider that widens
the fix by one line.** The trace holds: `runStructural` sets `model.textView?.isEditable = false`
(`:2397`) before three awaits — `autosave.flushNow()` (a writer-queue write; the buffer is dirty by
construction right after typing a paragraph), `awaitMintSettle()`, `session.runner.run` (a second writer
write) — plus a reader hop, and AppKit discards typed input to a non-editable text view. My walk's step 8
measured only that `cliclick`'s inter-process cadence with a quiet writer outruns the flight; it measured
neither a burst nor a held writer, and I said so in residual 4 — the reviewer answered the question I
left open, which is what the residual was for. I do not dispute on frequency: the doctrine the repo holds
is that nothing typed is lost, and this is now the app's hottest key.
**RIDER — the stash must be armed at the CLAIM, not inside `runStructural`.** There is a second window
the proposed flag does not cover: `runTypedReturn` claims the key synchronously and the act runs on the
NEXT main-actor turn (`scheduleTypedReturn` → `scheduleCrossBlockCompound`), so a keystroke delivered in
that gap finds the view still EDITABLE and is not dropped — it lands in the OLD block at the old caret,
and the act then re-derives from the moved caret. Traced outcome for `"alpha"` + ⏎ + `x`: the `x` lands at
offset 5 → the block is `"alphax"`, the re-derived act sees `intra == length` → `.emptyBlock(.below)` → the
store ends `["alphax", ""]`. Not a drop but a silent MISPLACEMENT, same class and arguably worse (nothing
beeps). Raising the stash flag in `runTypedReturn` — beside the claim, cleared by the same flight `defer`
— closes both windows with one flag, and the replay then lands every character in the block she meant.
A pin for it is the reviewer's own shape with the insert moved BEFORE the first `await Task.yield()`.

**R1-3 (MINOR — the trailing newline after ⏎⏎ exits a list) — AGREE; route it to QUESTIONS, and option
(a) is a restoration, not an invention.** The mechanism is what my own pins assert
(`ReturnKeyTraceTests.swift:802` → `["- one\n", "", "gamma"]`; `WalkBuildRoundTests.swift:475` →
`["- milk\n", ""]`): `.exitList` retracts the lead only (`MarkdownEditing.swift:313` returns
`lead: span.range.lowerBound..<span.content.lowerBound`), and the newline the CONTINUATION typed a
keystroke earlier stays as the block's content. §5.1 is silent on it, so it is hers.
**Rider for the entry:** under K5 alone that newline was not orphaned — the caret STAYED on the line it
made, and she typed prose there in the same block. U-1 moves the caret into a new block, which is what
turns the line into a leftover. So option (a) (the exit arm retracts the whole item it created — the lead
AND the newline before it when that line is the block's last) restores the gesture's pre-U-1 shape rather
than inventing one, which is worth saying in the entry beside the Markdown-export argument for (b).

**R1-4 (MINOR — mid-item ⏎ divides the list block) — AGREE; route it to QUESTIONS, and the entry should
say what the old key did.** Re-traced: `MarkdownEditing.returnKey` claims a list line only at its END
(`:298`, `guard offset == span.range.upperBound else { continue }`), so a mid-item caret answers
`.ordinary`, my claim runs, and `TypedReturn.act` sees text after the caret → `.divideAtCaret`.
**Rider:** pre-U-1 the same keystroke ALSO broke the item — it typed a content newline, leaving
`"- one"` and an unbulleted `" two"` on the next line inside one block — so the bullet was already lost
mid-item and what U-1 changed is one block becoming two. That makes option (a) (a caret anywhere on a
list line is the list door's, `.content`) an exact restoration of the shipped gesture, and option (b)
(split the item into two bullets) a genuinely NEW behaviour she has never had. Worth stating so the
choice is between "as it was" and "as Notes does it", not between two guesses.

**R1-5 (NIT — leading-whitespace asymmetry in `TypedReturn.act`) — AGREE.** Traced to the end: `"  hello"`
with the caret at 1 answers `.divideAtCaret`; the planner's interior word boundaries are `{2}`, `settle(1)`
picks 2, and `seamTrimmedHalves("  hello", 2)` returns `("", "hello")`, which `Operations.divide` refuses
as `invalidCut` — spoken, not silent, so nothing corrupts; the asymmetry is that caret 0 pushes the
paragraph down and caret 1 refuses. The fix belongs where the reviewer puts it — in the pure decision
(`text[..<intra]` all-whitespace mirrors the trailing rule) rather than in the door, since the decision is
the one place the two edges are stated together.

**R1-6 (NIT — `NewlineSplitting`'s header claims) — AGREE.** Traced the counter-example: `"one\n  \ntwo"`
has one break, and `seamTrimmedHalves` at the run's start gives `("one\n", "two")` — the two SPACES are
gone (`trimLeading` drops them; the k−1 arithmetic counts only newlines), so the page keeps its blank line
but is not byte-identical and something she typed did disappear. `bulkSegments` normalizes the same way,
so the behaviour is right and the sentence is what wants correcting. The second half of the note (the
break rule is `"\n"`-only while `seamTrimmedHalves` counts `Character.isNewline`) is worth stating for the
same reason: it is consistent with the app's `"\n"` separators, and a reader who spots the mismatch would
otherwise "fix" a rule that is deliberate.

**R1-7 (NIT — stale `Shared/` pointers to `KeepOnTopDivide`) — AGREE, and there is a THIRD.** Confirmed
`Sources/VariorumCore/Domain/KeepOnTopDivide.swift` is its home on this tree, and the stale mentions are
`FannedDefaults.swift:39` and `MobileStructural.swift:15` as named — plus `FannedDefaults.swift:17`
(*"in `Shared/`, so both platforms and all three Mac doors share one…"*), which the fix pass should sweep
with them.

**R1-8 (NIT — the chip's ON copy on the Takes bar) — AGREE, and the RECEIPT carries it too.** The copy is
mine (batch 2, correcting prose that still described the pre-U-1 model), and the qualifier is right:
`SegmentationFaceCopy.help` hovers both bars' chips (`CommitBar.swift:632` and
`TakesBottomBar.swift:378`), while ⏎ on a take pane is content and pinned as such
(`aTakePaneKeepsBothReturnsInTheTake` — a pane is a different view class the intercept never sees).
**Rider:** the same overstatement rides `mintOnNote` — *"Return makes a block wherever the cursor is"* —
which `SegmentationFaceCopy.note(asked:effectivelyMints:)` speaks from the Takes board as well
(`TakesViewModel.swift:1411`), so both strings want the "in Write" qualifier, not just the help.

#### IMPLEMENTER'S SANITY PASS (18c round 1)

*Opus, the batches' implementer, over `db8b44c4..9bee1fb1` (six fix commits). Read-only on code — I traced

build (rebuilt first; `io.lduarte.Variorum.debug`, `pgrep` clean before and after, `/Applications` never

refinement of the fix, not a regression against the pre-fix tree; reproduced twice in the walk).*

**R1-1 (the list continuation's soft road) — CLEAR, and confirmed in use.** `onNewlineWouldOpenTail` →
`EditorViewModel.typedNewlineWouldOpenTail()` asks `index.verdict(replacing: selection,
replacementIsSingleNewline: true, …) == .openTail` — a NON-MUTATING call on a value type, reading the
caret's own selection; nothing is written and `BlockRangeIndex` is untouched, so the door and the verdict
cannot drift (the fix's own claim, verified). **Inside a PASTED tail it correctly does not hold:** the
range is contained in the tail region, so `verdict` answers `.tail`, the predicate is false, and the
continuation types its plain newline into the tail exactly as before — B2's mint is not triggered, which
is the narrow-condition rationale kept. Under `one_block` the predicate is false too (the `.openTail`
branch excludes it), so the chip-off note is byte-unchanged. **Walk (captures 01–03):** at a note's END,
`- milk` ⏎ showed `- milk` / `- |` with the bar reading **`block 3/3`** — where my batch-2 walk's capture
08 read **`open tail`** at the same keystroke; `eggs` ⏎ kept it at `block 3/3`; a click on the `- eggs`
line reads `block 3/4` — **both items in ONE block**, the mid-note shape at the end of the note.

**R1-2 (deferred-and-replayed keystrokes) — RAISED (one item), with everything else CLEAR.**

*CLEAR on the interactions asked about:* **⇧⏎** replays correctly — the stash holds the NSEvent and the
replay re-enters `keyDown`, which re-applies `isSoftNewlineKey` → `withSoftNewline`, so a deferred soft
return is still a soft return. **The list door** never interacts with the stash: `.continueList`'s soft
road and `.ordinary`'s content road claim nothing, so no deferral is armed and the newline is synchronous.
**Marked text/IME:** `claimTypedReturn` still refuses under `hasMarkedText()`, so a Return mid-composition
arms nothing; a MENU flight during a composition now defers its keys instead of dropping them, which is
strictly better than before (the only residual is that replayed events carry their original timestamps —
theoretical, and the alternative is the drop the finding exists to end). **Key equivalents** are untouched
(`performKeyEquivalent` precedes `keyDown`). **A flight that REFUSES drains, never holds:** the early
`guard !structuralInFlight` return in `runStructural` happens BEFORE `beginDeferringKeystrokes()` (nothing
armed), and every other road — an op error, a dirty-flush refusal, a planner `.refusal`, the empty block's
`.nothing`, a `.content` re-derivation — is covered either by `runStructural`'s `defer` or by
`runTypedReturnAct`'s. I found no path that arms without a replay. **Walk (capture 04):** ⏎ at the end of
block 1 followed ~1 ms later (both statements in ONE `osascript`) by twenty characters → the whole string
landed COMPLETE in the block the key made, in order — the misplacement the rider traced is gone in use.

***RAISED — the outer replay CLEARS a deferral a replayed ⏎ just re-armed, and the surviving window
REORDERS the keystroke.*** `EditorTextView.replayDeferredKeystrokes()` sets `isDeferringKeystrokes = false`
UNCONDITIONALLY before it drains
(`App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Editor/EditorTextView.swift:126–133`), and
`EditorSurfaceController.runTypedReturnAct`'s `defer { model.textView?.replayDeferredKeystrokes() }`
(`:846`) runs AFTER `runStructural`'s own defer (`:2417`) has already replayed. So when a stashed ⏎ is
replayed, it claims → `EditorViewModel.runTypedReturn` calls `beginDeferringKeystrokes()` for the CHAINED
act (`:1539`) → the inner replay loop finishes → and the outer defer immediately clears that arming with
an empty stash. From there until the chained act's own `runStructural` re-arms, keys are NOT stashed:
they type into the surface the pending act is about to restructure, and the act then re-derives against
them. **Observed twice in the walk** (captures 05 · 06): ⏎ ⏎ + text in one `osascript` (three statements,
~1 ms apart) came out as **⏎ · text · ⏎** — the text in the block the FIRST return made and an EMPTY
block after it, `block 4/7` then `block 6/9` — where the same rig with a single ⏎ (capture 04) preserved
order exactly, and the pinned `twoQuickReturnsMakeOneEmptyBlockNotTwo` (⏎⏎ with nothing following) is
green. The alternative explanation — the letters overtaking the second Return in delivery — is discounted:
the events come FIFO from one process, and probe 04 through the same rig kept its order.
**Severity, honestly:** strictly better than the pre-fix tree (there the same burst DROPPED characters);
what remains is an ordering artifact of the fix's own seam, in a window of one task hop. **Shape offered,
the reviewer's call:** give the flag a generation — `beginDeferringKeystrokes()` bumps a counter,
`replayDeferredKeystrokes(from:)` clears only if the counter is unchanged — or have `runTypedReturnAct`'s
defer replay only on the roads that never reach a flight (`.content`/`.nothing`/a planner refusal), which
is what its own comment says it is for. A pin: the claim-gap pin's shape with TWO real ⏎ events and a
character, asserting `["alpha", "x"]` and ONE new block rather than two.

**R1-3 (the exit's retraction) — CLEAR, and confirmed in use.** `retractListContinuationNewline`
(`EditorViewModel.swift:1548–1590`) is correctly fenced: it runs through the ordinary editing path (the
`closeEmptyTailSeparator` shape), refuses unless the caret sits at its ENTRY's end (the walk over
`index.entries` returns early otherwise, and a caret in the TAIL matches no entry at all, so a tail
newline is never retracted), requires the character behind the caret to be `\n`, and requires the line
before it to satisfy `MarkdownSyntax.isListLead` — so U-19 (2)'s blank line and A11's k−1 seam are both
out of reach, as its two green-on-both guards assert. It runs before the act is read, and the act then
finds the caret at the previous list line's END, which R1-4's narrowing deliberately leaves to the
ordinary rules. **Walk (capture 03):** the empty item's ⏎ ended the list at `- eggs` with NO blank line
after it and the caret in a new block; the prose typed next landed there; the bar read `block 4/4`.

**R1-4 (a caret inside a list line is the list door's) — CLEAR.** `TypedReturn.act`'s guard sits AFTER the
leading-whitespace rule and BEFORE the trailing one, so a list line's very START still pushes the
paragraph down and its END still falls through — which is exactly what the exit road needs.
`lineRange(of:at:)` is O(line) and correct at both boundaries (offset 0 → start 0; a caret just after a
newline → that line's start). `MarkdownSyntax.isListLead` is a whole-line predicate (indent + `"- "`), so
checkboxes are covered and a bare `"-"` is not — consistent with the door's own vocabulary. The pure and
real-key pins pin the four positions.

**R1-5 (leading whitespace mirrors trailing) — CLEAR, with one observation for the record.** The prefix
test subsumes the old `intra == 0` branch, and caret 3 of `"  hello"` still divides. **Observation, not a
raise:** because the leading test now runs FIRST, a block whose text is ENTIRELY whitespace flips from

shape, and an all-whitespace block is nearly unreachable (crystallization trims; a whitespace-only tail
never mints). Recorded so the flip is not discovered as a surprise.

**R1-6 (`NewlineSplitting`'s header) — CLEAR.** I re-traced the counter-example: `"one\n  \ntwo"` cut at
the run's start gives `("one\n", "two")` — the two spaces gone, one newline kept by k−1 — which is what

#### ROUND 1 — THE RAISE'S FINAL WORD (fable-reviewer)

**The raise (R1-2's replay ordering) — CONFIRMED, traced in my own code before ruling.** The mechanism is exactly as the implementer read it: `runStructural`'s `defer` drains the stash; a replayed ⏎ inside that drain claims and re-arms for the chained act A2, and the keys behind it are (correctly) stashed for A2 — but `runTypedReturnAct`'s outer `defer` then cleared the flag unconditionally and drained A2's stash NOW, typing the text before A2 ran; A2 re-derived against the moved caret (`.emptyBlock(.below)`) and minted a spurious empty block. ⏎ ⏎ text → `["alpha", "text", ""]` where `["alpha", "text"]` is the meaning. Strictly better than the pre-fix drop, and an ordering artifact of my seam. **The shape chosen: the generation token** (the implementer's first offer). Every arming names its owner — `beginDeferringKeystrokes()` bumps and returns `deferralGeneration`; `replayDeferredKeystrokes(for:)` acts only when that generation is still the newest. `runStructural` keeps the generation it armed and replays for it; `runTypedReturnAct` reads the CURRENT generation at its start (the claim's — every key since the claim was stashed, never claimed) and replays for that, which a flight's newer arming makes inert on the flight road and leaves live on exactly the roads that never reach a flight — what the `defer`'s own comment said it was for, now true by construction rather than by hope. The second offer (scoping the outer replay by road) collapses into this one: "did a flight run" IS "did a newer generation arm". **Commit `393da766`.** Pin: `TypedReturnKeyTests.twoFastReturnsThenTypingLandTheTextInTheOneBlockTheyMade` (three real key events in one turn: ⏎ ⏎ `x` → `["alpha", "x"]`, two entries, the caret in the new block).
- **Revert RAISE, NAMED BEFORE IT STARTS:** on the committed tree `8660a5d0`, the line `guard generation == deferralGeneration else { return }` removed from `EditorTextView.replayDeferredKeystrokes(for:)` — every replay acts regardless of owner, i.e. the `9bee1fb1` semantics with the token plumbing left in place; `TypedReturnKeyTests` run; restored with `git checkout -- App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Editor/EditorTextView.swift`.
- **The run (revert RAISE in place):** `✘ Test run with 19 tests in 1 suite failed after 1.238 seconds with 3 issues.` — all three on `twoFastReturnsThenTypingLandTheTextInTheOneBlockTheyMade`: the store `["alpha", "x", ""]`, `entries.count → 3`, the caret in the spurious third block — the implementer's walk shape (captures 05/06), reproduced under the rig; the eighteen other U-1 key pins GREEN under the revert. Restored.

**The R1-5 observation (a whitespace-only block flipped `.below` → `.above` at its end) — PINNED AND FIXED, my call.** It is reachable in two keystrokes (⏎ makes an empty block; two spaces; ⏎), and it was a regression of my own prefix rule, so "recorded" was not enough. `TypedReturn.act` now answers `.emptyBlock(.below)` for any caret past the first offset of a block that is nothing but whitespace (the pre-R1-5 answer; the first offset still pushes down, U-1's letter for a block's start); every other block is untouched. **Commit `8660a5d0`.** Pin: `TypedReturnTests.aWhitespaceOnlyBlockStillMakesTheBlockBelow` (`"   "` carets 1…3 → `.below`; 0 → `.above`).
- **Revert OBS, NAMED BEFORE IT STARTS:** on the same tree, the whitespace-only guard (`if clamped > 0, (text as String).allSatisfy(\.isWhitespace) { … .below }`) removed from `TypedReturn.act`; `TypedReturnTests` run; restored with `git checkout -- App/Variorum/Shared/EditorBrain/TypedReturn.swift`.
- **The run (revert OBS in place):** `✘ Test run with 16 tests in 1 suite failed after 0.016 seconds with 3 issues.` — all three on `aWhitespaceOnlyBlockStillMakesTheBlockBelow` (carets 1…3 answering `.above`); the fifteen other pure pins GREEN. Restored.

#### Findings, severity-ranked

**[MAJOR] R2-1 · `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Editor/EditorSurfaceController.swift:855–856` (`runTypedReturnAct` reads the CURRENT `deferralGeneration` and replays for it) with `:2414–2427` (`runStructural` arms and drains its own generation), `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Editor/EditorTextView.swift:143–150` (`replayDeferredKeystrokes(for:)` — "newest generation" is the drain rule), `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Editor/EditorViewModel.swift:1541` (the claim's arming, its generation discarded) and `EditorSurfaceController.swift:1508–1523` (`scheduleCrossBlockCompound` — the chain a claim rides) · THE DRAIN RULE IS "THE NEWEST ARMING HAS ENDED", NOT "THE SURFACE IS IDLE" — so a keystroke compound scheduled INSIDE a drain (a seam ⌫ — A7's carve or #34's seam merge — or a seam-crossing edit) runs AFTER the drain's ⏎ has claimed, and that compound's own flight then drains the keys typed after the ⏎ BEFORE the ⏎'s act has run. Typed text lands on the wrong side of a paragraph break, silently — the exact misplacement class R1-2's rider was adopted to close, re-opened one seam over by the fix's own replay.**
- **Mechanism (traced):** a replay is a synchronous same-turn burst by construction (`for event in events { keyDown(with: event) }`, `EditorTextView.swift:149`). Seed `["alpha"]`, caret at 5; she presses ⏎ ⌫ ⏎ x faster than the first act lands — one turn in the harness, or any burst that arrives while the first flight waits on the writer (R1-2's own held-writer scenario). ⏎₁ → `insertNewline` → `.ordinary` → `claimTypedReturn` → `runTypedReturn` → `.emptyBlock(.below)` → `beginDeferringKeystrokes()` (gen 1) → A1 scheduled; ⌫ ⏎₂ x → stashed. A1: `deferral = 1`; `insertEmptyBlock` → `runStructural` arms gen 2, lands `["alpha", ""]` with the caret in the empty block; its `defer` → `replayDeferredKeystrokes(for: 2)` → newest → drains: **⌫** → `deleteBackward:` → `shouldChangeText` on the separator → `.refuse(.crossesBlockBoundary)` → `planEmptyBlockDeletion` (entry 1: empty, one take, nothing unshown) → C1 **scheduled** via `scheduleCrossBlockCompound` (chained behind A1's still-running task — nothing armed, nothing run yet); **⏎₂** → `keyDown` (flag down) → `insertNewline` → claim → `typedReturnAct()` = `.nothing` (the caret is in the empty block) ≠ `.content` → arms gen 3 → A2 scheduled behind C1; **x** → stashed (gen 3). A1's outer `defer` (gen 1 ≠ 3) is inert, as the raise's fix intends. **C1 runs:** `runEmptyBlockDeletion` → `runStructural` arms gen 4 → the carve tombstones the empty block, the caret lands at the END of `alpha` (`caretLandingAfterRemoving(1)`: no block below, so the block above's end, `:1148`) → its `defer` → `replayDeferredKeystrokes(for: 4)` → **newest → drains `[x]` → `"alphax"`**. A2 runs: `deferral = 4`; re-derives at the caret after `x` → `.emptyBlock(.below)` → a flight (gen 5) → `["alphax", ""]`. **Result `["alphax", ""]`; the meaning of ⏎ ⌫ ⏎ x is `["alpha", "x"]`** (a block, its removal, a block, the letter in it). The generation token answers "did a newer flight arm" and nothing else: a claim whose act is PENDING behind another compound is invisible to the compound's drain. Pre-fix (`db8b44c4`) the same burst DROPPED all three keys against the disabled surface; pre-U-1 there was no claim — so the misplacement is this fix's own, not a pre-existing shape moved.
- **Degradation:** the repo's doctrine (*"nothing typed … can be permanently lost"*) is honored — nothing drops — but R1-2's stated contract at `EditorTextView.swift:111–114` (*"a character lands at the landed caret — the block she meant"*) is false whenever a drain carries a refused-keystroke compound before a ⏎: a seam backspace, a seam merge, a whole-block delete, a cross-block or visible-take edit, all followed by Return and text, typed into a busy writer (an autosave of a large buffer, a sync pull's one-transaction apply — the R1-2 window). Backspace-then-Return-then-typing is the ordinary "wrong break, redo it" sequence.
- **Red-on-old pin (deterministic, the `TypedReturnKeyTests` harness):** seed `["alpha"]`; `caret(h, entry: 0, offset: 5)`; `returnKey`; a real Delete key event through `keyDown` (`keyCode: 51`, `characters: "\u{7F}"` — the standard binding routes it to `deleteBackward:`); `returnKey`; `characterKey("x")` — all four in one turn; `await h.flush()`; expect the store `["alpha", "x"]`, `entries.count == 2`, the caret in entry 1. **RED on this tree: `["alphax", ""]`**, the letter in the block above the break. (A second shape, same mechanism: seed `["alpha", "beta"]`, the same keys → the carve lands the caret at `beta`'s START (`:1147`), the drained `x` makes `"xbeta"`, and A2 speaks the divide door's "nothing to divide" refusal — `["alpha", "xbeta"]` where the meaning is `["alpha", "", "xbeta"]`.)
- **Fix — drain when IDLE, never when "newest":** replace the generation with the state the seam already has. `EditorTextView`: `beginDeferringKeystrokes()` raises the flag; `replayDeferredKeystrokes()` clears it and drains, unconditionally. `EditorSurfaceController` gains ONE gate, `replayDeferredKeystrokesIfIdle()` = `guard !structuralInFlight, pendingCrossBlockTask == nil else { return }; model.textView?.replayDeferredKeystrokes()`, called from exactly two places: `runStructural`'s `defer` (last, as today — it declines while a compound is pending and the compound's completion drains) and the tail of `scheduleCrossBlockCompound`'s task, right after `pendingCrossBlockTask = nil` when the task is the last in the chain (`pendingCrossBlockToken == token`). `runTypedReturnAct`'s own `defer` goes (the task tail covers every road — a refusal, `.nothing`, a stale `.content`). Traced against every shape: ⏎ x (the claim gap) → A1's flight declines (its task is pending), A1's tail drains → `["alpha", "x"]`; ⏎ ⏎ x (the raise) → A1's tail drains ⏎₂ x: ⏎₂ claims and re-arms, x is stashed, A2's tail drains it → `["alpha", "x"]`; ⏎ ⌫ ⏎ x (this finding) → A1's tail drains: ⌫ schedules C1, ⏎₂ claims A2 behind it, x is stashed; C1's flight declines (A2 pending), C1's tail is not last; A2 re-derives at `alpha`'s end → the block below; A2's tail drains x into it → `["alpha", "x"]`; a MENU flight during a pending act → whichever finishes last drains (`structuralInFlight` false AND the chain empty), so the disabled surface never sees a replay; the held-writer flight (R1-2's pin) unchanged. The harness's `flush()` already loops over a chained schedule replacing the handle, so a drain inside a task tail that schedules a new compound is awaited. The R1-2 pins, the raise's pin and this one must all be green after; the revert for this pin's red is "restore the generation check" (the `fa88acd3` semantics).

**[MAJOR] R2-2 · `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Editor/EditorViewModel.swift:1533–1535` (`runTypedReturn(afterListExit:)` retracts BEFORE the chip is consulted) with `:1560–1593` (`retractListContinuationNewline` — no segmentation guard) and `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Editor/EditorTextView.swift:211` (the exit road claims with `afterListExit: true` unconditionally) · UNDER THE CHIP OFF (`one_block` — every JOURNAL note by seed, and U-19 (3)'s ruled "Return is content again, as today"), ⏎ ON AN EMPTY LIST ITEM NOW DELETES THE LINE SHE IS STANDING ON, AND ⏎ CAN NO LONGER EXIT A LIST AT ALL: the exit's lead deletion runs, R1-3's retraction removes the continuation's newline too, and only THEN does `typedReturnAct()` answer `.content` and decline — so the caret lands at the END of the previous item, where the next ⏎ is `.continueList` again. `"- one\n- "` ⏎ → `"- one"` ⏎ → `"- one\n- "` ⏎ → `"- one"`, forever. A regression of 13k K5's shipped exit (which left her on the empty line to type prose) in the one mode where U-1 does not move the caret — and R1-3's own rationale (*"U-1 moves the caret into a new block, which is what turned the line into a leftover"*) does not apply there.**
- **Mechanism (traced):** `insertNewline` → `listReturn` = `.exitList(lead)` → `deleteWithoutNewline` → `"- one\n"`, caret 6 → `claimTypedReturn(afterListExit: true)` (`isEditable`, no marked text) → `runTypedReturn(afterListExit: true)` → `retractListContinuationNewline()`: entry 0 length 6, caret == its end ✓; `character(at: 5) == "\n"` ✓; the line before is `"- one"`, `isListLead` ✓ → `shouldChangeText` (`.block(0)`, ordinary content) → `replaceCharacters` → `"- one"`, caret 5. Then `guard typedReturnAct() != .content` — `TypedReturn.act`'s first line returns `.content` for `.oneBlock` — → `return false`. No act, no block; the line is gone. Under the chip ON the same road claims `.emptyBlock(.below)` and the retraction is exactly R1-3's design; under the chip OFF nothing follows it.
- **Degradation:** every journal note (`profile='journal'` seeds `one_block`, AppSpec §7) and every note she turned the chip off in: a list cannot be ended by Return. The escape is ⇧⏎ (a soft newline bypasses the list door), which nobody is told. This is spec drift against a ruling (U-19 (3): *"the chip OFF … makes Return content again, as today"*) introduced by `50e32cd7`.
- **Red-on-old pin (the chip-off harness shape, `withEditor(profile: .journal, …)`):** seed `["- one\n- "]`, `#expect(effectiveSegmentation == .oneBlock)`, caret at 8, real ⏎, `await h.flush()`; expect the store `["- one\n"]` (K5's exit: the lead goes, no newline typed, the caret on the empty line — `entries.count == 1`, `!hasOpenTail`). **RED on this tree: `["- one"]`.** Green on `db8b44c4` (before R1-3) and on 13k K5's tree.
- **Fix:** gate the retraction on the chip — `if afterListExit, header?.effectiveSegmentation != .oneBlock { retractListContinuationNewline() }` — the same first rule `TypedReturn.act` applies (`TypedReturn.swift:92`), so the retraction runs only where the key WILL claim (after it, a caret at a list line's end with nothing after it answers `.emptyBlock(.below)` by `act`'s own rules — the pure pin `aCaretInsideAListLineIsTheListDoorsAndStaysContent` holds that arm). Cleaner still, and the same one line: read the act's chip guard once in `runTypedReturn` and return `false` before touching the surface. The three re-pointed R1-3 pins and its two guards stay green (all chip ON); QUESTIONS #94(a)'s entry gains the sentence that the retraction is chip-ON only.

**[NIT] R2-3 · `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Editor/EditorViewModel.swift:1589–1592` with `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Formatting/MarkdownActs.swift:378–381` (`.exitList` → `deleteWithoutNewline`, no undo group) · The exit's lead deletion and R1-3's retraction are two separate text-undo registrations for one keystroke, against K5's own rule for the continuation (*"the two edits ride ONE undo group, so ⌘Z takes back the keystroke she pressed rather than half of it"*, `MarkdownActs.swift:365–366`). Moot in practice under the chip ON — the act's flight clears the text-undo stack on every structural rewrite (R-2 residual 1) — and, after R2-2, never reached under the chip OFF. Recorded so the next reader who relaxes the undo-clear directive (13u1's) finds the gap named; no pin needed (clarity).

#### Considered and cleared (traced, not findings)
- **The generation token's two pinned shapes are right by trace:** ⏎ x in one turn (the claim gap) and ⏎ ⏎ x (the raise) — A1's flight owns the drain, a replayed ⏎ re-arms for A2, the outer `defer` is inert (gen 1 ≠ 3), A2's own `defer` drains. R2-1 is the shape where a NON-claim compound sits between the claim and its act, which the token cannot see; the two pins stay green under R2-1's fix.
- **The claim-time arming for `.nothing`** (⏎ in an empty block arms, schedules a no-op act, and the act's `defer` drains one hop later) — one Task per empty-block Return; negligible and correct.
- **`runStructural`'s early `guard !structuralInFlight`** returns BEFORE arming, so a refused overlapping flight never leaves the flag up — the sanity pass's reading holds. A menu act (⌘⇧⏎, ⌘⇧D — key equivalents, never stashed) pressed while a typed act is in flight is refused-and-spoken as before U-1.
- **`isSoftNewline` has exactly ONE consumer** (`EditorViewModel.shouldChangeText:859` — B1's `replacementIsSingleNewline` withholding), so R1-1's soft road changes the VERDICT and nothing else: no mint, no k−1, no crystallization consults the flag. `typedNewlineWouldOpenTail` is `verdict`'s own non-mutating answer — false inside a pasted tail (`.tail`), false under an empty leftover tail (`tailLength == 0` ⇒ not `.openTail`; the caret at the last block's end reads `.block(last)`, the newline is content, the lead follows), false under `one_block`, never asked with a selection (`listReturn` answers `.ordinary` for one). B2's mint cannot fire from the soft road there (the verdict is `.block(last)`, never `.tail`).
- **`retractListContinuationNewline`'s three fences** hold as recorded: the caret's ENTRY (a tail caret matches no entry and returns), the caret at that entry's END, a `"\n"` behind it, and `isListLead` on the line before — U-19 (2)'s blank line and A11's mid-block seam are unreachable (both guard pins green here). A newline SHE typed with ⇧⏎ before a hand-typed `"- "` is indistinguishable from the continuation's and is retracted the same way — the outcome equals the ordinary road's, not a finding.
- **R1-4's rule order in `act`** (whitespace-only block → leading run → list line short of its end → trailing run → divide): a list line's start still pushes down, its end is the door's, the exit road reaches `act` only at a line end with nothing after it (→ `.emptyBlock(.below)`) or with prose below (→ `.divideAtCaret`, the guard pin's `["- a\n", "more"]`); `lineRange` is O(line) and correct at both edges. `act`'s `.content` for a list line can never coincide with the open-tail verdict (the caret is short of the line's end, so never at the document end).
- **The whitespace-only guard (`8660a5d0`)** sits before the prefix rule and is scoped by `clamped > 0`; offset 0 still `.above`; an EMPTY block is `.nothing` before either. `allSatisfy` short-circuits on the first non-whitespace character — O(1) for prose, O(block) only for a whitespace block.
- **⏎ repeated at a block's START** piles empty blocks above; at its END stops after one — round 1's reading, unchanged.
- **`applyPendingIfClean()` before the replay in `runStructural`'s `defer`**: a projection applied first can move the caret; the replayed keys then land there — the same order a projection and a key have always had.
- **The 13u fence holds through the fix range:** `BlockRangeIndex.swift`, `CrossBlockEditPlanner.swift` and `Sources/VariorumCore/Sync/` are absent from `git diff --stat db8b44c4..fa88acd3`; the only core file touched is `NewlineSplitting.swift`'s header comment (R1-6), no core behaviour moved (core 1493/162 recorded, consistent with batch 3's close).

#### IMPLEMENTER'S RESPONSES (18c round 2)

*Opus, the 18c implementer, read-only over `714a5ead` — every mechanism re-traced in my own context
against the tree (the deferral seam whole: `EditorTextView.swift:100–152`, `EditorViewModel.runTypedReturn`
`:1533–1545` and `retractListContinuationNewline` `:1560–1593`, `EditorSurfaceController.runTypedReturnAct`
`:848–868`, `runStructural` `:2405–2432`, `scheduleCrossBlockCompound` `:1508–1523`,
`planEmptyBlockDeletion`/`runEmptyBlockDeletion` `:1822–1834` and `caretLandingAfterRemoving` `:1145–1150`,
`MarkdownActs.insertNewlineContinuingLists`, `MarkdownEditing.returnKey`, `TypedReturn.act`).*
**Four findings: 4 AGREE · 0 DISPUTE.** Both MAJORs are mine by inheritance — R2-1 is the shape my own
sanity-pass raise's fix answered too narrowly, and R2-2 is a hole in a fix I cleared last round.

**R2-1 (MAJOR — the drain rule is "newest arming", not "idle") — AGREE; I re-traced the whole burst and it
lands where the finding says.** Step by step on `["alpha"]`, caret 5, keys ⏎ ⌫ ⏎ x in one turn: ⏎₁ claims
and arms **gen 1**, A1 is scheduled, the other three stash. A1 → `insertEmptyBlock` → `runStructural` arms
**gen 2**, lands `["alpha", ""]` with the caret in the empty block, and its `defer` drains for gen 2 (the
newest) — synchronously, inside A1's own task. In that drain: **⌫** at offset 6 proposes exactly the
separator `{5,1}` → `.refuse(.crossesBlockBoundary)` → the A7 carve's precondition holds on a
just-minted empty block (one live take, nothing unshown, unsealed) → **C1 scheduled**, nothing armed;
**⏎₂** finds the caret in the empty block → `.nothing` ≠ `.content` → arms **gen 3**, A2 scheduled behind
C1; **x** stashes for gen 3. A1's outer `defer` is inert (1 ≠ 3) — the raise's fix behaving exactly as
designed. Then **C1** runs, `runEmptyBlockDeletion` → `runStructural` arms **gen 4**, the carve tombstones
the block and `caretLandingAfterRemoving(1)` takes the `i > 0` arm (`:1148`) — the caret to `alpha`'s END —
and its `defer` drains **for gen 4, which is newest, but the stash belongs to gen 3**: `x` types into
`"alpha"` → `"alphax"`. A2 then re-derives at that caret → `.emptyBlock(.below)` → `["alphax", ""]`.
**The finding's own summary is exact.** The generation answers "has a newer arming begun", and the stash's
OWNER is a different question — a claim whose act is queued behind a non-claim compound is invisible to
that compound's drain.
**Riders for the fix pass.** (1) **Retire the generation with the rule it implemented.** If the drain
becomes `replayDeferredKeystrokesIfIdle()`, `beginDeferringKeystrokes`'s `@discardableResult Int` and
`replayDeferredKeystrokes(for:)`'s guard should go with it — two answers to "when does the stash drain"
living side by side is how this seam earned its second finding, and the idle gate subsumes the newest-gen
one (a newer flight is never idle). (2) **I traced the proposed fix against the three shapes and it
holds:** ⏎ x → A1's flight declines (its own task pending), A1's task tail drains → `["alpha", "x"]`;
⏎ ⏎ x → A1's tail drains ⏎₂ (which re-arms) then stashes x, A2 is a `.nothing` road with no flight and its
task tail drains → `["alpha", "x"]`; ⏎ ⌫ ⏎ x → A1's tail drains ⌫ (schedules C1) and ⏎₂ (schedules A2,
arms), x stashes; C1's flight and tail both decline (A2 pending, token moved); A2 re-derives at `alpha`'s
end, makes the block, and its tail drains x into it → `["alpha", "x"]`. The drain inside a task tail runs
after `pendingCrossBlockTask = nil`, so a compound scheduled BY the drain re-populates the handle and the
harness's `flush()` loop (`EditorTestSupport.swift:121–126`) still awaits it. (3) **A second pin worth
landing beside the finding's:** the same four keys on `["alpha", "beta"]` — the carve then takes
`caretLandingAfterRemoving`'s OTHER arm (`:1147`, the block below's start), so the drained `x` makes
`"xbeta"` and A2 meets the divide door's refusal; one pin per landing arm keeps the next reader from
reading the fix as caret-position-specific. (4) **One small correction to the finding's parenthetical,
which changes nothing in it:** on `db8b44c4` the same burst dropped all three keys only in the
held-writer variant — in ONE TURN the surface was still editable there, so ⌫ ⏎ x acted immediately on the
pre-flight text (a different mangling, not a drop). The finding's own claim — that the misplacement is the
fix's own and not a moved pre-existing shape — is unaffected: pre-U-1 there was no claim at all.

**R2-2 (MAJOR — the chip-OFF list can no longer be exited by Return) — AGREE; this is a hole in a fix I
cleared last round, and I traced it end to end.** Under `one_block` (`profile: .journal` by seed — AppSpec
§7 — and any note where she turned the chip off): `MarkdownActs.listReturn` is deliberately
chip-INDEPENDENT (13k K5 shipped lists in both modes; it consults only `isEditable` and the caret), so
`.exitList` fires and `deleteWithoutNewline` retracts the lead → `"- one\n"`, caret 6. The exit road then
claims unconditionally (`EditorTextView.swift:211`) → `runTypedReturn(afterListExit: true)` retracts
BEFORE any chip question (`:1534`) → `retractListContinuationNewline`'s four fences all pass (entry 0,
caret at its end, `"\n"` behind it, `"- one"` is a list lead) → `"- one"`, caret 5 → and only THEN
`typedReturnAct()` answers `.content` (`TypedReturn.swift:92`, the chip's own first rule) and the claim
returns false. Net: the line she was standing on is gone, no block is made, and the caret sits at the end
of a list line — where the next ⏎ is `.continueList` again, so `"- one\n- "` ⇄ `"- one"` forever. On
`db8b44c4` (before R1-3) the same keystroke left `"- one\n"` with the caret on the empty line, which is
K5's shipped exit. **A ruled regression** (U-19 (3): *"the chip OFF … makes Return content again, as
today"*), and R1-3's own rationale — the caret LEAVES for a new block, orphaning the line — is exactly
what does not happen under the chip.
**Riders.** (1) The gate belongs on the RETRACTION, not on `listReturn`: the door is chip-independent by
design and both modes must keep K5's continue/exit/outdent. (2) Of the two shapes offered I would take the
second — read the chip once in `runTypedReturn` and return `false` before touching the surface — because
it makes the order *decide, then act* on every road, not only this one; the first shape leaves a
surface-mutating call ahead of the decision for the next edge to fall into. (3) Under the chip ON the act
after an exit can never be `.content` (the caret is at a list line's END, which R1-4's guard excludes, and
the entry is non-empty), so the gate costs nothing there — the three re-pointed R1-3 pins and its two
guards stay green by construction. (4) #94(a)'s entry wants the sentence the finding names: the retraction
is chip-ON only, and under the chip OFF the exit is K5's, unchanged.

**R2-3 (NIT — two undo registrations for one keystroke) — AGREE, and it is moot for a reason worth
writing down.** `MarkdownActs.deleteWithoutNewline` (`:376–381`) and `retractListContinuationNewline`
(`:1589–1592`) each run the ordinary trio with no `beginUndoGrouping`, against K5's own rule for the
continuation (`MarkdownActs.swift:365–366`). It cannot bite today: under the chip ON the act that follows
is always a structural flight (after a retraction the block is non-empty, so `.nothing` is unreachable and
`.content` is excluded by R1-4's line-end rule), and every structural rewrite clears the text-undo stack
(R-2 residual 1); after R2-2's fix the retraction never runs under the chip OFF at all. So the gap is
real, unreachable, and exactly the kind that becomes live the day 13u1 relaxes the undo-clear directive —
which is why naming it in the comment is the right disposition and a pin would be pinning nothing.

**R2-4 (NIT — two unstated residuals of the deferral) — AGREE, both verified.** (i) `runStructural` drops
only `isEditable` (`:2409`); `isSelectable` stays true — which is what lets her scroll and select while a
flight lands — so a CLICK during a flight moves the caret immediately while the keys wait, and the replay
then lands them at the clicked position. Order inverted between a key and a click, never a drop; worth the
comment precisely because the fix's own sentence promises "the block she meant". (ii) The stash is
`keyDown`-scoped by construction, so input that arrives as `insertText:` with no key event — dictation,
Services, an accessibility insert — is still discarded by the disabled surface exactly as before R1-2.
Correctly scoped (stashing at `insertText:` would swallow the replay itself and every programmatic edit);
saying so in the comment is the whole fix.

#### ROUND 2 DISPOSITIONS + FIX PASS (fable-reviewer)

*The reviewer's final call on each round-2 finding after the implementer's pass (4 agree · 0 dispute · six riders, every rider adopted — my own trace agreed with each), and the fix pass run in this same session per the cross-model flow. Rules kept: one commit per piece, `git pull --rebase` then push after each; every bug-class fix with its red-on-old pin by BEHAVIOURAL REVERT on the committed tree, the revert state NAMED here before it starts and the tree restored with `git checkout --` afterwards; nothing in `Sources/VariorumCore/`, nothing in `BlockRangeIndex`, nothing in `Shared/` (the phone compiles nothing new — no iOS build owed).*

**R2-1 (MAJOR — the drain rule was "the newest arming has ended", not "the surface is idle") — CONFIRMED; the implementer's riders (1) retire the generation with its rule, (3) a second pin for the carve's other landing arm, (4) the parenthetical correction — ALL ADOPTED.** On (4) the implementer is right and my finding's parenthetical was wrong in one clause: on `db8b44c4` a same-TURN burst was not dropped — the surface was still editable in the claim gap, so ⌫ ⏎ x acted at once on the pre-flight text (a different mangling); only the held-writer variant dropped. The finding's claim that the misplacement is the fix's own stands: pre-U-1 there was no claim. **Fix (`0ce1040f`):** `EditorTextView` keeps the flag and the stash and loses the generation (`beginDeferringKeystrokes()` arms; `replayDeferredKeystrokes()` drains unconditionally and is called from ONE place); `EditorSurfaceController.replayDeferredKeystrokesIfIdle()` is the one drain rule — `guard !structuralInFlight, pendingCrossBlockTask == nil` — called from `runStructural`'s `defer` (last) and from the compound chain's tail (`scheduleCrossBlockCompound`'s task, right after `pendingCrossBlockTask = nil` when it was the last in the chain); `runTypedReturnAct`'s own `defer` is gone (the tail covers every road). #### IMPLEMENTER'S SANITY PASS (18c round 2)

*Opus, the 18c implementer, over `4b8720b2..dfc195c6` (`0ce1040f` · `722b2109`). Read-only on code; every
mechanism re-traced in my own context, and the three shapes the coordinator named walked on the dev-store
Debug build (rebuilt first; `io.lduarte.Variorum.debug`, `pgrep` clean before and after, `/Applications`
never launched, my instance quit with ⌘Q). **Four items: 4 CLEAR · 0 RAISED.** Two observations recorded
inside the items, neither worth a finding.*

**R2-1 (the idle gate) — CLEAR. I went looking for a stranded stash and could not build one.** The
armings are exactly two and each is inseparable from a drainer: the CLAIM
(`EditorViewModel.swift:1548`) arms and on the very next line schedules
(`scheduleTypedReturn` → `scheduleCrossBlockCompound`), whose task tail runs the gate for whichever task
is last in the chain (`EditorSurfaceController.swift:1517–1525`); a FLIGHT (`:2430`) installs its `defer`
— which ends in the gate (`:2447`) — before its first `await`. The two gate conditions are exact
complements, so a decline at one site is a promise from the other: a flight declines only while the chain
is non-empty (and that chain's last tail drains), a tail declines only while a flight is up (and that
flight's defer drains). I traced both interleavings — flight-finishes-first and tail-runs-first — and each
ends in a drain.
*The specific roads asked about:* **a refused flight** — `runStructural`'s `guard !structuralInFlight`
returns BEFORE the arming (`:2422` vs `:2430`), so it arms nothing, and the enclosing act's claim is
drained by the chain tail (I traced the case where the OTHER flight is a menu act: whichever of the two
finishes last finds the surface idle). **A thrown compound** — the scheduled bodies are `async -> Void`
and catch their own errors (`runStructural` returns `false` on a throw), so no body can skip its tail;
nothing anywhere cancels `pendingCrossBlockTask` (grep: assigned `:1509`, cleared `:1517`, read
`EditorViewModel.swift:554/557` and forwarded `:1703` — no `.cancel()`), so a scheduled tail always runs.
**Events stashed with no flight ever pending** — they drain on the queued act's own turn, never wait for
the next key: the `.content` / `.nothing` / spoken-refusal roads have no flight, and the tail (which
round 1's now-deleted `runTypedReturnAct` defer used to stand in for) runs for every scheduled body. I
traced `.nothing` explicitly — ⏎ in an empty block claims, arms, schedules, does nothing, and its tail
clears the handle and drains.
*Two observations, recorded rather than raised:* (i) the gate reads `pendingCrossBlockTask`, the handle
EVERY keystroke compound shares (the seam merges, the whole-block delete, the cross-block and
visible-take edits, `runSoftNewlineInTail`) — so an unrelated compound queued for its own reason holds
the stash until it completes. That is the rule's intent (idle means the chain is empty) and is bounded by
that compound's own life; worth knowing when reading the gate. (ii) The tail drains AFTER
`pendingCrossBlockTask = nil`, so a compound scheduled BY the drain captures `previous == nil` and does
not await the task whose tail is running — harmless (the tail is synchronous, so the new task cannot
start until it returns) and the harness's `flush()` loop follows the re-populated handle.

**R2-2 (decide, then act) — CLEAR, including the timing question.** The guard is the FIRST statement of
`runTypedReturn` (`EditorViewModel.swift:1544`), ahead of both the retraction and the act, so under
`one_block` nothing touches the surface — which is what makes the chip-OFF exit K5's again. **The chip
read's timing vs the claim:** it reads `header?.effectiveSegmentation`, the same source and the same
value `TypedReturn.act` consults (`TypedReturn.swift:92`), synchronously at the keystroke — one source,
no window between them, and the guard's outcome is behaviourally identical to the `.content` road it
short-circuits, so no other caret position changes. *Observation, not a raise:* if the chip were toggled
BETWEEN the claim and the act's turn, the act re-derives `.content` and does nothing while the keystroke
was already consumed — one lost newline in a window no hand can hit (a chip toggle between a keystroke
and the next main-actor turn); the same class as any re-derivation, and the re-derivation is the design.

**R2-3 (the two undo registrations) — CLEAR.** The paragraph landed on `retractListContinuationNewline`
with the unreachability spelled out (a flight always follows a retraction; the flight clears the stack;
after R2-2 the retraction never runs chip-OFF) and names the day it goes live (13u1 relaxing the
undo-clear directive). Comment-only, as dispositioned.

**R2-4 (the deferral's two residuals) — CLEAR.** Both are in the seam's rewritten doc comment
(`EditorTextView.swift:100–130`): the click-during-a-flight order inversion (`isSelectable` stays true —
verified, only `isEditable` drops at `:2426`) and the `insertText:`-only input still discarded, with the
