#### Findings, severity-ranked

**[MINOR] R3-1 · `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Editor/EditorViewModel.swift:546–559` (`finalizeWork` awaits the pending compound handle ONCE) with `App/Variorum/Variorum/VariorumAppDelegate.swift:58–68` (the quit door), `App/Variorum/Variorum/MacWillLock.swift:44–46` (the lock door), `EditorViewModel.swift:2278` (`toggleSeal`) and `:534` (teardown) · THE QUIT/LOCK/SEAL DOORS AWAIT ONE LINK OF THE COMPOUND CHAIN, AND THE DRAIN INSIDE THAT LINK'S TAIL CAN FORGE THE NEXT LINK: a stashed ⏎ (or a seam ⌫) replayed by the awaited task's tail schedules a NEW compound and re-populates `pendingCrossBlockTask`; `finalizeWork` never re-reads the handle, so that act — and every key stashed behind it — is still in the view when `reply(toApplicationShouldTerminate: true)` fires. Keys she typed end with the process: the R1-2 doctrine (*"nothing typed … can be permanently lost"*), on the quit road, one seam past where R2-1 closed it.**
- **Mechanism (traced):** seed `["alpha"]`, caret 5; she types ⏎ `x` ⏎ `y` inside one flight window and presses ⌘Q. ⏎₁ claims → arms → A1 scheduled; `x` ⏎₂ `y` stashed. `applicationShouldTerminate` → `finalizeWork()` → the guard passes (`pendingCrossBlockTask != nil`, `:553`) → its Task captures `pending` = A1 (`:557`) and awaits it. A1's flight lands `["alpha", ""]`; its `defer` declines (A1 pending); A1's TAIL (`EditorSurfaceController.swift:1516–1524`) clears the handle and drains: `x` lands in the empty block; ⏎₂ claims at `x`'s end → `.emptyBlock(.below)` → arms → **A2 scheduled — the handle re-populated** (`:1509`, `previous == nil`); `y` stashed for A2. A1 completes; `finalizeWork` resumes from the ONE await it holds, runs `autosave.flushNow()` (`:560`) and the mint loop, returns; the delegate replies terminate. A2 — a real flight, at least two writer hops behind `finalizeWork`'s one — is mid-air or unstarted; `y` sits in `deferredKeyEvents`, drained only by A2's tail. **The harness already knows this shape:** `EditorTestSupport.flush()` (`:121–126`) loops precisely because *"a chained schedule replaced the handle"*; the production doors do not.
- **Why the ordinary quit shapes are safe (traced, so the reach is honest):** ⏎ ⌘Q — A1 is the one link, awaited; ⏎ `x` ⌘Q — `x` drains INSIDE A1's tail, before A1 completes, and `finalizeWork`'s `flushNow` writes it; ⏎ ⏎ `x` ⌘Q — A2 is the `.nothing` road with no `await`, enqueued ahead of `finalizeWork`'s continuation, so its tail drains `x` before the door moves on. The loss needs a SECOND act that is itself a flight (a ⏎ after typed text; a seam ⌫'s carve) stashed inside the first act's window, keys behind it, and the door pressed inside that same window — hand-unreachable on a quiet writer (a few ms), reachable under a held writer (a sync pull's one-transaction apply, R1-2's own scenario). Pre-U-1 those keys were synchronous content and landed; on `db8b44c4` (pre-R1-2) they were dropped against the disabled surface — so this is R1-2's window narrowed to the quit door, not a new class. **MINOR, not MAJOR:** ⌘Q is not the hottest key and is never pressed mid-thought; the fix is one loop. Recorded for the letter's closeout — owner 13u1 (the seam's own item) or a 17 entry at her word. *(Neighbour, pre-existing and NOT this finding: a MENU flight up at quit is not awaited by any door — `finalizeWork`'s guard reads the dirty flags and the handle, never `structuralInFlight`; the flight's op sits in the writer queue at process exit. Older than U-1; named so the fixer sees the shape beside it.)*
- **Red-on-old pin (`TypedReturnKeyTests`; expected RED on this tree by the ordering above — the fix pass that lands it proves it, or dismisses the finding as hollow per the guard):** seed `["alpha"]`; `caret(h, entry: 0, offset: 5)`; `returnKey` · `characterKey("x")` · `returnKey` · `characterKey("y")` — four real key events in one turn; then `await h.model.finalizeWork()?.value` — THE DOOR'S OWN CALL, never `h.flush()`; then, with no further await, `#expect(h.model.pendingCrossBlockTask == nil, "the door left a link of the chain un-awaited")` and `#expect(!h.textView.isDeferringKeystrokes, "keys were still stashed when the door said done")`; then `await h.flush()` and `#expect(try h.storeSlots().map(\.text) == ["alpha", "x", "y"])`. On this tree the first two read A2 pending and the flag up (`y` stashed); after the fix both hold and the store reads three blocks.
- **Fix:** `finalizeWork`'s Task awaits the chain the way the harness does — `while let pending = pendingCrossBlockTask { await pending.value; if pending == pendingCrossBlockTask { break } }` ahead of its `flushNow` (`:557–560`), so a link forged by a drain is awaited in its turn and its tail drains the stash before the buffer is flushed. All four doors inherit it through the one call. No arming or draining site changes; the idle gate is untouched.

**[NIT] R3-2 · `App/Variorum/VariorumTests/ReturnKeyTraceTests.swift:644–652` · The raise's pin (`twoFastReturnsThenTypingLandTheTextInTheOneBlockTheyMade`) still narrates the RETIRED mechanism in its doc comment — *"An arming now names its owner and a replay acts only for the newest"* — the generation token R2-1 removed. The pin is green under the idle gate for a different reason (A1's tail drains ⏎₂, which re-arms; A2's tail drains the text), and a reader tracing it would look for `deferralGeneration`, which no longer exists. Fix: reword the last sentence to the idle rule and point at the gate; clarity, no pin.**

**[NIT] R3-3 · `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Editor/EditorTextView.swift:132–140` (the R2-4 residuals paragraph) · A THIRD residual of the same family is unstated where the next reader will look: KEY EQUIVALENTS (⌘V, ⌘X, ⌘Z…) route through `performKeyEquivalent`, never `keyDown`, so they are neither stashed nor deferred — during a flight they meet the disabled surface on AppKit's own terms (the same fate as `insertText:`-only input), and in the claim gap they act on the OLD block ahead of the pending act (the click residual's shape). ⏎ then ⌘V — "new paragraph, paste" — is an ordinary sequence; the gap is one main-actor hop and the flight is R1-2's window. Byte-identical to the pre-fix tree, so no pin; say it beside the other two so nobody "fixes" it by stashing key equivalents (which would swallow ⌘Q and ⌘Z for the flight's length). Clarity.**

#### Considered and cleared (traced, not findings — the sanity pass's *"no road arms and reaches neither drain"*, which I set out to falsify and could not)
- **The idle gate's two conditions are exact complements, every interleaving enumerated:** (i) a flight's `defer` runs while a task is pending → declines; that task's tail runs with no flight up → drains. (ii) A task's tail runs while a MENU flight is up → declines (and clears the handle); the flight's `defer` finds the handle nil → drains. (iii) A task's own flight → its `defer` declines on its own handle; its tail drains. (iv) A task's body refused by `runStructural`'s one-flight guard (`:2417`, BEFORE the arming at `:2431`) → arms nothing; its tail declines under the flight; the flight's `defer` drains. A tail whose token was superseded (`:1516`) neither clears nor drains, and the superseding task always runs its own tail: nothing cancels the handle (grep — assigned `:1509`, cleared `:1517`, read at `EditorViewModel.swift:554/557/1703`, no `.cancel()` anywhere in the App target).
- **A stash with NEITHER a flight nor a task pending cannot exist.** The App target has exactly two arming sites (grep): the CLAIM (`EditorViewModel.swift:1552`, `scheduleTypedReturn` on the very next line — the act is queued before any key can arrive) and `runStructural` (`:2431`, after the guard, before the first `await`). `replayDeferredKeystrokes()` has exactly one caller (the gate, `:1540`); the gate has exactly two (`:1524` the tail, `:2447` the `defer`, last).
- **No nested or synchronous drain:** both gate callers are async contexts; a replayed key can only SCHEDULE (`scheduleCrossBlockCompound` / `scheduleTypedReturn`) or edit synchronously through `shouldChangeText`, never run a flight inline. The stash is copied and cleared before the replay loop (`EditorTextView.swift:156–158`), so keys a replayed ⏎'s re-arming stashes go to a fresh array — `keyDown` reads the flag per event, as the comment says.
- **Ordering inside `runStructural`'s `defer`** (`:2433–2447`): `structuralInFlight = false` → `isEditable` restored → the idle clock → `applyPendingIfClean()` → the gate, LAST — the replay always meets an editable surface with the deferred projection applied; nothing between those statements yields.
- **The view is never reused across models:** `EditorSurface.makeNSView` / `InlineEditorSurface.makeNSView` (`EditorView.swift:393–397, 480–484`) mint a fresh `EditorTextView` per representable; `EditorViewModel.attach` is the one writer of `textView` and no road nils it, so a flight's `defer` always drains the view it armed. (A re-attach INSIDE a flight window would need a mode switch inside a few ms — the click residual's class; recorded, not a finding.)
- **R2-2's decide-then-act, adversarially:** the guard (`:1544`) reads the same source the act reads (`header?.effectiveSegmentation`; nil ⇒ `.paragraph` on both roads, `:1521`/`TypedReturn.swift:92` — consistent). Under chip ON a retraction followed by `.content` is unreachable by construction: the retraction runs only for a caret at its ENTRY's end (`:1593–1600`; a tail caret matches no entry ⇒ no retraction ⇒ the act's `.content` there mutates nothing) with a `\n` behind it (`:1603`) and a list line before that (`:1610`), so the entry is non-empty (never `.nothing`) and afterwards the caret sits at the previous list line's END — which R1-4's rule (`TypedReturn.swift:163–167`, `clamped < line.upperBound`) excludes — ⇒ `.emptyBlock(.below)`. The mid-block exit (`"- a\n- \nmore"` → no retraction → `.divideAtCaret` → the k−1 seam) and the blank-line guard re-traced; both fences hold. Under chip OFF the `.exitList` road's lead deletion stands (K5's letter, chip-independent by design — the implementer's rider) and the discarded claim result (`EditorTextView.swift:220`) runs nothing else.
- **A typed act meeting a MENU flight:** ⌘⇧D then ⏎ inside the flight → the ⏎ is stashed and replays after (correct); ⏎ then ⌘⇧D inside the claim gap → the act's `applyStructural` refuses at `:197`, SPOKEN (*"Another structural change is still landing."*), and the keys behind it land at the menu flight's caret. One-hop windows, never silent; not findings.
- **The R2-1 pins are the real key road:** `characterKey` (keyCode 7) and `deleteKey` (keyCode 51, `\u{7F}` → `deleteBackward:`) drive `keyDown`, never `insertText:`; both shapes traced end to end under the gate (`["alpha", "x"]`; `["alpha", "", "xbeta"]` — the carve's `caretLandingAfterRemoving` arms `:1144`/`:1145`, then ⏎₂'s `.above` at `beta`'s start) and under the token (`["alphax", ""]`; `["alpha", "xbeta"]` + the divide door's spoken refusal at a one-word block) — the reds in run (2) are those exact shapes.
- **The 13u fence through the fix range:** `git diff --stat 4b8720b2..dfc195c6` = three Mac editor files, one test file, `QUESTIONS.md`, this plan — nothing in `Sources/VariorumCore/` (Sync included), `BlockRangeIndex`, `CrossBlockEditPlanner`, or `App/Variorum/Shared/`; no core run and no iOS build owed, as the record says.

  three for a pair, **two for a run of three or more, never the pairing surface**.

  board's `mergeSelectedBlocks`, the workbench's `mergePicked`). U-16 gives every one of them a second
  road (plain links), and three copies of a two-road loop is three chances to disagree; each caller now
  does only what it does with the landing (Write moves a caret, the board parks a ring, the workbench
  speaks). The A5 pre-compound validation is gated to the CARRY-ALL road — the plain road resolves no
  displayed take and cannot suffer that race.

  DISPATCHER onto the surface's own standing roads (carry-all · instant · chain), each of which flushes,

  the sheet still dismisses only on landing.

**Piece 4 — THE DIVIDE SHEET'S ARRANGEMENT ROW (U-5 · U-3).** `DivideWCeremony.Arrangement`
(`keepOnTop · keepOnBottom · copyToBoth · perTake`, the ruled order) on `State`; the sheet's FIRST
control is the row of four (`VoicedChip`, the chrome vocabulary — batch 2 gives it the mockup's face),
`keepOnTop` selected on open.

- **ONE seeding brain, and it CONSUMES the instant default's.** `DivideWCeremony.arranged(_:as:seedID:)`
  routes the two parking presets THROUGH `KeepOnTopDivide.compose` / `compose(keepBelow:)` rather than
  re-deriving their arithmetic, so "the sheet's preset produces the same cuts dict as the instant
  divide, byte-for-byte" is true by construction. The SEED take is never re-placed (its ✂ is the
  gesture's, and hers once she moves it); `copyToBoth` gives the others no ✂ at all (they are duplicated,
  not cut); `perTake` fills any unplaced ✂ with the take's END — which IS U-3 — and leaves a hand-placed
  cut alone. An EMPTY take stays unmarked under a preset (it cannot park), matching
  `KeepOnTopDivide.unparkable` exactly.
- **`open` no longer seeds from `CutPropagation`** (the brief's "the proposal survives ONLY as the ⌥←/→
  strong step, never the default position"). It survives where it still answers a real question: the
  drift `refresh`'s re-seed of a take whose text moved under an open sheet, and Make Block's cross-take
  mapping. The arrangement SURVIVES a drift refresh and re-places every ✂ over the refreshed texts.
- **Confirm roads:** `keepOnTop` / `keepOnBottom` / `perTake` → `divideW(cuts:displayedVariantID:)`;
  `copyToBoth` → `divideDuplicating(displayedVariantID:cut:)`. The host protocol gained
  `runDivideDuplicating(blockID:displayedVariantID:cut:)` and `runDivideW` gained the anchor; BOTH hosts
  (`EditorSurfaceController`, `TakesViewModel`) conform.
- **A DECLARED CALL (it rides QUESTIONS #91, which already asks this exact question at the instant
  door):** the sheet's confirm passes the SEED as `displayedVariantID` on every `divideW` road, including
  "Choose per take…". Before, the ceremony passed nil and both successors anchored on A's RENDERED take —
  identical in Write, but on the Takes board the seed is whatever pane the caret is in, so a hand-placed
  divide could leave B rendering another take whole beside C's tail of the divided one. `divideW` ignores
  an anchor that is not among `cuts`, so an UNCHECKED seed falls back to today's behaviour by
  construction. Same question, same built answer, one road.
- **Faces (mechanics only, per the brief's "restructure the controls, do not restyle"):** the checkbox
  and both explainers appear only under "Choose per take…" (mockup A's caption); a preset's rows carry
  `presetRowNote` instead *(CORRECTED at the 18f ladder, R1-4 — superseded by batch 2's card: `presetRowNote`
  and `uncheckedRowNote` are RETIRED; every row's sentence lives in `CeremonyCardCopy.status`)*; under a preset the non-seed flows are SHOW-ONLY (mockup A: *"the columns below
  only show what will happen"*) — the drag is not attached, so nothing looks live that isn't — while the

  preset owns every mark; `minWidth` 560 → 720 so the row of four fits.

**Piece 6 — THE WALK (the acceptance gate — USE), and ROW 3's FIX.**

Walked by hand on the DEV-STORE Debug build (`io.lduarte.Variorum.debug`, launched from
`…/DerivedData/Variorum-dnpxwqhpxdqrdjbunktnzoahckpd/Build/Products/Debug/`) with `cliclick` +
`Tools/WalkKit`. Her `/Applications` app was never launched; every instance check
(`pgrep -fl 'Variorum.app/Contents/MacOS'`) showed only DerivedData Debug paths, and my instances were
quit at the end (zero at close). The fixture: the field guide's own three-take lesson block ("The ferry
crossed at dawn…"), and the store was left exactly as found (both acts undone).

**ROW 3 — THE MECHANISM, FOUND BY USE.** With §4.4's three brains green, the fault was where the plan
predicted: the sheet's gesture wiring. `DivideWSheet` armed its `@FocusState keyboardFocused` exactly
ONCE, in `onAppear`, and every control inside it takes first responder when clicked — so **←/→, ⌥←/→
and ⇥ were dead for the rest of the sheet's life after the first click on a control.** Her own session
made that click unavoidable: the lesson (and the sheet's own copy) tells you to uncheck a take before
confirming. What was left was dragging a ✂ into a 9-pt slot in a flow whose glyph overlaps its
neighbours — which is exactly *"the scissors couldn't be placed at the end of the block"*. **The
click/drag road to the end always worked** (walked: a click right of the last word parks the ✂ and the
row says so), which is why the pure pins could never have caught this.

**Three focus roads were tried and all three stayed deaf** (each walked): assigning
`keyboardFocused = true` (a no-op — `@FocusState` publishes edges), bouncing it false→true in the same
turn, bouncing it a runloop turn later; `.focusable(false)` on the chips and the checkbox did not stop
them taking first responder either. **The fix is a local key MONITOR** installed only while the sheet is
on screen (it is window-modal, so that is exactly its scope), acting on the four placement chords and
returning every other event untouched; `.onKeyPress` stays on the container as the ordinary road, and
both roads call the same two brains (`stepCut`, `stepTake`) so they cannot answer differently.
RED and GREEN both captured.

**Piece 1 — ROW 3, the end slot: §4.4's three links, pinned in order, ALL GREEN on the pre-fix tree.**
`App/Variorum/VariorumTests/CutFlowEndSlotTests.swift` (4 tests, 1 suite, green at
`-only-testing:VariorumTests/CutFlowEndSlotTests`):

- **(i) `theEndOfATakeIsALegalCutPosition` — PASSES.** `legalCutPositions(of: "Alpha beta.")` contains
  11, and the end survives every ending shape (terminator · trailing space · trailing newline · one
  word); the empty take is the one deliberate exception. `BoundarySnapping.boundaries` seeds its set
  with `[0, text.utf16.count]` before any scanner runs, so the end can never be dropped.
- **(ii) `theEndSlotIsLaidOutInsideTheFlowsOwnSize` — PASSES.** At a wrapping width whose LAST line is
  the widest (asserted in the pin, so it cannot go vacuous), the end slot's frame sits inside
  `geometry.size` in both axes. `size.width = min(widest, width)` cannot clip it: a slot is only ever
  placed at `x == 0` or where `x + slotWidth <= width`, and `widest` is taken after the end slot.
- **(iii) `aPointRightOfTheLastTokenResolvesToTheEndSlot` — PASSES.** Just past the end slot, far right
  of the row, and below the row all resolve to the end.
- Plus `steppingForwardReachesTheEndAndParksThere` (the keyboard road): → walks to the end and parks.

slot exists (boundary 356, frame `(374, 44, 9, 18)` at 1030), and `nearestSlot` answers 356 for every

**Piece 1 — `ProseCutGeometry`: THE TAKE, AS PROSE.**

Her row 5 names three things and this file answers the first: *"the spacing between the words"*. The
shipped face drew each take as a TOKEN FLOW — a word, a fixed **9-pt slot**, a word, a slot
(`CutFlowGeometry.slotWidth`) — so every legal cut position occupied real width and the prose could
never read as prose. `ProseCutGeometry` lays the take out with **Core Text** instead (one paragraph at
a time, so a take's own newlines survive as content — I4), places the legal cuts as **zero-width
marks**, and the ✂ is drawn OVER the inter-word gap rather than occupying one. **The cut is not an
input to the layout**, so placing or moving it cannot move a word.

- **ONE snapping brain, literally.** The marks are handed to **`CutFlowGeometry.nearestSlot`** — row
  first, then distance, strong slots pull — so the rule `CutFlowEndSlotTests` pins stays one function
  in one place rather than a second implementation that has to agree with the first. That is also why
  `CutFlowGeometry` survives (below).
- **No TextKit anywhere near it** (TEXTKIT2 §4): a sheet is chrome, and this is a measurement. Core
  Text, drawn into one `Canvas` — the L1 architecture kept, with a different thing laid out.
- **The air above every line (`markGlyphAir`) is part of the LAYOUT, not the drawing** — the ✂ glyph
  rides in it (the mockup's `.cut::after`). Found by walking: at the shipped leading the glyph landed
  in the middle of the line above. An offset applied at draw time only would have to be subtracted
  again at hit-test time, which is the two-mechanisms class the file exists to avoid.
- **The marks are placed in ONE pass beside the lines** and `lineIndex(for:)` is a binary search. The
  first cut looked each boundary's line up linearly and measured **1,374 ms** to mount her four
  3,000-word takes; the pin below is what caught it (258 ms now).

Pins: `ProseCutGeometryTests` (9). The natural-spacing one is **RED on the shipped renderer by
construction and non-vacuous**: it asserts the prose's width against the font's own measure AND prints
`CutFlowGeometry`'s width for the same sentence beside it.

**FOUR DEFECTS THE WALK FOUND AND FIXED** (each invisible to every pin — this is what the gate is for):

1. **The prose was clipped and the card had a hole under it.** The footer carried
   `.frame(maxHeight: .infinity, alignment: .bottom)`, so the FOOTER was the greedy view and the prose
   scroll view was squeezed to a minimum with empty space below it. Fixed with a `Spacer` between them.
2. **The ✂ glyph landed in the middle of the line above** (it rode 10 pt above its own line, and the
   shipped leading left only ~4 pt of air). Fixed in the LAYOUT — `markGlyphAir`, above.
3. **The facts line read "3 takes/cut where you clicked"** — it joined on the VOICE's own path
   separator, which is `/` in Terminal. Always the middle dot now.
4. **Every card was named "take"** — unlabelled takes now read *"take"* · *"take 2"* · *"take 3"* by
   fan position, which is the mockup's own naming. *(Two smaller ones the same way: "Copy takes to
   both" truncated its footer swatch labels at a three-column width, and the run list wore a redundant
   "shown here" badge on every row.)*

**THE MOCKUP COMPARISON — where the built face departs from figures A/B, and why:**

| Departure | Why |
|---|---|
| The header's facts line reads *"3 takes · cut where you clicked"*, not the mockup's *"block 4 of 8 · 3 takes · …"* | this sheet mounts on THREE surfaces (Write, the takes board, the lenses board) and only Write knows a block's position in its note. The number would be absent on two of them or would ride a new field on the ceremony's state — which is mechanics, batch 1's, and closed. |
| No *"✓ chosen"* badge on the cards | the ceremony's state has never carried which take is CHOSEN (only which is the seed — `shown here`). Adding it means a new field on the state and both openers: mechanics. Recorded rather than invented. |
| "Choose per take…" reads *"check, place and park each ✂ yourself"*, not the mockup's *"check, place, park — the full ceremony"* | **U-4 took the word "ceremony" out of every UI string** and batch 1 swept it; the mockup predates that sweep by a day. Pinned (`everyOptionCardHasALineAndNoneSaysCeremony`). |
| The keyboard legend omits `space` (check/uncheck) | the sheet does not implement it — batch 1's key monitor answers exactly ⇥ / ⇧⇥ / ← / →. A legend may only claim what the surface does. |
| The two half tints are `chosen`-green above and `ring` below, not the mockup's literal `#EAF1E8` / `#F1EBE1` | the caption grants the tint as latitude and P-8 forbids literals. In the STUDY voice these resolve to exactly the mockup's pair (faint green over warm paper); in Terminal the lower half wears the ✂'s own blue. Hues are placeholders revisable at 13s, like `alarm`'s. |
| The ✂ glyph rides ABOVE the line (the caption's explicit either/or) | on the line it would have to punch a hole in the prose at the cut; above it, with the layout's own air, nothing is covered. |
| More than three takes wrap to a SECOND ROW of the same three columns rather than becoming four narrower ones | §5.2's letter is "columns of equal width, each scrolling on its own; never a vertical stack you scroll to find the second take" — takes 1–3 stay side by side, each card's prose scrolls inside itself, and a fourth column at this width would be ~200 pt of prose. This is also what the mockup's own CSS grid does. |

Everything the captions fix by ruling is built as ruled: the four options and their order, keep-on-top
selected on open, every take checked, the non-seed ✂ at the end, the sentence in counts under the row.

### 18f LADDER — ROUND 1 FINDINGS (fable-reviewer)

*Cold review of `db02623a..a9e66a51` (batches 1 + 2) against §1 (U-3 · U-4 · U-5 · U-16), §3 rows 3 · 4 · 5 · 35 · 36, §4.4, §5.2, §8–§9, R-0, R-4/R-5 (records + adjudications), mockup figures A/B, QUESTIONS #91/#95, and the canon. Every file in the diff was read in this session; the nine letter suites were re-run here (`✔ 89 tests in 9 suites`), then sixteen ceremony-adjacent suites neither closing run named (138 tests — ONE red, finding R1-6). Format: `[severity] · file:line · what · evidence · fix`.*

#### Findings, severity-ranked

**R1-1 · [MAJOR] · `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Editor/DivideWSheet.swift:280–306` (`installKeyMonitor` / `handleKeyEvent`), `:384–403` (`stepCut`) · the sheet's key monitor is PROCESS-wide, not window-scoped — it swallows ⇥/←/→ typed in ANY of the app's windows while the sheet is up, and moves the ✂ with them.** Evidence: `NSEvent.addLocalMonitorForEvents(matching: .keyDown)` sees every key event the app dequeues; the only guard is `ceremony.state != nil` (`:283`), and `handleKeyEvent` returns `true` (event swallowed, `:284`) whenever `stepCut`/`stepTake` do — `stepCut` returns `true` for EVERY ←/→ under a preset, because the seed is always aimable (`:367–371`, `:387–402`; even when the cut does not move it sets `activeTakeID` and answers `true`). The sheet is window-modal for ONE window, and the app has others with live editors: the per-note `WindowGroup` (`VariorumApp.swift:295` → `NoteWindowView`), the Quick Note window (`:331`), the helper (`:351`), Settings (`:357`). Inputs → outcome: "Divide choosing takes" open in note window A; she clicks into note window B (open in its own window) and presses → / ⌥→ / ⇧→ to move or extend the caret — the monitor swallows it and steps the ✂ in A; B's caret never moves; under "Choose per take…" with two checked takes ⇥ in B is eaten too. `handleKeyEvent` also ignores ⌘/⌃ (it reads only `.shift`/`.option`), so ⌘→ (line end) is eaten the same way. The codebase already owns the correct shape of this door and this batch built a second, unscoped one beside it: `PageTurnKeyMonitor` (`Core/Views/PageTurnKeys.swift:90–98` — the Act 10 correction round's R1-5 — guards `event.window === window` and refuses every chord), and `PaneFocusCatcherView` (`Workspace/PaneFocusCatcher.swift:115–123`) — which compares **by `windowNumber`**, with the recorded reason ("`event.window` … answers nil for a window with no device — which is every window a test builds"). Fix: replace the inline `@State` monitor with a zero-size `NSViewRepresentable` monitor view inside the sheet (the `PageTurnKeyMonitor` shape: install in `viewDidMoveToWindow`, remove when the window goes), guarding `event.windowNumber == window.windowNumber`, refusing ⌘/⌃ chords, its decision an exposed `handleKeyDown(_:) -> Bool` body so a pin can drive it with a real `NSEvent` and a real second window's number; `DivideWSheet` keeps `handleKeyEvent`'s brains and drops the two install/remove functions. **Pin (red-on-old):** mount the sheet in an `NSWindow` exactly as `DivideCeremonyCostTests.mountSheet` does (which fires `onAppear` and installs the monitor in the test host), make a second `NSWindow`, post a → `NSEvent.keyEvent(… windowNumber: other.windowNumber …)` and settle — on old the seed's cut steps; on new it does not; the same event carrying the sheet window's number steps it on both (non-vacuity). If the host does not deliver posted events to a local monitor (the `PaneFocusCatcher` measurement warns it may not — it measured `sendEvent`, not `postEvent`), the honest form is the precedent's: assert installation + drive the exposed body with both window numbers, and record it as the rig's form (batch 1's own row-3 disposition).

**R1-2 · [MAJOR] · `EditorSurfaceController.swift:1445–1464` (`confirmMergeCeremony`'s preset branch) → `:376–391` (`runInstantMerge`, the unguarded read at `:379`), `:608` (`runCarryAllMerge`), `:673` (`runMergeChain`) · the two preset roads act on ENTRY INDICES captured when the sheet opened (`:1282`), after the sheet sat open for as long as she liked — and `runInstantMerge` indexes the projection with them unguarded.** Evidence: before this letter `runInstantMerge` ran in the same turn as its plan (the ⌘⇧M road, and `openMergeCeremony`'s open-time fallthrough); U-5's row makes it the CONFIRM of a sheet that can sit open while the projection re-renders — a sync apply landing under it (the drift class the pairing road already guards against, `:1466–1490`), or a menu chord that stays live under a window-modal sheet (`DivideWCeremony.swift:45–48`'s own note). Inputs → outcome: a two-block note `[A, B(fanned)]`, ⌥⌘⇧M at the seam → the sheet with `entryIndices 0...1`; block A dies (a remote tombstone, or Delete Block from the menu bar) and the index re-renders to ONE entry; "Only the two shown" → `model.index.entries[1]` (`:379`) → index out of range → the app traps. With blocks above the pair the same road does not trap but reads the WRONG entry's length: the caret lands off the join and the seam it reports (`.merge(leadingIndex:)`, `:387`) names the wrong entry, so `StructuralSeamEdit.derive` refuses (`StructuralSeamEdit.swift:208–211`, `fresh[i].blockID == mergedID`) and the differential apply silently becomes a full render. `runCarryAllMerge` (`:640`) and `runMergeChain` (`:676`) guard the trap (`index < count ? … : 0`) but keep the stale lengths — same wrong caret. The pairing road is immune (it re-reads both fans and merges by ID). The rule this breaks is the file's own: `runPlainDivide` `:404–411` — *"Re-resolved by BLOCK ID because the fallback road reaches here after a flush, and an index taken before a suspension can address a re-tiled surface."* Fix: in the dispatcher (`:1445`), re-resolve `state.blockIDs` against the CURRENT index at confirm time; any block absent → refuse in the refresh road's own voice ("These blocks' takes changed — try the merge again."), dismiss, touch nothing; pass the fresh indices to the three roads — and harden `runInstantMerge`'s length/seam reads to resolve by block ID so no future stale caller can trap it. **Pin (red-on-old, no trap):** a four-block note, the sheet on blocks 1–2 (the second fanned), block 0 deleted through ops + `reload()` under the open sheet, "Only the two shown", confirm → the caret sits at the join (`selectedRange().location == merged entry + leading length + 1`); on old it lands at the stale entry's arithmetic (one unit off with the fixture's lengths). The two-block shape traps on old — recorded here, deliberately not used as the pin.

**R1-3 · [MINOR] · `DivideWCeremony.swift:449–477` (`arrangementSentence`) with its call site `DivideWSheet.swift:115–118`; `MergeArrangement.swift:87–107` (`MergeArrangementCopy.sentence`) · the ONE sentence under each row is ungrammatical in the singular and double-counts empty takes — and the batch PINNED both defects.** Evidence: `takes(1)` + `" stay whole"` → **"1 other take stay whole on the top block"** on every TWO-take divide (the commonest fan), pinned as-is at `DivideArrangementTests.swift:289` (`"1 other take stay"`, "singular counts"); with an empty other take: **"2 other takes stay whole on the top block; … 1 empty take can't be placed and stays with the original block"** — `others` (`State.otherTakes.count`) includes the unparkable, so the first count is false by one; pinned as-is at `:274` (`"2 other takes"` with `unparkable: 1`). The merge row: **"1 other take come along whole"** / **"1 other take stay with the released blocks"**, pinned at `MergeArrangementTests.swift:276`. The J8 copy-truth doctrine (the record's own citation) says a sentence may claim only what the operation will do. Fix: conjugate by count and say the parked count (`others − unparkable`) for the two parking presets (copy-to-both keeps `others`: `divideDuplicating` duplicates an empty take too — see R1-5); re-pin the corrected words. **Pin (red-on-old):** `arrangementSentence(.keepOnTop, others: 2, unparkable: 1)` contains `"1 other take stays whole"`; `(.keepOnBottom, others: 1, unparkable: 0)` contains `"1 other take stays"`; `MergeArrangementCopy.sentence(.bringEveryTake, blocks: 2, otherTakes: 1)` contains `"1 other take comes along whole"`.

**R1-4 · [MINOR] · `ProseTakeCard.swift:85–86` (`status(.original)`), `:130` (`footerNote`); `DivideWCeremony.swift:481–499` (`presetRowNote`), `:647–650` (`uncheckedRowNote`) · the unchecked take's card dropped the 13f W10a answer the mockup keeps, and the two batch-1 copy homes of that answer went DEAD behind pins that stay green.** Evidence: mockup figure A, state 2, unchecked take: *"not cut — stays on the original block, in Block History → Came from"*; built: `"Not cut — stays whole on the original block, in lineage"` + footer `"original block · lineage"` — the PATH is gone from the row. 13f W10a (her sitting question, verbatim in the file: *"how do I even see the original one?"*) put the path on the row precisely because that is where she was looking. `presetRowNote` and `uncheckedRowNote` were batch 1's homes for the row copy; after batch 2 NO production caller remains (grep: definitions only) — while `OriginBlockDoorTests.swift:59` and `NoteTrayTests.swift:413` keep asserting `uncheckedRowNote.contains(TrayPath.originalsPath)` — green over a string no surface speaks (the F-3 vacuity class recorded in this very file's header, `DivideWSheet.swift:34–41`). The sheet's resting footer line still names the path, so the promise is half-kept, not lost. Fix: `CeremonyCardCopy.status(.original)` names `TrayPath.originalsPath` (the mockup's own words), the card becomes the ONE home (retire `presetRowNote`/`uncheckedRowNote`, or have the card read them), and the two pins point at the live home. **Pin (red-on-old):** `CeremonyCardCopy.status(.original, words: nil).contains(TrayPath.originalsPath)`.

**R1-5 · [MINOR] · `ProseTakeCard.swift:60–62` (`CeremonyCardCopy.destination`) · an EMPTY other take under "Copy takes to both" is told it stays on the original block, but `divideDuplicating` duplicates it onto BOTH successors.** Evidence: `if isEmpty { return marked && arrangement == .perTake ? .empty : .original }` runs BEFORE the `.copyToBoth` rule at `:62`; `arranged(.copyToBoth)` marks every take (`DivideWCeremony.swift:412–418`); `Operations+Structural.swift:598–606` fetches every other active take with no emptiness filter and `:640` mints `[half] + others.map(\.workingText)` on both sides. The sentence under the row counts it correctly ("N other takes ride whole onto BOTH blocks"); the card beneath it says the opposite — one sheet, two answers, the class the status/footer pair was built to prevent (`ProseCutGeometryTests.swift:270–280`, "a card can never claim two destinations"). Fix: the `.copyToBoth` rule before the emptiness rule. **Pin (red-on-old):** `destination(arrangement: .copyToBoth, isSeed: false, marked: true, isEmpty: true, cut: nil, …) == .both` (old answers `.original`).

**R1-6 · [MINOR] · `App/Variorum/VariorumTests/BoundaryFluidityTests.swift:382–398` (`mergeOnFannedBlocksSpeaksAboutTheFan`) · RED on the tree — this letter's own doing, outside BOTH closing runs (the J14 class the orchestrator recorded for exactly this reviewer).** Evidence: run here → `✘ … (status → "Blocks merged — the takes you were looking at joined, and 1 other take came along whole. …").contains("Block History → Came from")`. The pin drives `.mergeBlocks, ceremony: true` on a fanned pair and confirms at once, asserting the lineage road is named; U-5's row opens on "Bring every take", whose receipt is the carry-all's (`FannedDefaultsCopy.merged`) — correct copy, nothing goes to lineage on that road. Green at all three 18c closing runs (this file `:1836`, `:2125`, `:2261` — 47 suites, `BoundaryFluidityTests` named); red since batch 1 piece 5 (`21806a53`); neither 18f closing list (36 / 30 types) names the suite. Not a product bug. Fix: a justified edit, claim intact — the road where takes DO go to lineage: `setMergeArrangement(.onlyTheShownOnes)` before the confirm (`speakAboutMergedFans`, `:358–370`, names the path), with the comment updated. Process: the letter's close must run the FULL Mac suite once — both batches ran hand-picked lists, and this is the second suite (after `OriginBlockDoorTests`) whose premise a ruling moved unseen.

**R1-7 · [NIT] · `TakesBoardView.swift:464–479` + `:506/:512`; `BlocksWorkbenchView.swift:435/:442–452` · the bars' "Merge choosing takes ▾" is enabled below two picks while its neighbour "Merge Selected Blocks" is disabled there.** `mergeSelectedBlocks`/`mergePicked` speak a real refusal for one pick, so P-2 holds; the two doors disagree only in posture. Fix: `.disabled(picked.count < 2)` on the menu, or drop the neighbour's `.disabled` — one rule for the pair.

**R1-8 · [NIT] · `DivideWCeremony.swift:419–431` (`arranged(.perTake)`) · "Choose per take…" entered FROM "Keep takes on the bottom block" shows the non-seed ✂ at the START.** §5.2's letter: the checklist reveals "every take CHECKED, non-seed ✂ at the end (U-3)"; `perTake` fills only a nil cut, so the last preset's positions carry (`choosePerTakeRevealsTheChecklistAtTheEnds` pins the top→perTake road only). Defensible — the positions she just saw are the ones she gets — recorded as a reading for her, not a fix.

#### IMPLEMENTER'S RESPONSES (18f round 1)

*Batch 1's implementer, answering per finding under the cross-model flow. Every mechanism re-traced
against the tree at `2ac7bd79` (not against the record) before answering; where a finding lands on batch
2's face I traced that too, as the coordinator asked. **13 AGREE · 0 DISPUTE.** I fix nothing; what

**R1-1 · AGREE (MAJOR).** Re-traced: `NSEvent.addLocalMonitorForEvents(matching: .keyDown)` sees every
key event this PROCESS dequeues; `installKeyMonitor` (`DivideWSheet.swift:280–286`) guards only
`ceremony.state != nil`, and `handleKeyEvent` (`:294–306`) returns the step's own answer, which for ←/→
under a preset is ALWAYS `true` — `stepCut` (`:384–403`) answers `true` even when the cut does not move
(`else { activeTakeID = takeID }`), so the event is swallowed at every boundary, including the ends. The
app really does have the other windows named (`VariorumApp.swift:47`, `:295`, `:331`, `:351`, `:357`),
and both cited precedents are as quoted (`PageTurnKeys.swift:88–98` guards `event.window === window`;
`Workspace/PaneFocusCatcher.swift:113–124` compares `event.windowNumber == window.windowNumber` with its
recorded reason). **Two things the fix must carry, from the walk that produced this monitor** (the record's
piece 6): (a) it exists because THREE focus roads were tried and all three stayed deaf — plain assignment,
a same-turn `@FocusState` bounce, a next-turn bounce, plus `.focusable(false)` on the chips and the
checkbox — so the replacement must keep working while an AppKit control inside the `ScrollView` holds
first responder, which a `viewDidMoveToWindow`-installed monitor does and a focus re-arm does not; and
(b) `windowNumber`, not `event.window`, is the right comparison for exactly `PaneFocusCatcher`'s recorded
reason, which is what makes the pin's second window drivable at all. I would add one thing to the fix's
scope, from the same trace: **the swallow should follow the STEP, not the aim** — `stepCut` answering
`true` when nothing moved is what makes ←/→ eaten at a take's ends even in the sheet's own window, and it
is one `return next != nil && next != take.cut` away.

**R1-2 · AGREE (MAJOR).** Re-traced: `entryIndices` is captured at open (`:1282`,
`min(leading, trailing)...max(leading, trailing)`) and the dispatcher (`:1445–1464`) hands it to all three
roads unchanged; `runInstantMerge` (`:379`) subscripts `model.index.entries[trailingIndex]` with NO bound
— it traps. `runCarryAllMerge` (`:639`) and `runMergeChain` (`:676`) do carry the
`index < count ? … : 0` guard, so they miss the trap and keep the stale length, exactly as described.
**The framing is right and it is mine:** before this letter `runInstantMerge`'s only callers ran within a
bounded turn of their plan — `applyStructural`'s `.merge` case, and `openMergeCeremony`'s fallthrough
(after one flush and one read); U-5's row made it the confirm of a sheet that can sit open indefinitely,
which is what turned a latent shape into a reachable one. The reachability is real on both roads the
reviewer names: a sync apply re-renders the index under an open sheet, and menu key equivalents stay live
under a window-modal sheet — the note `DivideWCeremony.swift:45–48` makes for its own busy-door guard. The
rule broken is the one this file states two hundred lines away (`runPlainDivide`, `:404–411`:
*"Re-resolved by BLOCK ID … an index taken before a suspension can address a re-tiled surface"*); I
applied it to the divide roads in batch 1 and did not apply it to the merge dispatcher I added. The fix's
shape — re-resolve `state.blockIDs` at confirm, refuse in the refresh road's own voice when a block is
gone, and harden `runInstantMerge` to resolve by ID — is the right one, and hardening the callee is what
keeps a future caller from re-earning the trap.

**R1-3 · AGREE (MINOR).** Re-traced both brains. `DivideWCeremony.arrangementSentence` (`:449–477`):
`takes(1)` is `"1 other take"` and the clause is `" stay whole"` → *"1 other take stay whole"* on every
two-take fan; `others` is `State.otherTakes.count`, which counts the unparkable, so with one empty other
take the sentence claims two stay whole on the top block and then says one of them cannot be placed —
false by one. `MergeArrangementCopy.sentence` (`:87–107`) has the same singular defect twice
(*"1 other take come along whole"*, *"1 other take stay with the released blocks"*). **And I pinned all
three:** `DivideArrangementTests.swift:289` (`"1 other take stay"`, commented "singular counts" —
which is precisely the J8 failure the doctrine names, a pin that certifies the copy instead of checking
it), `:274` (`"2 other takes"` with `unparkable: 1`), `MergeArrangementTests.swift:276`. The proposed
split is also right in its one subtle half: **copy-to-both must keep `others`** — I verified
`Operations.divideDuplicating` (`Operations+Structural.swift:594–598`) fetches every other live ACTIVE
take with no emptiness filter and `:640` mints `[half] + others.map(\.workingText)` on both successors,
so an empty take genuinely does ride onto both there.

**R1-4 · AGREE (MINOR).** Re-traced against the mockup and the tree. The mockup's own words are
`not cut — <b>stays on the original block</b>, in Block History → Came from` with the footer
`original block · lineage` (grepped from the HTML); the card says
`"Not cut — stays whole on the original block, in lineage"` (`ProseTakeCard.swift:85–86`) plus that same
footer — the PATH is gone from the row, which is the half 13f W10a put there. And the vacuity is exactly
as described: `grep` finds **zero** production callers of `DivideWCeremony.presetRowNote` and
`uncheckedRowNote` — definitions plus `OriginBlockDoorTests.swift:59` and `NoteTrayTests.swift:413`,
both still asserting `uncheckedRowNote.contains(TrayPath.originalsPath)`. **This is the seam between the
two batches and my record is now wrong about it:** batch 1's record names `presetRowNote` as a live copy
home ("a preset's rows carry `presetRowNote` instead"), and batch 2's card superseded it without the
record being corrected — the fix pass should say so wherever it lands, because a future reader following
R-4's record would go looking for a caller that no longer exists. The card as the ONE home is the right
resolution.

**R1-5 · AGREE (MINOR).** Re-traced: `CeremonyCardCopy.destination` (`ProseTakeCard.swift:60–62`) tests
`isEmpty` FIRST and answers `.original` for any arrangement but `.perTake`; `arranged(.copyToBoth)`
(`DivideWCeremony.swift:412–418`) marks every take including an empty one; and `divideDuplicating` does
duplicate it onto both (verified above, R1-3). So the row's sentence and the card beneath it say opposite
things about the same take — the exact contradiction `ProseCutGeometryTests`' "a card can never claim two
destinations" was written to prevent. Ordering the `.copyToBoth` rule before the emptiness rule is the
one-line fix and it does not disturb `.perTake`, where `.empty` is still the honest answer (the sheet's
own "uncheck it" road).

**R1-6 · AGREE (MINOR) — and it is BATCH 1's, not batch 2's.** Re-ran it here:
`BoundaryFluidityTests.mergeOnFannedBlocksSpeaksAboutTheFan` fails at `:398` on

made the sheet open on "Bring every take", whose confirm is the carry-all road — whose receipt correctly

the receipt. The proposed edit is the right one and keeps the claim exactly: `setMergeArrangement(.onlyTheShownOnes)`
before the confirm puts the pin on the road where takes DO stay behind, which is the road
`speakAboutMergedFans` (`EditorSurfaceController.swift:358–370`) speaks for. **The process point is
correct and it is my miss:** my closing run named 36 suite TYPES and batch 2's named 30, and neither
included `BoundaryFluidityTests` — the 18c ladder's runs did (47 types), which is why it was green three
times before and red after. The letter's close should run the FULL Mac suite once; hand-picked lists
cannot see a suite whose premise a ruling moved.

#### ROUND 1 — THE RAISE'S FINAL WORD (fable-reviewer)

**The raise (R1-1's dropped `ceremony.state != nil` guard) — CONFIRMED and FIXED, `5c848a79`.** Traced against the tree: `DivideWSheet.state` is `ceremony.state ?? presented` (`DivideWSheet.swift:72–74`); after `state = nil` (Cancel, ⎋, a landed confirm) the sheet's view is still mounted — SwiftUI's dismissal animation, or a host that never unmounts it — so `stepCut` read the PRESENTED snapshot, found a take with boundaries and a `next != cut`, called `placeCut`, which no-opped inside `DivideWCeremony.placeCut`'s own `guard var current = state` (`DivideWCeremony.swift:526–532`), and answered `true`: the key was swallowed for nothing. Batch 1's monitor had `guard ceremony.state != nil else { return event }` as its first line (`installKeyMonitor`, the pre-fix `:283`); my R1-1 fix moved the guard to the window and did not carry that one. Narrow (a chord inside the dismissal) and correctly sized (one line). **Fix:** `guard ceremony.state != nil else { return false }` at the top of `handleKeyEvent`, before the chord rule. **Pin:** `DivideKeyMonitorTests.aKeyAfterTheCeremonyClosedIsNotTheSheets` — the sheet's own → is consumed while open (non-vacuity), `ceremony.cancel()`, the view still watching (asserted), then → / ← / ⇥ all answer `false` and nothing reopens. **Red on `50d47d85`, recorded:** `DivideKeyMonitorTests.swift:204 monitor.handleKeyDown(→) == false` failed and `:206 (←) == false` failed (⇥ passed on both sides — a preset never swallows it — and is kept as a guard, not evidence). Green: `DivideKeyMonitorTests · DivideCeremonyCostTests · DivideArrangementTests · DivideCeremonyRowTapTests · DivideWCeremonyTests · TakesDivideTests · OriginBlockDoorTests` → **`✔ Test run with 67 tests in 7 suites passed after 6.290 seconds.`**

**The walk fact — another app window's raise DISMISSES the divide sheet — my reading: a RECORDED BEHAVIOUR with an owner, not a round-2 finding.** It is outside this letter's diff (untouched by batch 1, batch 2 and this pass; the implementer's grep stands — `ceremony.cancel()` has one caller), so a fresh round-2 reviewer of `db02623a..HEAD` would rightly not be reviewing it; it is also a real thing she would feel (a half-arranged ceremony lost to a glance at another window — or to Quick Note from the File menu). One look at the mechanism's neighbourhood: the workspace tracks the key window through `WindowKeyReporter` / `WindowRegistry.keyWindowID` (`Workspace/WindowRegistry.swift:192–231`), and the note window's content is keyed `.id(postID)` (`NoteWindowView.swift:91`); the `.sheet(item: $model.divideWCeremony)` presenter (`EditorView.swift:185`) is dismissed when whatever the key-window change re-composes tears the presenter down — SwiftUI then writes nil into the item binding, which IS the cancel road without anyone calling `cancel()`. That is a sketch, not a trace: the exact re-composition is one read further than this pass owed, and it is the workspace's window family, not the ceremony's. **Owner, recommended:** a standing-fixes **17 entry at her word** (her own 17 rule — a quick bug she'd notice, hers to add), with `notes/KnownIssuesAndTechDebt.md` carrying the row until then under **13u2's pane/window family** if she declines; the round's closeout writes the row (the closeout owns this round's KnownIssues writes). Two notes for whoever takes it: (1) the merge sheet (`.sheet(item: $model.mergeCeremony)`) almost certainly shares the behaviour — check it in the same entry; (2) the R1-1 fix stands independently of it — the process-wide monitor also ate keys aimed at panels the sheet does not own (the implementer's Character Viewer case), and the pinned mechanism (a key for another window number) is exactly what a second window would send if the sheet survived the raise.

**Nothing else raised; 12 CLEAR stand.** Tree at the raise's close: `5c848a79` (code) — round 2 (fresh) may open on it.

### 18f LADDER — ROUND 2 FINDINGS (fable-reviewer, fresh)

*A REAL PASS over the round-1 fixes `53879b61..5c848a79` (`20e7cfd4` … `50d47d85`, `5c848a79`) against the letter's whole diff `db02623a..5c848a79` wherever a fix's blast radius reaches — with zero context from round 1's reviewer or the implementers; the anchor is the recorded findings, responses, dispositions, sanity pass and the raise's final word above. Every fix commit's production and test hunk was read in this session, then the files they land in at HEAD (`DivideWSheet` whole; `DivideWCeremony` whole; `EditorSurfaceController`'s merge family `:270–460`, `:600–760`, `:1150–1620`, `:2060–2100`, `:2760–2840`; `MergeArrangement` whole; `MergeCeremonySheet` `:1–215`; `ProseTakeCard` `:1–140`; `TakesViewModel` `:2220–2280`; `TakesStructureController` `:530–580`; `Operations+Structural` `:350–365`, `:440–510`, `:880–910`; `FannedDefaults`, `MergeJoin`, `PageTurnKeys`, `PaneFocusCatcher`; `DivideKeyMonitorTests` whole and every edited test). Re-run here: core `swift test --filter 'StructuralDivideTests|RedoStackTests'` → `✔ 50 tests in 2 suites`; Mac, ONE `xcodebuild test` over 20 suite TYPES (`DivideKeyMonitorTests · DivideArrangementTests · MergeArrangementTests · MergeCeremonySheetTests · ProseCutGeometryTests · CeremonyCardCopyTests · CutFlowEndSlotTests · DivideCeremonyCostTests · DivideCeremonyRowTapTests · WCutPlacementTests · TakesDivideTests · DivideWCeremonyTests · FannedDefaultsTests · FannedDefaultsBoardTests · OriginBlockDoorTests · NoteTrayTests · BoundaryFluidityTests · SeamMergeTests · MergeGestureTests · CapabilityRegistryTests`) → **`✔ Test run with 230 tests in 20 suites passed after 8.436 seconds.`** Format: `[severity] · file:line · what · evidence · fix`.*

#### Findings, severity-ranked

**R2-1 · [MAJOR] · `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Editor/EditorSurfaceController.swift:1574–1602` (`refreshMergeCeremony`), with `:1160–1198` (`MergeCeremonyState`'s three DEFAULTED fields) and `:1468–1479` (R1-2's dispatcher guard) · the pairing road's drift REFRESH rebuilds the state WITHOUT `blockIDs`, `otherTakes` and `arrangement` — so a drift under "Pair by hand…" silently flips the sheet to "Bring every take", hides her pairings, lies about the count, changes the sheet's identity, and R1-2's presence guard goes vacuous over an EMPTY `blockIDs`.** Evidence (traced, inputs → outcome): a pair `[A, B]` with B fanned (tail + spare); ⌥⌘⇧M → the sheet on `.bringEveryTake` (`openMergeCeremony:1305–1313` sets `blockIDs: [A, B]`, `otherTakes: 1`); she picks "Pair by hand…" (`setMergeArrangement`); a sync round lands an edit to a take's text while the sheet is up; ⏎ → `confirmMergeCeremony` → the `.pairByHand` branch's drift check (`:1501–1517`) → `refreshMergeCeremony(from:…)` → `:1588–1594` constructs `MergeCeremonyState(firstBlockID:, secondBlockID:, firstRenderedID:, secondRenderedID:, firstTakes:, secondTakes:, pairings:)` — the MEMBERWISE init, so `blockIDs = []`, `otherTakes = 0`, `arrangement = .bringEveryTake` (`:1168`, `:1176`, `:1186`). Four consequences, each read off the tree: **(1)** `MergeCeremonySheet.swift:84` renders `pairingBody` only under `.pairByHand`, so the refreshed sheet shows the PRESET preview with "Bring every take" selected — her pairings survive in `next.pairings` (`:1597–1599`) but are no longer on screen, while the status says *"review the pairings before merging"* (`:1601`); **(2)** `arrangementSentence` (`:1213–1217`) reads `otherTakes == 0` → *"There are no other takes to bring."* — false for a fanned pair; **(3)** `id` is `blockIDs.joined(separator: "+")` (`:1198`) → `""`, so `.sheet(item: $model.mergeCeremony)` (`EditorView.swift:187`) sees a NEW item and re-presents the sheet; **(4)** the second ⏎ takes the dispatcher's preset branch, where `state.blockIDs.allSatisfy { entryIndex(of: $0) != nil }` over `[]` is TRUE by vacuity, and `runCarryAllMerge` lands the carry-all where she asked for her hand-placed pairings. The existing pin `MergeCeremonySheetTests.confirmRefusesToJoinDriftedText:342–345` (*"Confirming the refreshed sheet lands"*) is GREEN FOR THIS WRONG REASON — its one pairing IS the carry-all's pair (head + rendered tail), so the rows coincide and the flipped road is invisible to it. Contrast the divide side: `DivideWCeremony.refresh` (`DivideWCeremony.swift:703–710`) carries `arrangement` and re-arranges over the refreshed texts — batch 1 did the divide half and not the merge half (`21806a53` added the three fields with defaults and left `refreshMergeCeremony` untouched); R1-2's fix (`20e7cfd4`) then made the dispatcher's safety depend on the very field the refresh drops. Inside this letter's diff on both counts. **Fix:** `refreshMergeCeremony` carries `blockIDs: state.blockIDs`, `arrangement: state.arrangement`, `runBlocks: state.runBlocks`, and recomputes `otherTakes` from the fresh fans (`max(0, firstFan.count − 1) + max(0, secondFan.count − 1)`); harden the dispatcher's guard against vacuity (`!state.blockIDs.isEmpty && …`). **Pins (RED on `5c848a79`):** (a) in `confirmRefusesToJoinDriftedText`, after the refresh: `state.arrangement == .pairByHand`, `state.blockIDs == [a, b]`, `state.otherTakes == 1`, `state.id` unchanged; (b) the behavioural pin — under "Pair by hand…" `removeMergePairing` the default and `pairMergeTakes(firstID: head, secondID: spare)`; drift the rendered TAIL's text through ops; confirm (refreshes); confirm again → the merged block carries ONE take reading head + " " + spare's text and the tail stays in lineage (`stayBehindCount` 1) — on old, the flipped carry-all mints head + tail joined WITH the spare carried (two takes on the merged block).

**R2-2 · [MINOR] · `EditorSurfaceController.swift:1468–1493` (the preset branch) vs `:1495–1517` (the pairing branch's drift check); `runCarryAllMerge:641–645` · the two preset roads confirm against ACT-time "shown" takes while the sheet displays the OPEN-time ones, and no drift check stands on them.** Evidence: `openMergeCeremony` captures `firstRenderedID`/`secondRenderedID` (`:1301–1304`) and the preview columns mark exactly those as *"＋ pairs with →"* (`MergeCeremonySheet.swift:180–212`); `runCarryAllMerge` composes from `model.index.entries.first { $0.blockID == leadingID }?.variantID` at act time (`:642`, `:644`), and `runInstantMerge` → `ops.merge` reads the store's CHOSEN. Inputs → outcome: the sheet open on `[A(fan h, h′), B]`; a sync apply re-points A's chosen to h′ and the projection adopts it (nothing suppresses adoption under the sheet — the pairing branch's own comment at `:1495–1500` says so); ⏎ on "Bring every take" joins h′ + B while the sheet showed h ＋ B, and the receipt says *"the takes you were looking at joined"*. The pairing road refreshes on ANY drift (*"instead of confirming what the author wasn't shown"*); the preset roads check only presence (R1-2). Narrow — a remote chosen re-point in the window, and ⌘⇧M made the same read within one turn before U-5 — but the sheet's open-ended window is what widens it, and the S4-23 drift doctrine is this letter's own. **Fix:** compose the carry-all from the STATE's rendered ids (`firstRenderedID`/`secondRenderedID`; a rendered take no longer live → `CarryAllMerge.compose`'s own nil → `takesChangedMerge`), or give the preset branch the pairing branch's check (rendered ids or texts changed → `refreshMergeCeremony`, which R2-1 makes safe). **Pin (red-on-old):** the sheet on a fanned pair, `.bringEveryTake`; re-point the trailing block's chosen to its spare through ops + `reload()` under the open sheet; confirm → the joined take reads head + the ORIGINAL tail (or the sheet refreshes and nothing lands) — on old it reads head + spare.

**R2-3 · [NIT] · `TakesViewModel.swift:2244`, `:2270` · the board's two sheet receipts read `divided(kept: 0)` — *"Block divided — ⌥⌘Z undoes it."* — while the result in hand knows what rode onto the first block, and the board's own instant road counts it** (`TakesStructureController.swift:571–573`: `kept: result.first.variants.count − 1`). Truthful (claims nothing false — R1-9's call), thinner than the same act's instant receipt. Copy: the `divideW` road counts `max(0, result.first.variants.count − 1)`; the duplicating road keeps its line ("stayed on the first" would misdescribe copies).

**R2-4 · [NIT] · `TakesViewModel.swift:2239`, `:2266` · a reading for R1-13's PROVISIONAL (the closeout's #96), not a fix: `result.second.variants.first` is the FIRST CONTRIBUTOR in fan order** (`Operations+Structural.swift:485–500` — `side` is the marked takes in A's fan order), which under keep-on-top IS the seed's tail (C has one take, so the board's instant road and the sheet agree, as the record claims) but under keep-on-bottom or a hand-placed sheet may be another take parked at its start rather than the anchor's half — C's CHOSEN (`chosenIndex: renderedIndex`). Write is unaffected (the caret lands at the block's start). If the ring should follow the take she divided, park on the chosen (`variants[renderedIndex]`); recorded beside the alternative.

#### IMPLEMENTER'S RESPONSES (18f round 2)

*Batch 1's implementer, cross-model flow, every mechanism re-traced against the tree at `94a0af74`.
**4 AGREE · 0 DISPUTE.** I fix nothing; what follows adds the implementer context the reviewer could not
have — whose miss each is, and what a fix should carry beyond the finding.*

**R2-1 · AGREE (MAJOR) — and it is BATCH 1's, with a root cause worth naming.** Re-traced line by line:
`refreshMergeCeremony` (`:1588–1594`) calls `MergeCeremonyState(firstBlockID:secondBlockID:firstRenderedID:secondRenderedID:firstTakes:secondTakes:pairings:)`
— the MEMBERWISE init — so the four defaulted fields take their defaults: `blockIDs = []` (`:1168`),
`otherTakes = 0` (`:1176`), `runBlocks = []` (`:1181`), `arrangement = .bringEveryTake` (`:1186`). All
four consequences check out on the tree: the sheet renders `pairingBody` only under `.pairByHand`
(`MergeCeremonySheet.swift:84–89`, else `previewColumns`), so her pairings leave the screen while the
status still says *"review the pairings before merging"*; `arrangementSentence` takes the
`otherTakes > 0` guard's else branch (`MergeArrangement.swift:96–99`) and says *"There are no other takes
to bring"* about a fanned pair; `id` is `blockIDs.joined(separator: "+")` (`:1198`) → `""`, a new item to
`.sheet(item:)`; and the dispatcher's `state.blockIDs.allSatisfy { … }` (`:1473–1478`) is vacuously true
over `[]`. And the pin is green for the reason stated: `confirmRefusesToJoinDriftedText:342–345` asserts
only `landed.count == 1` after the second confirm, and its ONE pairing is the pre-pair (head + rendered
tail), which the carry-all also mints — the difference (the carry-all CARRIES the spare, so the merged
block ends with two takes rather than one) is a fact the pin never looks at.

**Whose miss, precisely, and why two reviews missed it:** batch 1 (`21806a53`) added the three fields to
`MergeCeremonyState` **with defaults** and updated the two OPEN sites (`openMergeCeremony:1305`,
`openMergeRunChoice:1379`) — and not the third construction site. Swift's memberwise init makes an
omitted defaulted argument **silent**: no warning, no diagnostic, nothing for a reader to notice. That is
the whole reason this survived a batch record, a cold round and a sanity pass. Worse for me: I DID apply
the rule on the divide side in the same batch — `DivideWCeremony.refresh` carries
`arrangement: current.arrangement` and re-arranges over the refreshed texts (`DivideWCeremony.swift:703–715`),
with a comment saying why — so the letter states the rule and implements it on one side only. R1-2 then
made a safety guard depend on the field the other side drops, which is what turns a display bug into a
road change.

**A rider on the fix, offered because it is the class and not the instance:** carrying the four fields in
`refreshMergeCeremony` and hardening the guard against vacuity are both right and necessary. The thing
that would have PREVENTED it is one line further out — **give `MergeCeremonyState` an explicit
initializer with no defaults**, so a construction site cannot silently omit a field and a future one
fails to compile instead. The run arm's `runBlocks`/`otherTakes` and the pair arm's `firstTakes` already
have to be supplied at every site anyway; only the defaults make omission possible. The reviewer's two
pins are the right pair — the state assertions catch the shape, the behavioural one catches the road —
and I would add one assertion to the first: `state.id == before.id`, since the identity change is what
re-presents the sheet under her.

**R2-2 · AGREE (MINOR).** Re-traced: `runCarryAllMerge` composes from
`model.index.entries.first { $0.blockID == leadingID }?.variantID` at ACT time (`:642`, `:644`) while the
sheet's preview marks `firstRenderedID`/`secondRenderedID` captured at open (`:1301–1304`,
`MergeCeremonySheet`'s `previewColumns`), and `runInstantMerge` → `ops.merge` reads the store's chosen
then too. The pairing branch refuses on drift; the preset branch checks only presence (R1-2's guard).
Reachability is the same class the pairing branch's own comment names (`:1495–1500`: the sheet may sit
open while a sync round lands), and the receipt would still say *"the takes you were looking at joined"*
— the J8 copy-truth line this letter cites twice.

**One fact that narrows the reviewer's second option, and it argues for the first:** the pairing branch's
drift check compares **texts and count only** (`:1508–1514`: `current.count != snapshot.count ||
current.contains { snapshot[$0.id] != $0.workingText }`). A pure chosen RE-POINT — the exact input R2-2
describes — leaves every take's text and the fan's size unchanged, so *the pairing road would not catch
it either*. Giving the preset branch "the pairing branch's check" therefore does not close R2-2 unless
that check is widened to the rendered ids as well. **Composing the carry-all from the STATE's
`firstRenderedID`/`secondRenderedID`** does close it, is one line, and makes the sheet's own promise
literal — a take that has since died falls out through `CarryAllMerge.compose`'s existing nil →
`takesChangedMerge`, which is the refusal that road already speaks. That is the option I would take, and
if the check is widened as well it should be widened for BOTH branches, since the pairing road shares the
blind spot.

**R2-3 · AGREE (NIT).** Re-traced: the board's sheet roads pass `FannedDefaultsCopy.divided(kept: 0)`
(`TakesViewModel.swift:2244`, `:2270`) while the board's own instant road counts from the op's result
(`TakesStructureController.swift:571–573`, `kept: max(0, result.first.variants.count - 1)` plus
`stayedBehind:`). Truthful but thinner than the same act one door over — R1-9 was right that the retired
title had to go and the zero-count line was the safe landing; the count was always available on the
`divideW` road. The reviewer's split is right: only the `divideW` road can count "stayed on the first";
the duplicating road must keep the short line, because its others are COPIES on both blocks and
"stayed on the first" would misdescribe them.

**R2-4 · AGREE (NIT), as a reading.** Re-traced through the op: `divideWRows` mints each side with
`texts: side.map(\.text)`, `defaultIndex: renderedIndex ?? 0`, **`chosenIndex: renderedIndex`**
(`Operations+Structural.swift:485–490`), and `side` is the contributing takes in A's fan order — so
`result.second.variants[0]` is the first CONTRIBUTOR to C, which is the anchor only when `renderedIndex`
happens to be 0. Under keep-on-top C has exactly one contributor and the two coincide (the record's claim
holds); under keep-on-bottom, or a hand-placed sheet where another take parks at its start ahead of the
seed in fan order, the board's ring lands on a take that is neither the one she divided nor C's CHOSEN.
Write is unaffected (it lands a block, not a take). **The alternative needs no core change:**
`FanHalf.sourceVariantIDs` is public and index-aligned with `variants`, so
`result.second.sourceVariantIDs.firstIndex(of: <the anchor id>)` recovers `renderedIndex` at the call
site — the board can park on the chosen without the op growing a field. Worth recording beside the
alternative, since "we would need the op to tell us" would otherwise read as the reason not to.

**Counts: 4 AGREE · 0 DISPUTE** (1 MAJOR · 1 MINOR · 2 NIT). Two of the four are batch 1's own doctrine
applied on one side and not the other — R2-1 (the divide refresh carries its arrangement, the merge
refresh does not) and R2-2 (the pairing road refuses on drift, the preset roads do not) — and R2-1's root
cause is a silent memberwise init, which is why a rider that makes the omission a compile error is worth
more than the instance fix. Nothing here turns on undefined product behaviour, so nothing goes to
QUESTIONS; R2-4 is a reading for R1-13's PROVISIONAL at the closeout.

A chosen re-point committed to the store with the surface not yet caught up therefore passed the check and `ops.merge` joined a take the sheet never showed, under a row whose words are *"the others stay with their original blocks"* and a receipt saying *"the takes you were looking at joined"*. The implementer's rider — read the two blocks' chosen in the `database.read` the check already opens — adopted as the fix, through `Queries.renderPost(db, containerID:)` (ONE resolution rule, pinned equivalent to `Operations.renderedVariant`; one indexed query ∝ the note, on a deliberate confirm). **The projection-side compare is DROPPED, not kept beside it:** the op acts on the store, the projection is a view of it, and comparing both would refresh in a loop until the observation delivered (each confirm re-marking the sheet from the store while the projection still disagreed). `renderedIDs(of:)` left with its one caller. The refresh re-marks the shown pair from the STORE's truth. The implementer's alternative (routing the plain road through `mergeCeremony` with one pairing and no carries) was not taken, for its stated reason — it would cost the road its "Merge" undo label and its `speakAboutMergedFans` receipt. **Pin:** `MergeArrangementTests.aChosenRePointInTheStoreButNotYetOnTheSurfaceStillRefreshes` — the chosen re-pointed in the store with NO reload (non-vacuity: the surface still shows the head, the store already renders the spare); "Only the two shown" → the sheet REFRESHES (nothing joined, `firstRenderedID` re-marked from the store, the row kept); after the surface catches up the second ⏎ joins exactly what it shows, the head left in lineage. **Red on `6e14e9e8`, recorded:** `MergeArrangementTests.swift:451 try h.storeSlots().count == 2` failed (the pair joined from the store's re-pointed chosen) and `:452 mergeCeremony → nil`. #### Findings, severity-ranked

**R3-1 · [MINOR] · `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Takes/TakesViewModel.swift:2260–2261` (`runDivideW`'s receipt — R2-3's fix, `5f72d1e9`) · the board's SHEET receipt now counts with the INSTANT road's formula on two roads whose shape the formula does not fit — it overclaims where, before the fix, it claimed nothing.** Evidence (traced): `kept = first.variants.count − 1` assumes exactly one seed-half sits in `first`. That holds on the instant road (`TakesStructureController:572`, the formula's home): `KeepOnTopDivide.compose` (`KeepOnTopDivide.swift:105–125`) passes the seed's cut through untouched and *"a cut at the displayed take's very start or end is left for the op to refuse"*, so the instant seed is always split. The SHEET is not so bounded: (i) under keep-on-top the seed may park at its START — `presetBlocker` (`DivideWCeremony.swift:219`) blocks only `parkedAtEnd`, and round 1's cleared list names this shape legal (*"the seed rides whole into the bottom, the others onto the top"*); `arranged(.keepOnTop)` keeps the seed's own cut (`:386–398`); (ii) under "Choose per take…" any checked take may be cut by hand. Inputs → outcome: a three-take block, the sheet on take 1, ← to the start (cut 0), keep-on-top, ⏎ → `divideW` parks the seed WHOLE into C (`first.variants` = [take 2, take 3], `second.variants` = [take 1]) → `kept = 1` → the toast reads *"Block divided — the take you were in split across both blocks; 1 other take stayed on the first. ⌥⌘Z undoes."* — the seed did not split and TWO other takes stayed on the first. Under "Choose per take…" with another take cut in the middle, its head is counted as a take that *"stayed on the first"*. Before `5f72d1e9` both roads spoke `divided(kept: 0)` → *"Block divided — ⌥⌘Z undoes it."*, which claimed nothing (R1-9's deliberate landing). `FannedDefaultsCopy.divided(kept:stayedBehind:)` (`FannedDefaults.swift:187–201`) is the copy home and is correct for the shape it was written for. Write's `runDivideW` (`EditorSurfaceController.swift:2910–2919`) speaks no receipt, so this is the board's alone. J8 copy-truth — the class this letter rated MINOR throughout (R1-3). **Fix shape:** the op's `FanHalf.sourceVariantIDs` (index-aligned with `variants`, `Operations+Structural.swift:273–284`) names the shape exactly — speak the count only when the seed's id is in BOTH halves' `sourceVariantIDs` and every other id in `first` is absent from `second` (the instant shape: seed split, others whole on the first); otherwise the zero line, or a truthful line naming the seed riding whole (a `seedSplit:` flag on `divided`). **Pin (red on `d9c25f6e`):** the board's sheet, three takes, the seed at cut 0, `.keepOnTop`, confirm → the receipt does not contain "split across both" and does not read "1 other take stayed" (today it reads both).

**R3-2 · [MINOR] · `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Tutorial/TutorialSeeder.swift:985–988` (the deleting chapter's "what a block leaves behind" lesson, card 2) and `:1034` (its staged take's own text) · the field guide still TEACHES the retired 13j J4 duplicate default for ⌘⇧D — the U-2 sweep re-worded the structure chapter's divide lesson and missed this one.** Evidence: the lesson says *"Put your cursor in the middle of it and press ⌘⇧D: the take you are looking at splits across two new blocks, and the other take is copied whole onto both"*; the staged take she reads in the fan says *"I ride WHOLE onto both halves when you press ⌘⇧D"*. Since 18c batch 1 (U-2) ⌘⇧D keeps the other take whole on the TOP block (`runKeepOnTopDivide` → `divideW` with the others parked at their END); the receipt she gets says *"1 other take stayed on the first"* — the lesson predicts a shape the app no longer produces, in the chapter whose whole point is doing the acts. R-4's record knew the guide *"still promised the other way"* and fixed **`structure` ▸ "divide"** only; both texts date from 13j batch 7 (`428e2ce5`, `git log -S`), so no `eremony` grep could see them. The helper's rows (`HelperView.swift:97`, `:302–311`) and the structure chapter (`TutorialSeeder.swift:664–710`) are correct. **Fix shape:** the two sentences re-worded to keep-on-top (*"…the take you are looking at splits across two new blocks, and the other take stays whole on the first"*; the take's text likewise), the two test comments with them. **Sequencing (load-bearing):** her ONE restore act at the round's end re-seeds the book — this must land BEFORE the handoff's restore note, or the restore seeds the stale lesson and she owes a second restore. **Pin (red on `d9c25f6e`):** in `TutorialDeletionActsTests`, the lesson's prose does not contain "copied whole onto both" and the staged take's text does not contain "onto both halves"; plus, after `.divideAtCursor` on the staged fan, the two successors carry [2, 1] takes (green today — the act already keeps on top; it pins the words to the act).

**R3-3 · [MINOR] · `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Takes/TakesBottomBar.swift:73–91` (`structuralBar`) + `:42–66` (`renderedCapabilities`) · the handed-off (a), WEIGHED AND CONFIRMED MINOR: the Takes AND Lenses boards' `block ▾` group renders NEITHER ceremony row while the registry declares `.bottomBar` on both (`CapabilityRegistry.swift:196`, `:220`), and the walk cannot see it.** Evidence: `structuralBar` is Divide · Extract · Make Block · Merge; `renderedCapabilities` lists `.divide` and `.merge` and neither ceremony; `theBottomBarsRenderExactlyTheDeclaredBarDoors` (`CapabilityRegistryTests.swift:232–236`) asserts the UNION of the two bars, so `CommitBar` (`:112`, `:502`, `:508`) alone satisfies the declaration — and the bar's own header comment (*"Every declared door is still rendered here … the registry walk proves it"*) is exactly the claim the union cannot prove. **PIECE 5a — THE MEASUREMENT, and what it changed (the standing-17 rule 4).** Reporting on every caret
move meant a `UserDefaults` write per keystroke. Measured rather than assumed
(a throwaway probe, 10,000 calls, deleted after): **`CurrentBlockMemory.remember` ~103 µs per call, of
which ~102 µs is `UserDefaults.standard.set` itself** on this machine. O(1) in document size, so P1 is
not at risk — but ~100 µs of main-thread IPC per keystroke for a value nothing reads until a landing is
not a price worth paying. So the memory SPLIT: **the session dict takes every report (free); the
persisted copy is written when the block or its take changes, and once more when the note leaves the
screen** — `CurrentBlockMemory.persist(postID:)`, called from `CommandRouter.unregisterDetail` and from
`registerDetail` when the note changes. A caret walking inside one block now touches `UserDefaults` zero
times. The only thing a hard quit can cost is the offset's drift within the block she was last in — the
BLOCK is always current, so a relaunch still lands in the right paragraph, which is strictly better than
before this batch. Pinned (`LandingOffsetTests`, the memory pin's new arm: the walk inside a block is
not written; `persist` writes it).
