**PIECE 1 — the fact, the memory, the router, the reporters (DONE).**

- `FocusedBlock` gains `utf16Offset: Int?` (`BlockHistoryPane.swift`) — **nil is UNKNOWN, never zero**;
  the name is `LandingTarget`'s on purpose.
- `CurrentBlockMemory` persists `blockID|variantID|utf16Offset`; a two-field string written before this
  batch reads back with a nil offset (the behaviour it had when it was written). **`remember`'s nil rule:
  a report with no offset for the SAME block keeps what is known** — Blocks' focused row and the
  outline's card hold no caret, and letting them erase her place would land her at the top of the
  paragraph she was standing in the middle of, which is the whole complaint. A report for ANOTHER block
  resets it.
- **`CommandRouter`: the offset rides BESIDE the observed identity, never in it.** `focusedBlock` is now
  a computed read that merges a `private` observed `focusedBlockIdentity` (assigned only when the block
  or its take changes — row 33's cadence, unmoved) with an `@ObservationIgnored` `focusedOffset`
  (rewritten on every report). **Why:** the offset must be fresh at caret rate, and the one view that
  registers a `body` dependency on this fact (the Block History pane's jump door) must not re-render on
  every keystroke — which is the cost row 33's own comment exists to refuse. One value, one truth, two
  lifetimes. `noteFocusedBlock` gains `utf16Offset: Int? = nil`; `registerDetail`'s restore, the
  teardown and `forgetCurrentBlock` move both halves together.
- **Reporters:** Write's `updateCaretContext` now reports on EVERY caret move (the block-change guard is
  retired *for the report* — an offset stale by one paragraph of typing is the defect; the decor pass
  keeps its own guard); `TakesViewModel.notePaneSelection` reports the LIVE pane's caret (the live pane
  only — a read pane's selection is not a caret, P-3's line); Read's `edit` pane gains
  `onSelectionChange` → `BlockTOCViewModel.notePaneCaret`. Blocks' row and the Library's Open report nil
  and land at the start, as today.
- **THE TRACE THIS BATCH FOUND, AND ITS FIX (measured, not read):** with only the above, the round trip
  landed at **113 — the block's END**. A probe named the culprit: *a take pane going live reports its
  block's END* (`[probe] TAKES pane reports offset 54`), because a read pane's `view.string = text` had
  left AppKit's selection there and the ring's responder claim reported it. That report became the
  note's remembered offset. The honest answer is the brief's own walk step (*"→ Takes → the live pane's
  caret is there"*): **a pane going live LANDS at the note's remembered place.** `PaneSurfaceView`/its
  representable/its coordinator gain `caretOnGoingLive: Int?`, applied on the live TRANSITION
  (`noteLiveness`'s existing one-shot occasion) and **before** the responder claim, so the pane's own
  report is that place. Armed by the models at the focus door — `TakesViewModel.armPaneCaretLanding`
  (both `focus` and the N9 `landAuthorityFocus`; the room's `focusWithCaretAtEnd` clears it — the END was
  asked for explicitly) and `BlockTOCViewModel.makeLive` — and **only for the note's CURRENT block**, so
  a click into another block keeps the place the click brought. Read at render through
  `caretLanding(forVariant:)`, `@ObservationIgnored`: a caret place is data, not a signal.

**PIECE 2 — Write's landing (DONE).** `bareArrivalLanding(fact:)` = the block's start + the remembered
offset, clamped to the block's CURRENT length (it may have shrunk while she was away; landing past its
end would put her in the next block — worse than the start) and to the document; a nil offset is the
block's start, unchanged. The bare arrival's reveal is **`JumpPrimitive.jump`**, not
`scrollRangeToVisible` (TEXTKIT2 §2 / the Phase 6 hybrid road), with the arrival's next-turn responder
retry kept.

**One neighbouring pin edited, justified:** `BlockHistoryPaneTests.withNote` now forgets its note's
current-block memory on entry and exit. Every world mints the SAME post id (`.incrementing` from zero)
and the memory persists per note into `UserDefaults.standard`, so one suite's caret arrived as this
suite's remembered offset and its whole-fact equality read `utf16Offset: 0` — the order-dependence the
Act 10 batch-G record already names, widened by one field. The pin's assertion is untouched.

**PIECE 3 — the reveal (DONE; row 20).**

- **ONE reveal for every landing in Write** — `EditorViewModel.revealLanding(_:textView:)`, consumed by
  all three arms of `applyFocusSeeds`: the bare arrival, the `pendingCaretSeed` arm (the Blocks
  workbench's Open in Write, the Index column's "Jump to block in Write", Read's `onJump`, the canvas
  card's — all of them `PostDetailView.jump(to:)`), and `applyWriteIntent` (the fire-and-DO landing,
  which had the same hole: a follow-up gesture that runs off-screen is a gesture she cannot see happen).
- **It is PARKED, not performed on the spot — and that is the finding of this piece.** The first cut
  jumped inline and measured `caret line top 6937 · visible 24.0…743.0`: at landing time
  `setSurfaceContent` has just replaced the storage, the text view's FRAME is still the pre-fill height,
  so `JumpPrimitive`'s scroll range (`docHeight − viewport`) is a couple of dozen points and the
  correction loop clamps there. The reveal is therefore spent at the **N14 layout-settle hop**
  (`overlayLayoutDidSettle`'s async turn — the same post-layout occasion the badge/decor family already
  waits for, and the first moment the frame is honest). Safe by construction: every caller sits inside
  `applyFocusSeeds`, which runs only on the INITIAL RENDER, so a geometry generation always bumps first
  and the hop always arrives. **After: `caret line top 6944 · visible 6944.1…7663.1`** — the landing is
  pixel-exact at the viewport top, the Phase 6 memo's promise.
- **The reveal is GUARDED, and this is a judgment call worth her eye (J-note below):** a caret already
  inside the visible rect scrolls nothing. Reveal means *make visible*, never *move* — without it a bare
  arrival would haul the caret's line to the viewport top on every ⌘2-then-⌘1, overriding the scroll
  anchor `renderFull` had just restored. Pinned both ways (block 200 → the page jumps and lands exact;
  block 2 → `visible.minY == 0`, the page never moves). The reveal also abandons itself if the caret has
  moved since it was parked — a second jump must never yank her away from a place she chose.
- `JumpPrimitive` gains **`lineTop(ofOffset:in:)`** — the geometry the jump itself steers (fragment frame
  + container origin), so "is it on screen?" and "put it on screen" can never disagree. The pin reads the
  same function rather than a copy of it.

**MACHINERY HONESTY:** the two mounted landing pins, written as near-identical `@MainActor`
`withDependencies` closures, **crashed swift-frontend** in the `SendNonSendable` SIL pass (Xcode 26,
`swift-version 6`; `scratchpad/suite-3.log` has the backtrace). Factoring the world into one private

**PIECE 4 — the board's article goes where the landing went (DONE; row 8).**

Her words: *"open takes mode in write from the takes menu is also not automatically navigating you to
where the take is"* — the fan panel's "Open Takes mode" parked `.bloom`, the choreographer bloomed the
block and put the ring on its pane, and the article column stayed at the top. `TakesBoardLayout` (the
board's session-only view geometry — its exact remit) gains `articleLanding` / `landArticle(on:)` /
`clearArticleLanding()`, carrying a **`placementID`** because that is the article `ForEach`'s own
identity (the Act 10 ladder's R1-3 lesson: the identity IS the anchor; an explicit `.id` drifts under a
lazy column's estimate). `TakesChoreographer` sets it on the bloom arm **and** on the N9 arm when the
note's current block named the slot — the same event, one rule: *the board enters on the current block,
so the page shows it*. The fall-through to the first slot asks for nothing (it is already at the top;
asking would fight a restored position for nothing). `TakesBoardView.singleColumnArticle` gains a
`ScrollViewReader` and an `.onChange(of: model.articleLanding, initial: true)` that scrolls
**unanimated** to the id and spends the request, so a page she scrolls afterwards is never hauled back.
Pins (`TakesLandingTests` ×3 — model-level, because the real-click rig cannot mount the whole board,

  judgment calls below are mechanism, not product, and both are recorded where they happen.
- **Two judgment calls for the orchestrator's J-list.** **(1)** The offset rides
  `@ObservationIgnored` beside the observed identity rather than in it, so row 33's "reported only when
  the block changes" cadence is preserved exactly — a caret walking inside one paragraph still wakes no
  observer (the alternative, an observable write per keystroke, would re-render the Block History pane
  at typing rate). **(2)** The reveal is GUARDED: a caret already inside the visible rect scrolls
  nothing. Her words ask for "the page is scrolled to it"; without the guard a bare arrival hauls the

### R-6 · ADJUDICATION (the successor orchestrator, 2026-09-08)

**Verified against git, not trusted:** eight commits `35c11948..4dd54c54` on `main` == `origin/main`; `git diff --stat 48beaa9a..HEAD` — 19 files, all under `App/Variorum/Variorum/`, `App/Variorum/VariorumTests/`, this plan and `.gitignore`; `Sources/` and `App/Variorum/Shared/` empty diffs as the record says; 14 captures on disk; captures 03 · 05 · 08 read and matching the record's claims (block 7/215 with the caret at "Sh|erlock" after Write → Takes → Write; block 162/215 at the viewport top from the Blocks workbench; the fan panel's landing bloomed at block 3/4 of the field-guide note). Closing run `350/61` claimed, log local. **Rows 7 · 8 · 20 · 42 — FIXED, accepted**, pending the Fable round.

**Judgment calls (for her morning):**
- **(J18) The two mechanism calls the implementer flagged — ACCEPTED as built.** (1) The caret offset rides `@ObservationIgnored` beside the observed block identity in `CommandRouter`, so the Act 10 row-33 cadence (observers wake only when the block or take changes) is preserved and the Block History pane never re-renders at typing rate. (2) **The reveal is GUARDED:** a landing whose caret is already inside the visible rect scrolls nothing — "reveal" means make visible, never move. Reason: without it every ⌘2-then-⌘1 would haul the caret line to the viewport top, overriding the restored scroll anchor. Her words ("the page is scrolled to it") are satisfied whenever the caret is off screen. If she prefers the unguarded form (always land the line at the top), it is one condition to remove.
- **(J19) Piece 5a's coalescing — ACCEPTED.** Measured `UserDefaults.standard.set` at ~102 µs per call; the persisted copy is now written at block/take changes and when the note leaves the screen (`CurrentBlockMemory.persist`), the session dict taking every caret report. A hard kill (never a ⌘Q — the router's unregister persists) can cost only the offset drift inside the block she was last in; the block itself is always current. Capture 11 shows the persisted offset surviving a full relaunch.
- **A pointer correction, recorded:** Blocks → Write does not run through a `WriteIntent` (the brief's and the ledger's reading); it runs `PostDetailView.jump(to:)` → `EditorView(caretSeed:)` → `applyFocusSeeds`' seed arm, which set the selection and never scrolled. Row 20's mechanism, one road over; both doors now consume the one `revealLanding`. Ledger row 20's trace column is superseded by this record.
- **Machinery note:** the batch's first commits swept `scratchpad/` logs into git with `git add -A` (undone and gitignored at `3aa81980`; nothing canon touched). Path-scoped `git add` is the standing rule; restated in the next briefs.

#### Findings, severity-ranked

**R1-1 · BLOCKER · `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Takes/PostDetailView.swift:95, :343–344, :682` + `Features/Editor/EditorView.swift:60` + `Features/Editor/EditorViewModel.swift:1820–1839, :1879` · after ONE jump into Write (Blocks' *open in write*, the Index column's jump, Read's `onJump`, the canvas card, `initialBlockID`), EVERY later mount of Write in that note visit re-lands at the jump's block START — the remembered offset is never consulted again, and the memory is overwritten with offset 0.** Mechanism: `jump(to:)` sets `@State pendingCaret` (`:682`) and it is never cleared anywhere (the only writes are `:95`'s declaration and `:682`). The Write branch constructs `EditorView(session:postID:caretSeed: pendingCaret)` (`:343`); a mode switch away and back destroys and re-creates that branch's view, so `EditorView.init` runs again and sets `model.pendingCaretSeed = caretSeed` on a FRESH model (`EditorView.swift:59–62`). `applyFocusSeeds` then takes the seed arm (`EditorViewModel.swift:1820`): selection = the block's start, `caretClaimed = true` (`:1838`), so the bare arrival — the only arm that reads `bareArrivalLanding(fact:)` — is skipped (`:1879`). The seed arm's `updateCaretContext()` (`:1825`) then reports `(block, variant, 0)` through `noteFocusedBlock`, so the remembered offset becomes 0 as well. Concrete road: Blocks ▸ *open in write* on block 162 → type mid-paragraph → ⌘2 (the pane lands at the typed place, correct) → ⌘1 → the caret is at block 162's START — her U-20 complaint verbatim, one gesture after the batch's own row-20 door; and thereafter, Takes → click block 30's pane → ⌘1 → Write lands at block 162's start, not block 30 (the Act 10 "one current block" rule broken too). The walk never exercised jump-then-round-trip on one note visit (rows 8 → 42 detoured through the field-guide note, which re-created the `PostDetailView` and reset `pendingCaret`), and the continuity pin's Blocks step uses the row click, never the jump. The seed's persistence PREDATES the batch, but the batch edited exactly this arm (`:1826–1837`), claims "every door into Write lands at the remembered caret offset", and rows 7 · 42 are dispositioned FIXED on it. Fix: the seed is one-shot — `PostDetailView` clears `pendingCaret` in `.onChange(of: mode)` when the new mode is not `.write` (the `EditorView` is already gone there, so the `.id` change at `:344` remounts nothing; clearing while IN Write would remount the editor). Pin (red on this tree, the continuity rig): mount `PostDetailView(session:postID:initialBlockID: blocks[1])` → Write lands at block 2's start → `setSelectedRange(blockTwo.location + 6)` → settle → ⌘2 → settle (pane live) → ⌘1 → `selectedRange().location == blockTwo.location + 6` (red: `blockTwo.location`).

**R1-2 · MAJOR · `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Takes/TakesViewModel.swift:517–522, :579–586` + `Features/Takes/TakesShared.swift:55, :63` + `Surface/PaneSurface.swift:529–533, :769–771, :802–811, :942–946` · a CLICK into a not-yet-live pane of the note's current block is yanked to the remembered offset — the click's own place is discarded.** Mechanism: `PaneTextView.mouseDown` runs `super.mouseDown` first (`:529`, the selection lands at the click point — the pane is `isSelectable`), then `onPlainClick` → `coordinator.onFocusRequest` (`:769–771`) → `TakesCellView`'s `model.focus(key)` (`TakesShared.swift:55`) → `armPaneCaretLanding(for:)` (`:522`), which arms whenever `fact.blockID == pane.blockID` and an offset is known (`:580–582`) — it never asks whether a click brought its own place, and it does not compare the VARIANT. The re-render sets `coordinator.caretOnGoingLive` (`:808`) and `noteLiveness(true)` (`:811`) runs the live transition, whose `setSelectedRange(landing)` (`:942–946`) overwrites the click's selection before the responder claim. Definite road: the LENSES board — every column is a `TakesCellView` (`LensesBoardView.swift:517`) showing a different take of the SAME block; column A live at offset 200, click column B at offset 10 → B goes live at min(200, len B). Before the batch B went live at 10. (The Takes board's article column is one pane per block, so a click there always targets another block → armed nil → unaffected; the fan-strip `CollapsibleTakeCard` builds its `PaneSurfaceView` without `caretOnGoingLive` (`TakesBoardView.swift:1289–1294`) → unaffected.) The record's rule — *"a click into another block keeps the place the click brought"* — was written for another BLOCK and missed another TAKE. Fix (shared with R1-3, one place): the coordinator knows when a click brought the place — `onPlainClick` sets a `clickBroughtPlace` flag before `onFocusRequest`; `noteLiveness`'s transition leaves the selection alone when the flag is set (and clears it), and applies `caretOnGoingLive` only for a programmatic transition. Pin (red on this tree, `PaneSurfaceTests`' rig): mount a read pane with `caretOnGoingLive: 65`, `setSelectedRange(10)`, invoke `view.onPlainClick?(10)` whose `onFocusRequest` flips the host's `isLive` to true, re-render → `selectedRange().location == 10` (red: 65).

**R1-3 · MAJOR · `App/Variorum/Variorum/Surface/PaneSurface.swift:942–952` + `Features/Takes/TakesViewModel.swift:1749–1759` + `Features/Takes/TakesChoreographer.swift:191–205` + `Features/TOC/BlockTOCView.swift:233–247, :271–279` · a PROGRAMMATIC ring landing with NO remembered offset leaves the pane's caret at the take's END, the live pane then REPORTS that END as "where she was", and the next door into Write lands at the END of the paragraph — a regression from "the block's start as today", on every nil-offset road.** Mechanism: the arm is `nil` whenever `fact.utf16Offset` is nil (`TakesViewModel.swift:580–582`; `BlockTOCView.swift:256–258`); with `caretOnGoingLive == nil` the transition does nothing (`PaneSurface.swift:942`) and the selection stays where AppKit's `string =` replacement left it — the END (the record's own probe: *"a take pane going live reports its block's END … `[probe] TAKES pane reports offset 54`"*). The responder claim's selection notification reaches `onSelectionChange` (`:1112–1115`) → `notePaneSelection` → `noteFocusedBlock(… utf16Offset: END)` (`TakesViewModel.swift:1755–1758`) — the memory now says END. Concrete road, unwalked: Blocks' row click on block 162 reports `nil` (`BlocksWorkbenchView.swift:523`; a DIFFERENT block than before, so the memory's offset resets to nil) → ⌘2 → the N9 landing (`TakesChoreographer.swift:195–198`) rings pane 162 with `caretLanding == nil` → caret at END → reported → ⌘1 → `bareArrivalLanding` = 162's start + min(END, length) = the paragraph's END. Before the batch this road landed at the START; the record itself calls landing past the place *"worse than the start"*. The same on the N9 fall-through (a note opened fresh in Takes, then ⌘1 → the first block's END), the Library's Open → Takes → Write, and Read's `edit` face entered with no memory. **The orchestrator's note (a)** — the Blocks workbench's context panel parks its pane's caret at the END (`BlocksWorkbenchView.swift:854–859`, a `BlockTOCViewModel.makeLive` host with no `caretOnGoingLive`) — is this class, one surface over. Fix (with R1-2's flag): a programmatic transition applies `caretOnGoingLive ?? 0` — the take's START when nothing is remembered, Write's own nil rule (Act 10 rows 39/49; the batch's *"land at the block's start as today"*) applied to the pane; and the workbench panel passes `caretOnGoingLive` like its siblings. The pane-side START is the spec-defensible choice, built PROVISIONAL: if the implementer disputes it on product grounds (the END as "continue writing"), it is QUESTIONS #97, never a ruling here — the REPORTED-END half is a bug either way. Pins (red on this tree): (a) `PaneSurfaceTests`' rig — a pane flipped live programmatically with `caretOnGoingLive: nil` → `selectedRange().location == 0` (red: the text's length); (b) the continuity rig from Blocks' row click (nil offset) → ⌘2 → ⌘1 → `selectedRange().location == blockTwoRange.location` (red: `blockTwoRange.upperBound`) and `router.currentBlock(for:)?.utf16Offset == 0`.

**R1-4 · MAJOR · `App/Variorum/Variorum/Registry/CommandRouter.swift:65, :112` + `VariorumAppDelegate.swift:53–69` + `Features/Takes/PostDetailView.swift:489–493` · the persisted caret offset is NEVER written at quit — `persist` rides only `registerDetail` (a note change) and `unregisterDetail` (`onDisappear`), and neither runs at app termination; the record's claim "a hard kill (never a ⌘Q — the router's unregister persists)" is false, and capture 11's evidence was produced by the pre-5a binary.** Mechanism: `applicationShouldTerminate` (`:53–69`) awaits the editor's `finalizeWork` and the `quitFinalize` registry and replies; nothing there — and nothing registered with `quitFinalize` (`QuickNoteView` is the only registrant) — touches `CurrentBlockMemory`; `PostDetailView.onDisappear` (`:489`) does not fire when the process exits, so `unregisterDetail`'s `persist` (`:112`) never runs; `MacWillLock.swift:47` is the same. Since piece 5a, `remember` writes only on a block/take change (`CurrentBlockMemory.swift:81–83`), so after ⌘Q the persisted offset is the block's ENTRY offset (where the caret was the moment that block became current), never where she left. Capture 11's ⌘Q was on the pieces-1…4 binary (the walk table: *"captures 01–10 … the piece-1…4 binary"*, and the bonus row is *"⌘Q, rebuild, relaunch"*), whose `remember` wrote UserDefaults on every report (`git show a0aade08:…/CurrentBlockMemory.swift`, the unconditional `defaults.set`); the FINAL binary only read that value. J19's "a hard kill … can cost only the drift" is therefore true of ⌘Q as well. Fix: `CommandRouter.persistCurrentBlocks()` (persists the active note's session fact) called from `applicationShouldTerminate`'s BOTH branches (before `.terminateNow`, and before `reply(toApplicationShouldTerminate:)`) and from `MacWillLock`'s road — or the router observes `NSApplication.willTerminateNotification`; the same method is what a future crash-safety tick would call. Pin (red on this tree): `registerDetail` → `noteFocusedBlock(b1, v1, offset: 3)` (persisted at the block change) → `noteFocusedBlock(b1, v1, offset: 55)` → invoke the termination hook → `CurrentBlockMemory().current(for:)?.utf16Offset == 55` from a fresh instance (red: 3).

**R1-5 · MAJOR (timing-dependent — traced by reading, not observed; the pin drives the ordering explicitly) · `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Editor/EditorViewModel.swift:1917–1931, :1938–1953, :2845–2869` (with `:352`, `:1002`, `:626–636`) · the parked reveal can be spent by a settle hop scheduled for an OLDER geometry generation, i.e. against the pre-fill frame the park exists to wait out — the row-20 defect recurring under a timing window.** Mechanism: `attach` bumps the generation to G₁ (`:352`); the empty editor's first display pass runs `overlayLayoutDidSettle` (`:2845`), which schedules hop(G₁) via `DispatchQueue.main.async`. The projection's first value arrives as a main-actor job (`observeSlots` → `applyProjection`, `:626–636`) — FIFO on the same main queue as the hop. If the fetch completes between `attach` and that display pass, the delivery job sits AHEAD of hop(G₁): the next drain runs `renderFull` (`storageDidEdit` bumps to G₂, `:1002`) and `applyFocusSeeds` parks the reveal (`:1931`), then hop(G₁) runs `consumePendingReveal` (`:2858`) with no display pass in between — the text view's frame is still the pre-fill height (the record's own measurement: *"the text view's FRAME is still the pre-fill height, so `JumpPrimitive`'s scroll range … is a couple of dozen points"*), the caret is not visible, `jump` clamps at `docHeight − viewport ≈ 24–48`, and the park is cleared (`:1940`). The following display pass sees G₂ ≠ delivered G₁ and schedules hop(G₂), which finds nothing parked. Landing short — *"puts your cursor in the right place but doesn't move you there"*. The record's "a geometry generation always bumps first and the hop always arrives" is true; what it misses is that a hop for the EARLIER generation may already be queued behind the park. Fix (one line each side): stamp the park with `overlayGeometryGeneration`; the hop consumes only when its captured `generation >= pendingRevealGeneration`. Pin (red on this tree; needs `overlayLayoutDidSettle` and `pendingRevealOffset` test-visible, the `pendingIntentTask` precedent): attach → `overlayLayoutDidSettle()` (hop for G₁ queued) → `applyProjection(slots)` synchronously with a current-block fact far down the note (parks) → drain the main queue → `pendingRevealOffset != nil` (red: nil — consumed by the stale hop).

**R1-6 · MINOR · `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Editor/EditorViewModel.swift:1887–1898` + `Features/Editor/EditorSurfaceController.swift:159–162` · the bare arrival exempts a landing at offset 0 from the reveal, and the reveal is now visibility-guarded anyway — so the exemption only ever HIDES a caret.** Mechanism: `renderFull` restores the note's session scroll anchor through `JumpPrimitive.jump` (`EditorSurfaceController.swift:159–162`, `editorRouter.scrollAnchors[postID]`); a note she scrolled mid-document and left with the caret at the document's very start (a real `utf16Offset == 0` on block 1, or no memory at all) then lands at 0 with the page restored mid-document, and `if landing > 0` skips the reveal — "⌘1 then type" types off-screen. The old code had the same guard, but it guarded a `scrollRangeToVisible` that moved the page on every arrival; the new reveal moves nothing when the caret is on screen (`:1947–1950`), so the `> 0` exemption no longer protects anything. Fix: drop the `landing > 0` condition (the visibility guard is the whole rule). Pin (red on this tree): seed `editorRouter.scrollAnchors[postID]` at block 200's offset, forget the memory, mount → the caret at 0 → `visible.minY == 0` after the settle (red: the restored anchor's y).

**R1-7 · MINOR (cleanliness; no pin) · `App/Variorum/VariorumTests/BlockHistoryPaneTests.swift:758–764` + `LandingOffsetTests.swift:99–129` + `CommandRoutingTests.swift` (the three `TakesLandingTests` pins) + `Registry/CurrentBlockMemory.swift:64, :87` · the Mac suites write and erase the DEV-STORE APP's `UserDefaults.standard` current-block memories (the test host IS `io.lduarte.Variorum.debug`), for post ids the `.incrementing` UUID mints identically in every world — the recorded Act 10 batch-G order dependence, now one field wider and with NO KnownIssues owner.** What degrades: a suite that mounts an editor for post-id-0 leaves an offset behind that any later suite comparing a whole `FocusedBlock` (now offset-bearing) reads as its own state — the implementer met exactly this and patched the one suite that broke (`withNote`'s forget/defer), which is adequate for that suite and leaves the class in place for the next whole-fact comparison; and every run rewrites the developer's real dev-store memories for whichever notes share those ids. Under CLAUDE.md's rule (nothing unowned): either inject the defaults (`CurrentBlockMemory` takes its `UserDefaults` from the session — a `suiteName` under test, `.standard` in the app; the memory API already threads `defaults:` through every method, so the seam is a one-line stored property) or a KnownIssues row owned by 12 (Hardening) beside the rig row. The `withNote` edit itself (the orchestrator's note (c)) is correct as far as it goes.

**R1-8 · NIT · `App/Variorum/VariorumTests/LandingOffsetTests.swift:44` · `UserDefaults(suiteName: "landing-offset-<uuid>")` is never removed — one orphan plist per run in the test host's preferences.** Fix: `defer { defaults.removePersistentDomain(forName: name) }`.

**R1-9 · NIT · `App/Variorum/Variorum/Registry/CommandRouter.swift:184` · `focusedBlockIdentity = fact` stores the RAW report's `utf16Offset` inside the observed half (the computed `focusedBlock` overrides it on read, so nothing reads it) — dead data that invites the next reader to trust the wrong half.** Fix: strip it (`var identity = fact; identity.utf16Offset = nil`) so the two lifetimes cannot be confused; same at `:67`.

**R1-10 · NIT · `App/Variorum/VariorumTests/LandingOffsetTests.swift:99–129` · `theRouterCarriesTheOffsetWithoutMovingTheObservedIdentity` asserts values only; the cadence its name promises — a same-block offset report wakes no observer — is J18(1)'s load-bearing claim and is unpinned.** It holds by inspection (`focusedOffset` is `@ObservationIgnored`, `focusedBlockIdentity` is assigned only in the guarded branch, `currentBlocks` is `@ObservationIgnored` at `:191`); a `withObservationTracking` arm (track `router.focusedBlock`; report the same block at another offset → `onChange` not called; report another block → called) makes it a pin. One arm, no code change.

**R1-11 · NIT · `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Editor/EditorViewModel.swift:1949` · the reveal's visibility test reads the line's TOP only (`top <= visible.maxY − 1`), so a landing on a line whose top is inside the viewport but whose body is clipped at the bottom counts as visible and reveals nothing.** Fix: test the line's bottom too (`FragmentLines` already yields the line; `typographicBounds.maxY` is one property away).

#### Considered and cleared (traced, not findings)

- **The orchestrator's note (b)** (the fan panel's "Open Takes mode" on a four-block note scrolls nothing): correct by geometry — `scrollTo` on content that fits the viewport is a no-op; the request is still made and spent (`TakesBoardView.swift:608–611`). The row-8 mechanism is real and the ⌘2 landing on block 162 of 215 (capture 06) is the honest evidence. Cleared.
- **The orchestrator's note (c)** (the `withNote` edit): see R1-7 — the edit is right; the class it patches around is what needs an owner.
- **P1 under per-caret reporting:** `updateCaretContext` → `noteFocusedBlock` → `remember` is O(1) (a dictionary write; the persisted write only on a block/take change, `CurrentBlockMemory.swift:81–83`); `currentBlocks` is `@ObservationIgnored` (`CommandRouter.swift:191`), so a body calling `currentBlock(for:)` registers no dependency on it. No cost ∝ document. Cleared.
- **The fill's transient report:** `renderFull` ends with `updateCaretContext()` on the fill's end-of-document selection (`EditorSurfaceController.swift:164`), which now writes `(last block, END)` into the memory; the arrival fact is read BEFORE the fill (`EditorViewModel.swift:674`) and the landing's own report corrects the memory in the same synchronous run. Two persisted writes per Write entry (~200 µs), no wrong landing. Cleared (and pre-existing in shape).
- **The inline book/pool editor:** `revealLanding` refuses a nil `scrollView`; the bare arrival was already gated `scrollView != nil` (`:1879`) and the seed/intent arms never scrolled, so nothing the inline editor did before is lost. Cleared.

The walk's second ⌘2 landing on block 162 (capture 06) is consistent with this. Cleared.
- **`bareArrivalLanding`'s clamp:** `range(ofEntryAt:)` excludes the separator (`BlockRangeIndex.swift:74–80`) and `updateCaretContext` attributes an inclusive end to the same block (`:2771`), so a clamped landing never reads as the next block. Cleared.

- **`focusWithCaretAtEnd` vs the arm:** the arm is cleared synchronously after `focus` (`TakesViewModel.swift:565`) before any render; `applyCaretToEndIfRequested` runs last in `updateNSView`. No conflict. Cleared.
- **Register/unregister ordering on a note switch:** whichever runs first persists the leaving note (`registerDetail` persists `activePostID` before restoring; `unregisterDetail` is token-guarded). Cleared — the quit path is the hole (R1-4), not the switch.

#### 17o batch b round 1 — the implementer's responses

*Written by the batch-b implementer (opus-implementer) under the cross-model flow: agree/dispute with
rationale, traced against the code in this session — no fix implemented, no code or test file touched;
the only file this edits is the plan. **11 AGREED · 0 DISPUTED.** The reviewer makes the final call on
each and implements its confirmed fixes itself.*

**R1-1 (BLOCKER, the never-cleared caret seed) — AGREE.** Traced and confirmed: `pendingCaret` is
written at exactly one place (`PostDetailView.swift:682`), read at `:343`/`:344`, and cleared **nowhere**
— `.onChange(of: mode)` (`:517–519`) writes the mode memory, tells the router and closes the history
pane, and never touches it. The Write branch is a `switch mode` case (`:341–344`), so leaving Write
removes the `EditorView` and returning re-runs `EditorView.init` → `model.pendingCaretSeed = caretSeed`
(`EditorView.swift:59–62`) on a fresh model; `applyFocusSeeds`' seed arm (`EditorViewModel.swift:1820–
1839`) then sets the block's START and `caretClaimed = true`, so the bare arrival — the only arm that
reads `bareArrivalLanding(fact:)` (`:1879`) — never runs, and the arm's own `updateCaretContext()`
reports offset 0 into the memory. **This is my defect, not a pre-existing one:** the seed's persistence
predates the batch, but the batch put the offset road behind it and dispositioned rows 7 · 42 FIXED on a
claim this road falsifies. My walk missed it exactly as the reviewer says — rows 8 → 42 detoured through
the field-guide note, which re-created `PostDetailView` and reset the `@State`.
**Riders (fix shape).** (1) The pin must carry the reviewer's SECOND road as its own arm: after the
stale seed, Takes → click a DIFFERENT block's pane → ⌘1 must land in THAT block. A pin that only checks
the offset would go green on a fix that cleared the offset but left the stale block — and it is the Act
10 "one current block" rule that breaks there, not just U-20. (2) The reviewer's placement is the right
one and the reason is worth keeping in the comment: clearing on LEAVING Write is safe precisely because
the `.id` at `:344` is out of the tree at that moment; clearing while IN Write changes the `.id`,
remounts the editor mid-visit and re-runs the arrival — so the tempting "clear it where it is consumed"
shape must not be used.

**R1-2 (MAJOR, a click into a not-live pane of the current block is yanked) — AGREE.** Traced:
`PaneTextView` sets `isSelectable = true` unconditionally (`PaneSurface.swift:543`), so on a READ pane
`super.mouseDown` (`:529`) really does land the click's selection before `onPlainClick` (`:533`) →
`onFocusRequest` → `TakesCellView`'s `model.focus(key)` (`TakesShared.swift:55`);
`armPaneCaretLanding` (`TakesViewModel.swift:579–586`) compares `fact.blockID == pane.blockID` and
nothing else, so it arms for another TAKE of the same block, and the live transition's
`setSelectedRange` (`PaneSurface.swift:942–946`) overwrites the click before the responder claim.
**The reviewer's scoping is exactly right, and I verified it by grep rather than by reading its word for
it:** only two call sites pass `caretOnGoingLive` — `TakesShared.swift:65` (`TakesCellView`, used by the
Takes article column AND every Lenses column) and `ReadEditPane.swift:153`; the fan/stack cards
(`TakesBoardView.swift:1289`, `:1476`) pass none. The Takes ARTICLE column is one pane per block, so a
click there always targets another block (armed nil); Read's non-live branch is a `Text` stack
(`ReadEditPane.swift:145–160`) that brings no caret at all. **Lenses is the one live road.** My record's
rule — *"a click into another block keeps the place the click brought"* — is the wording that missed
another TAKE of the same block.
**Riders.** (1) The flag should be set in `PaneTextView.mouseDown` itself, not inside the
`onPlainClick` closure, so the ⌘-click claim path (`:524–528`, which returns before
`super.mouseDown`) can never leave it set. (2) It must be cleared unconditionally at the end of
`noteLiveness` — including when no transition ran — or a click that does not change liveness leaves it
armed for a later programmatic landing.

**R1-3 (MAJOR, a nil-offset programmatic ring lands at the take's END, reports it, and Write honors it)
— AGREE on the bug; I do NOT dispute the pane-side START on product grounds.** Traced: with
`caretOnGoingLive == nil` the transition does nothing (`PaneSurface.swift:942`) and the selection stays
where the read pane's `view.string = text` left it — the END; the responder claim's notification then
reaches `onSelectionChange` → `notePaneSelection` → `noteFocusedBlock(… utf16Offset: END)`
(`TakesViewModel.swift:1755–1758`), so the memory says END and `bareArrivalLanding` lands there. **This
is a regression I introduced**, and it is worse than the reviewer states in one respect worth recording:
`notePaneSelection` existed before this batch but only fed `choreography.paneSelection` — the END was
always there and was always harmless because nothing carried it to Write. The batch is what made the
artifact load-bearing. The road is unwalked because my walk always had a remembered offset by the time a
pane went live.
**On the product edge:** I do not dispute START. The case for END is the N9 "just start typing"
doctrine (an arriving surface is for typing, and appending is what "continue" means); the case for START
is that Write's own no-memory landing IS the block's start (Act 10 rows 39/49) and this batch promised
*"surfaces with no caret … land at the block's start as today"*. START is the spec-defensible one and I
would build it. But it belongs on the ratify list as **QUESTIONS #97** exactly as the reviewer routes
it, for a reason neither of us should paper over: the END was never a ruling — it is AppKit's leftover —
and turning an artifact into a decision is hers, not ours. The REPORTED-END half is a bug either way and
is not PROVISIONAL.
**Riders (a real interaction the fix must not break).** (1) `focusWithCaretAtEnd`
(`TakesViewModel.swift:554–566`) CLEARS the arm so the room's landing gets the END. With
`caretOnGoingLive ?? 0`, a pane **born live** under a room request would land at 0, because
`makeNSView` ADOPTS the caret-to-end baseline rather than applying it (`PaneSurface.swift:783–785`;
`applyCaretToEndIfRequested` runs last in `updateNSView` only) — so the `?? 0` default would win where
the END stands today. The fix should either have `focusWithCaretAtEnd` arm the END explicitly, or have
`noteLiveness` skip the default while a caret-to-end token is outstanding. (2) **Correcting my own
record's note (a), which the finding cites:** the Blocks workbench panel's pane
(`BlocksWorkbenchView.swift:854–863`) passes neither `onSelectionChange` NOR `caretOnGoingLive`, so its
caret-at-END reports nothing and is cosmetic — it is not a landing defect. Passing `caretOnGoingLive`
there is a consistency improvement I support; passing `onSelectionChange` would mint a FOURTH reporter,
which this batch deliberately did not do and which should not ride a ladder fix.

**R1-4 (MAJOR, the persisted offset is never written at quit; capture 11's evidence is from the pre-5a
binary) — AGREE, including the half that is against my own record.** Traced:
`applicationShouldTerminate` (`VariorumAppDelegate.swift:53–68`) awaits
`editorRouter.active?.finalizeWork()` and `quitFinalize.finalizeAll()`; the only registrants are
`QuickNoteView.swift:179` and `AppSession.swift:219` (the per-post autosave holds) — none touches
`CurrentBlockMemory`; `PostDetailView.onDisappear` (`:489–493`) does not run at process exit, so
`unregisterDetail`'s `persist` (`CommandRouter.swift:112`) never runs at ⌘Q. Since `edc16c1b` the
persisted offset is therefore the block's ENTRY offset. **And the evidence half is right and mine to
own:** the walk table's bonus row credits capture 11 to "piece 5a's coalescing proving itself", but that
⌘Q ran on the pieces-1…4 binary, whose `remember` wrote `UserDefaults` on every report
(`git show a0aade08:App/Variorum/Variorum/Registry/CurrentBlockMemory.swift`); the final binary only
READ that value. What capture 11 honestly proves is "the memory survives a relaunch" — not the write
path. That sentence in the record is wrong and should be corrected in the fix pass (I am not editing my
own record's claim under this flow; naming it here is the correction of record).
**Rider.** Call the new `persistCurrentBlocks()` on `MacWillLock`'s road too (`MacWillLock.swift:47`) —
locking the store is a leave-the-note event of the same kind — and keep it in BOTH branches of
`applicationShouldTerminate` as the reviewer says rather than on `willTerminateNotification` alone, so
the `.terminateNow` path is covered without depending on notification delivery order.

**R1-5 (MAJOR, timing — the parked reveal spendable by a stale-generation hop) — AGREE.** Traced the
machinery rather than the window: `attach` bumps at `EditorViewModel.swift:352`;
`overlayLayoutDidSettle` (`:2845–2869`) captures `let generation` at schedule time and the hop marks
`overlayGenerationDelivered = generation` and calls `consumePendingReveal()` FIRST, which clears the
park unconditionally at `:1940`; the park is set inside `applyFocusSeeds` (`:1931`), which runs in
`applyProjection`'s `.initialRender` arm (`:664–676`). So a hop queued for G₁ before the projection
drains can spend the park against the pre-fill frame — and the record's own justification for parking
at all is precisely that the frame is not honest at that moment, so I cannot call the window
theoretical. I did not observe it (every walk landing was pixel-exact), which is consistent with a
narrow ordering window, not with its absence.
**Riders.** (1) The refusing hop must LEAVE the park, not clear it — and re-arming is already
guaranteed: `storageDidEdit` bumped G₂ (`:1002`) while `overlayGenerationDelivered` becomes G₁, so the
next display pass schedules hop(G₂). (2) Worth one more pin arm while the seam is open: the park should
not survive into ANOTHER note's editor — `pendingRevealOffset` is per-model and models are per-mount,
so I believe it cannot, but the pin that proves the generation stamp is the cheapest place to say so.

**R1-6 (MINOR, the `landing > 0` exemption) — AGREE.** Verified: the exemption at
`EditorViewModel.swift:1887`, the anchor restore it can hide behind at
`EditorSurfaceController.swift:159–162`, and the visibility guard that makes the exemption pointless at

`scrollRangeToVisible` that moved the page on every arrival, and the new reveal already moves nothing
when the caret is on screen. **Rider:** dropping it costs one `JumpPrimitive.lineTop` per bare arrival —
the same single-fragment `ensuresLayout` the jump itself would perform, so P1 is unmoved.

**R1-7 (MINOR, the suites write the dev-store app's `UserDefaults.standard`) — AGREE.** Verified: the
memory threads `defaults:` through every method (`CurrentBlockMemory.swift:42`, `:64`, `:78`, `:84`) but
the ROUTER holds no seam (`CommandRouter.swift:191` constructs the memory bare), and the test host is
`io.lduarte.Variorum.debug`, so every mounted-editor suite writes real app domains for `.incrementing`
post ids. My `withNote` edit patched the one suite that broke and left the class standing — the reviewer
is right that under CLAUDE.md's rule that class now needs an owner.
**Rider on the fix shape (a preference, not a dispute).** In THIS round I would take the KnownIssues row
(owner 12, beside the rig row) over the injection: the injection needs a `UserDefaults` on
`CommandRouter` and a way for a test world to set it, which reaches `AppSession`'s construction — wider
than a ladder fix should carry and touching a seam no finding requires. If the reviewer prefers the

**R1-8 (NIT, the orphan suite plist) — AGREE.** `LandingOffsetTests.swift:44` mints
`UserDefaults(suiteName:)` and never removes the domain; `removePersistentDomain(forName:)` in a `defer`
is the whole fix.

**R1-9 (NIT, the raw offset stored inside the observed half) — AGREE.** Verified both sites:
`CommandRouter.swift:184` (`focusedBlockIdentity = fact`) and `:67` (`= restored`). **Rider:** at `:67`
the strip must come AFTER `focusedOffset = restored?.utf16Offset` on the next line, or the restore loses
the offset it exists to carry — the two lines are adjacent and the order is load-bearing.

**R1-10 (NIT, the cadence is unpinned) — AGREE.** The name promises what J18(1) rests on, and only the
values are asserted (`LandingOffsetTests.swift:99–129`). **Rider:** the `withObservationTracking` arm
should assert the POSITIVE as well — a BLOCK change DOES wake the observer — or the pin would pass on a
router that notifies nobody at all, which is the failure the cadence claim is worth guarding against.

**R1-11 (NIT, the visibility test reads the line's top only) — AGREE.** `EditorViewModel.swift:1949`
tests `top <= visible.maxY − 1`. **Rider:** `JumpPrimitive.lineTop` returns only the top, so the fix
needs the line's bounds — either a second return or a `lineRect(ofOffset:in:)` beside it, both reading
the same `typographicBounds` the function already has in hand — and the existing 1-pt slack should be
kept so a line flush with the bottom edge still reveals rather than sitting half-clipped.

**On the "considered and cleared" list:** I read all ten and agree with every clearance. Two are worth a
sentence back. The **fill's transient report** clearance is right and I had not traced it — `renderFull`
ends with `updateCaretContext()` on the fill's end-of-document selection
(`EditorSurfaceController.swift:164`), and the arrival fact is read BEFORE the fill
(`EditorViewModel.swift:674`), so the memory is corrected in the same synchronous run; the two persisted
writes per Write entry are the honest cost of my per-caret reporting and are worth the record. And the

`@ObservationIgnored`: the boards' models are per-mount `@State`, so the arm is re-derived at every
entry and cannot outlive a mode switch.

START/END → **QUESTIONS #97**, not disputed on product grounds by me. Nothing here was implemented; the

#### 17o batch b round 1 — dispositions + fixes (the reviewer)

*The cross-model flow's second half (fable-reviewer, 2026-09-08): the final call on each finding, then the fixes implemented here, each pin run RED on the pre-fix tree before the fix landed (the standing-17 order — pin, red, fix, green; piece B's red measured under an in-place revert of its committed mechanisms, the LIVE line below naming the state first), committed per piece, `git pull --rebase` + push after every commit, no trailer lines, zero `Sources/`. The implementer's eleven AGREEs were each re-checked against the code, not adopted on their word; every rider was weighed — adopted where it named a real interaction, refined where it would have re-opened the hole.*

**LIVE — CLOSED: no revert in flight; the tree is clean; the closing run is recorded below.** *(Historical: piece B's red-on-old ran under an in-place hand-revert of `PaneSurface.swift`'s `noteLiveness` transition body, `TakesViewModel.focusWithCaretAtEnd`'s arm line and `BlocksWorkbenchView`'s `caretOnGoingLive` argument to `4dd54c54`'s text — the new `PaneTextView` members kept so the pins compiled — after piece B's commit; restored with `git checkout HEAD --` from the repo root; `git status` clean.)*

**Final calls, in severity order:**

- **R1-1 (BLOCKER) — CONFIRMED · fixed `1ed87482`.** `PostDetailView` clears `pendingCaret` in `.onChange(of: mode)` when the new mode is not `.write` — the Write branch is out of the tree there, so the `.id` that carries the seed changes under nothing mounted (the implementer's rider 2, adopted with its reason in the comment: clearing where the seed is consumed would change that `.id` under a mounted editor and remount it mid-visit). **Pin** `CurrentBlockContinuityTests.aJumpsCaretSeedIsSpentByItsOwnMountAndNeverReLandsALaterOne` — TWO arms (rider 1 adopted): after the jump (`initialBlockID`, the same `pendingCaret` road as Blocks' *open in write*), Write → Takes → Write lands at +6; then Takes → a click into block THREE's pane → Write lands in block 3. **Red on the pre-fix tree** (`scratchpad/r6-r1-1-red.log`): arm 1 `46 == 52` failed — 46 is block 2's START; arm 2 the caret at 46 and `router.focusedBlock` on block 2 (`…0004`) where block 3 (`…0007`) was owed. **Green after** (the suite 4/4).
- **R1-2 (MAJOR) — CONFIRMED · fixed `3ded9683`** (one place with R1-3: the pane coordinator's live transition). `PaneTextView.clickBroughtPlace`, set by `plainClick(at:)` — the ONE plain-click road, which `mouseDown`'s tail calls after `super.mouseDown` and the pins call directly (rider 1 adopted: the ⌘-click claim, the link and the checkbox paths all return before it, so none can set it); the transition leaves the selection alone when a click brought it. **Rider 2 refined, not adopted verbatim:** the flag is spent by ANY transition and by a live render — but KEPT across a read→read render, because the flip a click asks for can take a render to arrive, and an unconditional clear on that intermediate render would re-open the yank the finding traced; a click on a pane that already holds the ring is spent by that live render, so nothing outlives its gesture. **Pin** `PaneSurfaceTests.aClickIntoAReadPaneKeepsTheClicksOwnPlaceOverTheRememberedOne` — **red under the in-place revert:** `30 == 10` failed (the remembered 30 overwrote the click's 10); **green after.**
- **R1-3 (MAJOR) — CONFIRMED · fixed `3ded9683`.** A PROGRAMMATIC transition lands at `caretOnGoingLive ?? 0` — the take's START when nothing is remembered (Write's nil rule; the END AppKit's `string =` left was an artifact the live pane then REPORTED as her place). **The START is built PROVISIONAL — QUESTIONS #97** written as routed (START built, END the alternative, one default to flip; the implementer did not dispute on product grounds). **Rider 1 adopted:** `focusWithCaretAtEnd` now ARMS the END (`Int.max`, clamped in the transition) instead of clearing the arm — a pane born live under the room's request only adopts the caret-to-end token at its first render, so a cleared arm plus the nil-means-START default would have landed it at 0. **Rider 2 adopted:** the Blocks workbench panel passes `caretOnGoingLive` like its siblings and still passes no `onSelectionChange` (a fourth reporter is a design act); the record's note (a) is thereby re-dispositioned — the panel's caret at the END was cosmetic (it reports nothing), and it now lands like every other pane. **Pins:** `PaneSurfaceTests.aProgrammaticTransitionWithNothingRememberedLandsAtTheTakesStart` — **red under the revert:** `64 == 0` failed (64 of 64, the END); `CurrentBlockContinuityTests.aRingLandingWithNothingRememberedLandsAtTheTakesStartAndWriteFollowsIt` — the literal Blocks-row report (nil offset) → ⌘2 → ⌘1: **red under the revert** at every place it asserts — the pane's caret `60 of 60`, the memory's offset never 0, Write at `148` (block 3's END) where `88` (its start) was owed; **green after.** Guard (green both sides, counted as such): `…aProgrammaticTransitionLandsAtTheRememberedPlaceAndIntMaxMeansTheEnd`.
- **R1-4 (MAJOR) — CONFIRMED · fixed `83afcfe7`.** `CommandRouter.persistCurrentBlocks()`, called synchronously at the TOP of `applicationShouldTerminate` (ahead of both branches — the rider's "both branches, not the notification" adopted by construction) and on `macWillLock`'s road (the rider adopted). **The record's capture-11 sentence corrected** by a dated line under the walk table, the implementer's words left as written. **Pin** `LandingOffsetTests.theQuitAndLockDoorsPersistTheCaretPlaceTheSessionDictHolds` — the real delegate door (`VariorumAppDelegate.applicationShouldTerminate(NSApp)`, asserting the synchronous `.terminateNow` branch so the test host is never asked to terminate) and `macWillLock(session:stopServices:)()`. **Red on the pre-fix tree** (`scratchpad/r6-r1-4-red.log`): a fresh `CurrentBlockMemory` read **3** — the block's ENTRY offset — at both doors where 55 and 72 were owed; **green after** (6/6).
- **R1-5 (MAJOR, timing) — CONFIRMED · fixed `97889b66`.** The park is stamped with `overlayGeometryGeneration`; `consumePendingReveal(deliveredBy:)` LEAVES a park whose generation is newer than the hop's (rider 1 adopted — never clears it; hop(G₂) is guaranteed by the bump that parked it). `overlayLayoutDidSettle` internal and `pendingRevealOffset` `private(set)` so the pin can drive the ordering. **Pin** `LandingOffsetTests.aSettleHopForAnOlderGenerationLeavesTheParkedRevealForTheHonestOne` — an unloaded model with a real surface attached (G₁), hop(G₁) queued by hand, a synchronous `applyProjection` under G₂ parking at 1711, a REAL suspension letting the stale hop run; the rider's per-model arm rides it. **Red on the pre-fix tree** (`scratchpad/r6-r1-5-red.log`): `nil == 1711` failed — the stale hop spent the park; **green after** (7/7). *Machinery honesty, recorded for the next session that meets it:* the pin's first two cuts were GREEN on both sides for reasons of their own — (i) a nested `RunLoop.main.run(until:)` cannot re-enter the main dispatch queue from a main-actor job, and a bare `Task.yield()` re-enqueues ahead of the older dispatch block, so the hop never ran (a `Task.sleep` hands the queue back); (ii) the DB read's `await` sat between the hop's scheduling and the park, so the hop ran before there was anything to spend (the read now precedes the hop). Neither was the tree's doing; both are why the finding's window is narrow in practice — and why the stamp is still right: the window is the ordering, not the rig.

#### 17o batch b round 2 — the implementer's responses

*The batch-b implementer (opus-implementer), 2026-09-08: agree/dispute with rationale, traced against
the code at `6e9a5e26` in my own context — nothing implemented, no code or test file touched, the plan
is the only file this edits. **4 AGREED · 0 DISPUTED.** The reviewer makes the final call on each and
implements its confirmed fixes itself.*

**R2-1 (MAJOR — the landing at the note's very END) — AGREE, and the arithmetic falls straight out of
the code, which is why I can agree without re-running it.** Every link traced:

- **The landing really can be `documentLength`.** `bareArrivalLanding` returns
  `min(range.location + min(remembered, range.length), documentLength)`
  (`EditorViewModel.swift:1992–1998`); for the LAST entry `range.location + range.length ==
  documentLength` (no trailing separator — `BlockRangeIndex`'s tiling), and `updateCaretContext`
  attributes an inclusive end to its own block (`caret <= location + entry.length`, `:2823`), so a caret
  resting at the end of the last block reports exactly `entry.length` and the arrival lands at the
  document's end. The reviewer's "with no open tail" qualifier is the right one: an open tail is storage
  past the last entry, and then the landing is short of `documentLength`. So the road is the ordinary
  *"I stopped typing at the end of the note, then ⌘2, then ⌘1"* — not a corner.
- **The nil is structural, not incidental.** `lineSpan` builds `target =
  location(documentRange.location, offsetBy: clamped)` (`JumpPrimitive.swift:123–125`) and enumerates
  `from: target`; the document's END location is inside no element's range, so the callback never fires
  and `span` stays nil at `:146`. `consumePendingReveal` reads `if let span = …` (`:1970`) — nil skips
  the guard entirely rather than failing safe.
- **And the jump cannot correct, by construction.** `jump` runs the SAME enumeration from the SAME
  location inside `JumpCorrectionLoop`'s closure (`:206–213`), so `targetMinY` answers nil on the first
  call and `run` bails at its own `guard let minY … else { return Result(finalTop: top, iterations: 0,
  converged: false) }` (`:41–43`) — **after** the closure has already scrolled. The place it scrolled to
  is `estimate = Double(clamped) / Double(docLength) * textHeight` (`:204`), which for `clamped ==
  docLength` is `1.0 × textHeight`, clamped by `scroll(to:)` to `maxScroll = docHeight − clip.height`
  (`:180`, `:196`). On the reviewer's three-block note: `docHeight 791`, `room 702` ⇒ `textHeight 89`,
  `maxScroll 72` — and the measurement reads `visible 72.0…791.0`. The number is the code's, and it is
  the typewriter room that makes it a full-viewport lurch rather than a few points.
- **It is the batch's own regression, and my walk could not have caught it.** Before the batch a bare
  arrival was always a block's START (Act 10 rows 39/49), so no landing ever sat at the document's end;
  U-20's offset created the road and my reveal is what acts on it. My walk's round trips all started
  from a caret placed by a CLICK inside a paragraph — never from the note's end, which is exactly where
  a writer's caret rests.

**Riders on the fix shape** (the reviewer's shape is right; these are the edges I would want stated):

1. **The adjustment belongs to the enumeration START only.** `enumerateTextLayoutFragments(from: clamped
   − 1 …)` when `clamped == length && length > 0`, while the value handed to
   `FragmentLines.lineIndex(atLocalOffset: max(0, clamped − fragmentStart))` stays `clamped` — whose
   "past the last range ⇒ the last line" rule (`JumpPrimitive.swift:88–93`) is what makes the answer the
   LAST line rather than the second-to-last. Both functions need it; `lineTop` inherits it for free
   because it delegates (`:106`). The `else` arm (`span = (fragmentTop, frame.maxY)`) must stay for a
   fragment that reports no line fragments.
2. **The guard now RUNS at the note's end, which is the whole point** — and it is worth saying in the
   record that the new behaviour is *"nothing moves when the last line is already on screen"*, i.e.
   J18(2)'s accepted rule finally reaching the one offset it could never see. The page will still jump
   when the end is genuinely off screen, which is correct.
3. The 17-pt shortfall (`8647.1` against `maxScroll 8647.5`) is the room's own arithmetic — the estimate
   excludes the room while `maxScroll` includes it — and is pre-existing in every jump. Not worth
   chasing inside this finding.

**On the blast radius the coordinator asked about — can the end-of-document resolution disturb the Phase
6 hybrid jump's other callers, `JumpCorrectionLoopTests`' contract in particular? Traced: no, and in
three independent ways.**

- **`JumpCorrectionLoopTests` cannot be touched by construction.** Its three tests drive the PURE
  `JumpCorrectionLoop.run` with fabricated `targetMinY` closures (`JumpAndUndoTests.swift:20–45`) —
  stable geometry, an exact estimate, shifting estimates within the cap. The fix changes only which
  `NSTextLocation` the closure *inside* `JumpPrimitive.jump` enumerates from; `run`'s signature, its
  tolerance, its iteration cap, its clamp-means-converged rule and its nil-bail are all unmoved. The
  loop's contract is not in the diff's reach.
- **The scroll-anchor callers can never reach the new branch.** `renderFull` restores through
  `JumpPrimitive.jump(toOffset: min(anchor, documentLength) …)` (`EditorSurfaceController.swift:156–162`)
  where the anchor is `topVisibleOffset()` or `editorRouter.scrollAnchors[postID]` — itself a
  `topVisibleOffset()` value. That function returns `fragmentStart + lines[i].characterRange.location`
  (`EditorViewModel.swift:3268–3279`): a LINE START, or nil when `textLayoutFragment(for:)` finds nothing
  (which is precisely what happens when the page is scrolled into the typewriter room). A line start
  equals `documentLength` only for an empty document, where `jump` has already returned at `guard
  docLength > 0` (`:189`). So `renderFull`'s restore and `restoreTopVisibleOffset` (`:3281–3288`) stay
  byte-identical, and the anchor round trip (`topVisibleOffset` ⇄ `jump`, whose exactness the Phase 6
  memo and 13c 1A both rest on) is untouched.
- **No pin anywhere encodes the current end-of-document behaviour.** The three direct `jump` call sites
  in tests all target a block START mid-document — `EditorWindowSmokeTests.swift:59` (block 40, which
  then asserts `topVisibleOffset() == target`), `:120` (block 25), `:233` (`paragraphs / 2`). Nothing
  green today says "a jump to the end lands at `maxScroll`", so nothing can flip red for the wrong
  reason.

**R2-2 (NIT — the flag's doc overclaims) — AGREE.** The two lines really do contradict: the doc at
`PaneSurface.swift:230–233` says *"it can never outlive the gesture that set it"* while `:1003`'s rule
deliberately KEEPS it across a read→read render (R1-2's refinement, which I agreed to and still think is
right). The workbench panel is the host where that has a consequence: its `isLive` is `model.liveBlockID
== row.blockID && !row.variantLocked` (`BlocksWorkbenchView.swift:856`), so a click on a LOCKED take's
pane moves the authority without flipping the pane, and the flag sits armed until that pane's next
transition. Benign for the reason given — the panel passes no `onSelectionChange`, so nothing it holds
reaches the note's memory, and if an in-place unlock later flips it live the click's own place is the
better landing anyway. **Rider: bundle a THIRD stale comment into the same sweep** —
`BlocksWorkbenchView.swift:865–871` still says the panel's pane *"enters the note's current block where

**R2-3 (NIT — #97's (a) quotes retired code) — AGREE.** Verified against `notes/QUESTIONS.md:1212`: (a)
still reads *"`noteLiveness`: `caretOnGoingLive ?? 0`"* and *"the typewriter room still asks for the END

**R2-4 (NIT — the arm and its read duplicated across the two models) — AGREE.** Verified byte-for-byte:

the one that matters: this is the very rule #97 asks her to ratify, and two copies of it are two places a

`Optional<String> == String` is false when the tuple is nil, so the branch cannot be entered — but the

FACT, not the router (`PaneCaretLanding.entering(_ fact: FocusedBlock?, block: String) ->

#### 17o batch b round 2 — dispositions + fixes (the reviewer)

*The cross-model flow's second half (the round-2 fable-reviewer, 2026-09-08): the final call on each finding with every rider weighed, then the fixes implemented in the SHARED tree — the R2-1 pin and fix brought over from the scratch worktree where the red was measured, committed per piece, path-scoped `git add`, `git pull --rebase` + push after each, no trailer lines, zero `Sources/`, no revert in the shared tree (the red-on-old numbers below were measured in the worktree at HEAD before the fix, the worktree removed afterwards). The implementer's four AGREEs were each re-checked against the code; every rider was adopted — none needed refining.*

**Final calls, in severity order:**

- **R2-1 (MAJOR) — CONFIRMED · fixed `5520f33a`.** `JumpPrimitive.lineSpan` and `jump` both enumerate from the character BEFORE the document's end when the offset IS the end (`clamped == length && length > 0`; `jump`'s `docLength > 0` guard already covers the empty case), while the LOCAL offset handed to `FragmentLines.lineIndex(atLocalOffset:)` stays `clamped` — its past-the-last-range rule resolves to the LAST line, never the one before (rider 1 adopted exactly; the `else` arm for a fragment with no line fragments stays). `lineTop` inherits it by delegation. **The new behaviour, said as the implementer asked (rider 2):** nothing moves when the last line is already on screen — J18(2)'s accepted rule reaching the one offset it could never see; the page still jumps when the end is genuinely off screen, and lands the last line at the top. *The flow's last word (the round-2 fable-reviewer, 2026-09-08): the sanity pass's one raise, traced in my own context before the call, the orchestrator's J21 weighed against the code and TAKEN, the pins run RED on the pre-fix tree first, the fix landed, the closing set re-run. Commit: `c60d1d66`.*

- **RAISE-M1 (the cold-mount saved-anchor restore does not restore) — CONFIRMED · fixed `c60d1d66`.** **The mechanism, traced:** `EditorViewModel.topVisibleOffset()` guards on `loaded` (`:3253`), and `loaded` becomes true only AFTER `renderFull` returns (`applyProjection`'s `.initialRender` arm, `:675–676`) — so on every cold mount the live anchor is nil by construction and `renderFull` takes the `saved` branch (`EditorSurfaceController.swift:159–162`), jumping to `editorRouter.scrollAnchors[postID]` against the pre-fill frame, whose `maxScroll` is a couple of dozen points; the jump clamps there (`JumpCorrectionLoop`'s "clamped means converged"), and nothing revisits it: the width-reflow restore (`EditorView.swift:753–762`, `:788`) replays a `stableAnchor` captured only from later scroll events, and the hop restores nothing. The reveal then decides the page alone — re-topping the caret's block when it is off the clamped view (the implementer's M08), and leaving a no-memory arrival at ~24 pt. Pre-existing in shape (the anchor road predates the batch), but the batch's reveal is what turned "unrestored" into "re-topped on every round trip", and her row 7 has a PAGE half that this leaves standing. **J21 taken — fixed here, with the batch's own mechanism:** the cold mount PARKS the saved anchor (`EditorViewModel.parkAnchorRestore`, stamped with the geometry generation exactly as the reveal is; `renderFull` calls it when `!model.loaded`, the `loaded` flag now `private(set)`), and the N14 settle hop consumes it BEFORE `consumePendingReveal` (`overlayLayoutDidSettle`), so the reveal's guard judges "already on screen" against the view she left. A warm re-render's immediate restore is byte-unchanged (its frame is honest — the `model.loaded` branch), the inline book/pool editors refuse the park (no scroll view of their own, as the reveal does), and a stale hop leaves the park by R1-5's rule. **Why it is inside a ladder's reach, checked:** the pure loop is untouched; the width-reflow road (`stableAnchor`) captures from scroll events only, so it cannot fight a hop-parked restore in either order; no pin reads `scrollAnchors` (grep), and every `topVisibleOffset`/`restoreTopVisibleOffset` consumer suite (`ScrollAnchorTests` · `EditorWindowSmokeTests` · `TypewriterRoomTests` · `TypewriterRoomReachabilityTests`) plus `PostDetailModeMemoryTests` (which reads no anchor) and the editor family ran green at the fix (94 tests / 14 suites). Row 20's Blocks jump keeps its top landing (the seeded far block is off the restored view, so the reveal still tops it; `aSeededCaretFarDownALongNoteArrivesInsideTheVisibleRect` green). **Pins (both in `LandingOffsetTests`, both RED on the pre-fix tree — `scratchpad/m1-red2.log`, local — then green):** `aColdMountRestoresTheSessionScrollAnchorAtTheSettleHop` — a 250-paragraph note, the session anchor at block 200's start, no memory, the bare arrival: red `anchor line top 6937.1 · visible 24.0…743.0` (the pre-fill clamp; 6913 pt short), green `visible 6944.1…7663.1` (exact); `aModeRoundTripReturnsToTheViewSheLeftWhenTheCaretIsInsideIt` — the same anchor with the caret remembered at block 203's start + 5 (inside the restored view): red `visible.minY 7060.1 == the caret line's top` (re-topped, 123 pt off the anchor), green the anchor's view `6944.1…7663.1` with the caret's line `7067.1…7084.1` inside it, untouched — her row 7's page half. *(Machinery honesty: the pins' first cut crashed swift-frontend in `SendNonSendable` — the batch record's class; the helper reshaped to Ints and views only, no dictionary `defer` in the async body, compiles.)* **The PRODUCT half — routed, never ruled: QUESTIONS #98** (the remembered caret OFF the restored view: (a) BUILT, the caret's line at the top — the one reveal shape shared with row 20's jump; (b) reveal minimally on a round trip; (c) do not move; one call site named).

#### Findings, severity-ranked

**R3-1 · MINOR · `App/Variorum/Variorum/Features/Editor/EditorViewModel.swift:1890` (`applyFocusSeeds`, the bare arrival's `if landing > 0`) with `:1973–1990` (`parkAnchorRestore` / `consumePendingAnchorRestore`) and `:2924–2925` (the hop's order) · round 1's R1-6 is REVIVED by round 2's RAISE-M1 fix — the dismissal's premise ("the cold-mount anchor restore clamps at 24 pt, so the exemption hides nothing") no longer holds, and a bare arrival whose landing is 0 under a far session scroll anchor now leaves the caret OFF SCREEN: the page returns to the view she left (correct) and no reveal is parked, so "⌘1 then type" (N12's literal promise) lands a keystroke off screen until AppKit's own insertion reveal hauls the page to the top.** Mechanism: `renderFull` parks the saved anchor on the cold mount (`EditorSurfaceController.swift:175`), the hop restores it FIRST (`:2924`) — now exact (`visible.minY == anchorTop`, the RAISE-M1 pin) — then `consumePendingReveal` (`:2925`) finds NOTHING parked, because the bare arrival's arm parks only `if landing > 0` (`:1890`): a real remembered 0 (the caret at the note's very start — a note opened fresh lands there with no memory, and a click at the start puts it there), a no-offset fact on the first block, or no fact at all, all land at 0 and skip `revealLanding`. QUESTIONS #98 records (a) as BUILT — "when the remembered caret is NOT inside the restored view … the caret's line lands at the viewport TOP" — and the code honors it at every landing except 0, where it behaves as #98's (c) instead; the seed arm (`:1826–1839`) and the intent arm reveal 0 unconditionally, so the bare arrival is the one road that differs. Concrete road: Boscombe opened fresh (the caret at block 1's start), scroll by trackpad to block 200 reading (no click — nothing reports), ⌘2, ⌘1 → the page comes back at block 200 with the caret at the top of the note, off screen. **Measured (the worktree, a probe arm on `mountedLongNoteWithAnchor(anchorEntry: 199, memoryEntry: 0, memoryOffset: 0)`): RED on `5e5e7c3c`** — `caret 0 · caret line 24.0…41.0 · anchor top 6944.1 · visible 6944.1…7663.1 · selected 0`; and **GREEN under the in-place revert of RAISE-M1's fix** — `caret line 24.0…41.0 · visible 24.0…743.0`, which is round 1's R1-6 hollow measurement reproduced exactly, so the revival is the fix's own doing and nothing else's. Pre-existing in shape (the exemption predates the batch), reachable only since `c60d1d66`. **Pin:** `LandingOffsetTests` — the RAISE-M1 helper with `memoryEntry: 0, memoryOffset: 0` (a real remembered 0 on block 1 under the block-200 anchor), the bare arrival mounted, after the settle `JumpPrimitive.lineSpan(ofOffset: 0, in: editor)` inside `scrollView.contentView.bounds` (red today: the line at 24 against a viewport at 6944); the no-anchor 0 case stays a guard (`aSeededCaretTheViewportAlreadyShowsDoesNotScrollThePage`, `:451`, green both sides). **Fix (R1-6's own shape, one condition):** drop the `landing > 0` exemption — `revealLanding(landing, textView:)` unconditionally on the bare arrival; the reveal's visibility guard already moves nothing when the line is on screen, so a 0 with no anchor stays put (its line at 24 is inside 0…719), and a 0 off the restored view lands at the top per #98(a). Cost: one single-fragment `lineSpan` per bare arrival at 0 (P1 unmoved — the same `ensuresLayout` the jump performs). If she rules #98 (b) or (c) the same call site changes shape; the exemption's removal is right under every option, because under (c) the reveal at 0 should be the guard's decision, not a special case's. **Routing (the orchestrator's):** a one-condition fix with a pin — a 17o batch d row beside the cap's other residue, or the closeout commit; the round-1 dispositions' R1-6 line should gain a dated sentence that its premise ended at `c60d1d66`.

**R3-2 · NIT · `App/Variorum/Variorum/Surface/PaneSurface.swift:713–715` (the doc on `PaneSurfaceView.caretOnGoingLive`, `:718`) · the parameter's own doc still says "nil means the take's START (Write's nil rule …)" and "`Int.max` asks for the END explicitly (the room's gesture)" — the `Int?`-era sentences, with the RAISE-2 sentence appended after them; the type is `PaneCaretLanding`, which has neither a nil nor an `Int.max`.** The R2-3 class (a comment quoting retired code), one the R2-2/R2-3 sweep missed — it fixed the flag's doc, the workbench panel's comment and #97's text. Two newlines: elements `[(0,22),(22,44),(44,45)]`, `lineSpan(45) = (89, 106)`, the caret rect 65 + 24 = 89. Through the fill (`withEditor(profile: .journal, seedBlocks: ["…\n"])`): `documentLength 44`, no open tail, `bareArrivalLanding` = 44, `lineSpan(44)` the empty line. #### ROW 16 — MEASURED FIRST, AND IT IS REAL (not hollow)

The brief said *"TRACE FIRST — measure before believing"*, and the measurement **changed the fix**. The
brief's proposed remedy — *"run `ensureLayout(for: documentRange)` on the PANE after a SHRINKING edit
then remeasure"* — **is already what the shipped code does on every measurement**
(`PaneMeasurement.height` called it before reading), and it does **not** fix the row. Recorded because
the next reader would otherwise repeat the guess.

**The probe** (a live pane, 400 pt wide, 30 lines of prose; lines 6–30 deleted through the real
responder path — select + `deleteBackward`):

| turn | text | `usageBoundsForTextContainer` | `PaneMeasurement.height` | the pane's frame |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| mounted | 30 lines | 1223.0 | 1235.0 | 1235.0 |
| after the delete | 5 lines | 1189.0 | 1201.0 | 1235.0 |
| +1 turn … +6 turns | 5 lines | **1189.0 (unmoved)** | **1201.0 (unmoved)** | **1201.0 (unmoved)** |

So a five-line take kept a **1201-pt frame** — ~1000 pt of dead gap under it — and no number moved for
six main-queue turns. That is her *"empty gap… until you switch modes"* exactly: only a remount (a mode
switch) mints a fresh text container, and a fresh container starts its high-water mark over.

**The mechanism, named:** on macOS 26 `NSTextLayoutManager.usageBoundsForTextContainer` is a **HIGH-WATER
MARK for its container's life** — it grows and never shrinks. Two remedies were measured and BOTH
failed: the `ensureLayout(for: documentRange)` already in the measurement (1189 → 1189), and an explicit
`invalidateLayout(for: documentRange)` ahead of it (1189 → 1189). What tracks the text is the fragments'
own geometry: after the same deletion the last laid-out fragment's bottom read **198.0**, and after ten
lines were typed back it read **608.0**, while the property sat at 1189 throughout — so the ratchet is
one-way in BOTH senses (a pane that shrank could not grow back to an honest number either).

**The fix (ONE place — the one measurement brain, P-3):** `PaneMeasurement` reads the laid-out
fragments' own bottom instead of the property, falling back to the property when a document lays out no
fragment at all (the EMPTY document — the only shape where the two differ).

**Why it moves no converged pane by a point** — measured at steady state on eight shapes:

| shape | property | fragments |
|---|---|---|
| empty | 17.0 | 0.0 → falls back to 17.0 |
| one line | 17.0 | 17.0 |
| trailing newline | 41.0 | 41.0 |
| two trailing newlines | 58.0 | 58.0 |
| blank lines between paragraphs | 113.0 | 113.0 |
| one long wrapping line | 340.0 | 340.0 |
| 30 lines | 1223.0 | 1223.0 |
| 400 lines | 16393.0 | 16393.0 |

**Cost (P1, measured):** 0.58 ms vs 0.41 ms per measurement on a 400-line / 16,393-pt pane; 0.12 ms at
30 lines. `.ensuresLayout` does the layout the old call did; the added work is one frame read per
fragment, and a pane's document is ONE take by construction — the bound is the unit on screen, which is
the same P1 argument `PaneSurface.swift` already makes in its header for full-document `ensureLayout`.

**The pin:** `TakePaneMeasureTests.aPaneThatLosesMostOfItsTextShrinksWithinOneTurn` — mounts a live
30-line pane, deletes 25 lines through the responder path, and asserts the frame falls below a third of
its old height within ONE main-queue turn (plus a regrow arm, and two non-vacuity assertions). Beside it,
`aFreshPanesMeasurementIsUnchangedByRow16sFix` states the no-regression half (green on both sides
deliberately — it is not the pin). **RED-ON-OLD PROVED by behavioral revert** (the fragment read swapped
back for the property, on the committed tree): 2 issues —
`(short → 1201.0) < (tall / 3 → 411.67)` failed, and the regrow arm
`(pane.bounds.height → 1201.0) > (short + 200 → 1401.0)` failed. Green again on restore.

**For 13u2's kickoff (row 32, the general pane-stability trace — this row is item 1's member):** the
high-water mark above is a *class*, not a one-off. Anything in the app that sizes from
`usageBoundsForTextContainer` inherits it; the full-document EDITOR is the other surface kind and was
NOT examined here (out of this batch's one surface). 13u2 should start from that question.
