#### ROW 6 — THE RING FOLLOWS THE PREFERENCE, THROUGH THE ONE COMPONENT

**Her U-7, verbatim:** *"the take mode always shows a ring around what piece you're editing — it doesn't
follow the preference on cursor being enough, wanting background wash, or ring"*; **U-19 (5)** confirms
the preference governs EVERY live pane.

**The traced mechanism (confirmed by reading, then by the revert):** `PaneSurfaceView.strokeColor`
returned `tokens.ring` whenever `isLive` and read no setting at all, while the Write surface has honoured
the three styles since R14/R22 (`BlockDecor.renderIndicator`, `:513–535`: `.off` draws nothing,
`.outline` strokes `tokens.ring`, `.background` fills `tokens.wash`). So the setting she chose was
honoured in Write and overridden in every pane.

**R22's reason, recorded as the brief requires — and PRESERVED, not overruled.** R22's ring exists
because *among many editable surfaces the ring is how you find the one live editor*, and that is exactly
what `.outline` still does: it is the shipped look, it stays the default, and it is what a pane wears
wherever the appearance push has not reached (the pins, the tutorial samples). What her ruling changes is
narrower and right: the pane may no longer OVERRIDE a preference she set — under `.off` the caret is
enough (her words), and under `.background` the live pane wears the same wash Write puts behind the caret
block, in the same token. Nothing about "find the live editor" is lost; the mark just obeys her.

**The shape — ONE decision, in the ONE component (P-3):** `PaneDecoration` (`Surface/PaneSurface.swift`)
is a pure `resolve(isLive:indicator:tokens:)` returning stroke · lineWidth · fill; `PaneSurfaceView`
renders it (a `.background` fill and an `.overlay` stroke on the SAME rounded shape, so the two can never
disagree and the composition's padding stays the card's). No host contains ring or wash logic.

**The push (the brief's road, followed exactly):** the style rides in beside `tokens` —
`PaneSurfaceView.indicator`, defaulting to `.outline` — the way `EditorAppearance` already carries theme
facts into the Write surface. A **View** reads the store; the component never pulls a setting. The
fallback is a decision, so it has exactly ONE copy: `ThemeStore.editingIndicator(of:)`, which every host
calls. **All TEN production construction sites in eight files pass it** (audited by grep after the
change): `TakesShared` (the cell both boards compose) · `TakesBoardView` ×2 (the mini card, the stack
card) · `CompareBoardView` ×2 · `CanvasView` · `WriteTakesPanel` · `ReadEditPane` · `BookIndexView` ·
`BlocksWorkbenchView`. Lenses reaches every pane through `TakesCellView`, so it needs no site of its own.

**The three hosts that drew their OWN ring now CONSUME the decision** (`PaneDecoration.hostRing`, nil ⇒
"draw the frame you draw at rest"): `ReadEditPane.swift` (the overlay the brief names), `BookIndexView`
(the same shape, found by grepping `tokens.ring` — the brief named only Read's), and
`BlocksWorkbenchView`'s row card (`ring : line` — the third, same grep). No second rule survives in the
app.

**Pins** (`PaneSurfaceTests`): `theLivePanesDecorationFollowsTheIndicatorPreference` (the three styles
plus the read-pane arm across all cases) · `aHostsOwnFrameConsumesTheSameRule` ·
`aPaneWithNoPushKeepsTheShippedRing` (the default is a decision, so it is stated) ·
`theIndicatorNeverMovesThePanesHeight` (mounted under all three — the decoration is a background and an
overlay, so it can never re-open the batch-12 clipped-take class one setting over).
**RED-ON-OLD proved by behavioral revert** (the pre-fix rule restored: the ring whatever the setting):
**3 issues** — `off → PaneDecoration(stroke: #3F7FD6FF, lineWidth: 1.5, fill: nil)` ≠ the resting
hairline; `background.fill → nil` ≠ `tokens.wash → #EEF0EAFF`; and `background.stroke == rest.stroke →
false`. Green again on restore.

**Stated honestly:** the three HOSTS' own frames are walked, not pinned — the rig cannot mount those
whole surfaces (KnownIssues' rig row; the brief's own "pin brains, walk faces"). The rule they consume is
pinned; that they consume it is a one-line read and a capture.

#### ROW 14 — THE CHOSEN BAR RUNS THE CARD'S FULL HEIGHT

**Her punchlist 11, verbatim:** *"the chosen green line doesn't go all the way down"* (image 6 —
`greenchosenlinedoesntgoallthewaydown.png`, which I read: the STACK style, wash on, block 4/8, a 581-word
chosen take whose card runs past the viewport while the green bar stops about a third of the way down).

**The traced mechanism:** `TakeCardFrame` drew the 3-pt bar as an HStack SIBLING of the content. A
flexible `Color` inside a stack is sized against the height the stack has RESOLVED — and beside a
`PaneSurfaceView` whose measured height publishes on a LATER main-queue turn (`PanePublisher`'s
deliberate deferral, and the A47b FRAME LEVER which moves the AppKit frame directly), the height the
stack resolved with is a previous one. The card's own background and border chase the settled content;
the sibling bar does not.

**The fix:** the bar is an `.overlay(alignment: .leading)` of the finished card (the brief's own
prescription), laid out against the card's resolved height rather than beside it, so it is the card's
height by construction however many turns the pane takes to settle. The content keeps its exact leading
inset (the 3 pt the bar's column used to occupy is now a `.padding(.leading, 3)`), so nothing moves.

**The geometry oracle** (`TakeCardGeometry`, the `LensesGeometry` DEBUG pattern one surface over): the
bar and the card each report their frame in the card's own coordinate space; the oracle keeps EVERY
report rather than last-one-wins, because a board draws many cards.

**MEASURED, both sides, by behavioral revert (the HStack sibling restored in place — no `git checkout`
this time):**

| arm | pre-fix | fixed |
|---|---|---|
| the real STACK board (her image 6's surface), tallest card vs tallest bar | **card 1246.0 · bar 1220.5** | **card 1246.0 · bar 1246.0** |
| a bare synthetic 40-line card, ten settle turns | equal | equal |

**Stated honestly:** the SYNTHETIC arm is green on both sides — a bare mount settles and the pre-fix
HStack catches up, so it states the law without catching the defect. It is kept for the law (a future
card composition that never reaches a board still owes it) and the arm that CATCHES the row is
`theChosenBarRunsTheWholeCardOnTheRealStackBoard`. The board arm compares MAXIMA, because the oracle
cannot say which bar belongs to which card; `max(bar) < max(card)` still means some card's bar falls
short, which is her report exactly.

**Pins:** `TakesCardHeightTests.theChosenBarRunsTheWholeCardOnTheRealStackBoard` (the pin) ·
`…theChosenBarRunsTheWholeHeightOfALongCard` (the law). Suites at this commit:
`TakesCardHeightTests` · `WriteTakesPanelTests` · `TakesBoardTests` · `TakesPresentationTests` ·
`TakesBoardSoakTests` — **38/5 green** (`WriteTakesPanel` composes `TakeCardFrame` too).

#### ROW 15 — THE FIRST BLOCK CAN BE MADE WHERE SHE WAS LOOKING

**Her punchlist 10, verbatim:** *"you should be able to make a first block there"* (image 3 —
`cantstartintakes.png`, which I read: an empty note in Takes, slot-split style, offering the line
*"Nothing here yet — blocks are born by typing."* and exactly one door, *"Start writing (Write mode)"*).

**The traced mechanism:** the empty state of BOTH takes-shaped boards rendered one door — into Write —
and the article's own `＋ new block` door was gated on `!slots.isEmpty` (`TakesBoardView`), so it
appeared only once a block already existed. The CAPABILITY was never missing: `.addBlockAtEnd` is native
to the take family (`CapabilityRegistry`, availability `takeFamily`, which does not look at the block
count) and it is already on the bottom bar and the menu bar. What was missing is the DOOR on the surface
she was looking at — a P-2 shape: the one place a first block is wanted was the one place it could not
be made.

**The fix:** ONE shared face, `TakesEmptyStateView` in `TakesShared.swift` (that file's stated purpose —
the grammar both boards share, in one home, so two empty states can never drift). It renders
`TakesEmptyState.doors == [.newBlock, .write]`: the new-block door leads, wearing the SAME dashed
grammar the article's end-of-note door already wears (so the act reads as the one she knows), with
"Start writing (Write mode)" beside it, byte-unchanged in style. Both boards compose it; neither keeps a
copy.

**Copy truth (J8's rule).** The old line *"Nothing here yet — blocks are born by typing."* stopped being
the whole truth the moment a door here mints one, so it now reads **"Nothing here yet — make the first
block here, or start writing."**

**The act is the existing road, with no new mechanism:** the door calls `model.addBlockAtEnd()`, whose
second half already parks focus (`TakesChoreographer.parkFocus`) so the minted pane takes the ring when
its slot arrives — P3's ONE live pane through `PaneTextAuthority`, exactly as the brief requires. The
minted block is legal EMPTY (I10: one live variant, empty text), and 18c's Return rule makes it an
ordinary block from the first keystroke.

**Pins** (`TakesEmptyStateTests`, a new suite):
`theTakesBoardOffersAFirstBlockOnAnEmptyNote` · `theLensesBoardOffersAFirstBlockOnAnEmptyNote` — the
REAL boards mounted on a blockless note, reading a DEBUG door oracle (`TakesEmptyStateDoors`, the
`LensesGeometry` pattern) because a SwiftUI button on a whole board is not an `NSView` a pin can find and
the real-click rig cannot mount a board (KnownIssues' rig row; "pin mechanisms, walk faces") ·
`bothBoardsRenderTheOneDoorList` · `theDoorMintsTheFirstBlockAndItsPaneTakesTheRing` (the act at the
model: one block, one live EMPTY variant, and the ring on its pane).
**RED-ON-OLD proved by behavioral revert** (the new-block door filtered out of the rendered list, in
place): **2 issues** — both boards recorded `[write]` and failed `.contains(.newBlock)`. Green again on
restore. Suites at this commit: `TakesEmptyStateTests` · `TakesBoardTests` · `LensesReorderTests` ·
`TakesArticleRenderTests` — **23/4 green**.

**A trap I hit, recorded so the next session does not** (§8's own warning, *"never `git checkout` over
uncommitted work"*): after the row-6 revert probe I restored three files with `git checkout --`, which
discarded the row-6 work in them because it was not yet committed — `PaneSurface.swift`,
`ReadEditPane.swift`, `BookIndexView.swift` went back to the row-16 commit. Nothing was lost (the edits
were re-made from the same scripts and the grep audit + suites re-run), but the rule is exactly right:
**a revert probe must be run on a COMMITTED tree, or in a scratch worktree.** Row 16's probe was safe
because its file was committed first; row 6's was not.

#### WHAT THE LETTER'S CLOSEOUT INHERITS FROM THIS BATCH

- **A canon delta IS owed, and it is small.** AppSpec §12's Phase 13 surfaces paragraph records the
  editing indicator as *"the editing indicator a setting (off/outline/background — R14/R22)"* inside the
  **editor language** list, where it reads as Write's alone. Her U-7 + U-19 (5) make it govern **every
  live pane**, which is a widening of a shipped ruling's reach, not a new behaviour — one clause, the
  orchestrator's to place. **Nothing else moves:** no schema (the setting is the shipped
  `EditingIndicatorStyle` on the synced `setting` row), no operation, no invariant.
- **No new QUESTIONS entry.** Nothing here needed a decision the spec and §1 do not make. The one place
  I could have invented — what `.off` and `.background` MEAN on a pane — is answered by `BlockDecor`'s
  shipped three-way rule, which I copied rather than re-designed.
- **Two judgment calls for the orchestrator's J-list.** **(1)** The indicator rides an explicit
  parameter beside `tokens` (the brief's *"the tokens already ride that road — follow them exactly"*)
  rather than a SwiftUI environment value. The parameter is greppable and all ten sites are audited;
  the environment road would have needed no per-host line at all. Both fail SAFE to `.outline` (the
  shipped ring) where the push does not reach, so the choice is about drift risk, not behaviour — and
  the fallback lives in ONE place (`ThemeStore.editingIndicator(of:)`) precisely so it cannot drift.
  **(2)** Row 16's fix moves the app's ONE measurement brain — the widest blast radius in the batch —
  so it is fenced by measurement rather than by argument: eight steady-state shapes measured identical,
  the empty document explicitly falling back to the old property, and the cost measured at both ends.
- **Mobile parity (for the round's ONE line):** the phone owes a version of **"the live pane's mark
  follows the editing-indicator preference"** — its take surfaces read the same synced
  `editingIndicator` setting and should honour off / outline / background the way the Mac's panes now
  do. Nothing here is `Shared/`, so the phone inherits nothing by construction. Rows 14 · 15 · 16 are
  Mac-surface-specific (the card frame, the boards' empty states, TK2 on macOS 26) — the phone should be
  CHECKED for row 16's class (whether iOS's `usageBoundsForTextContainer` ratchets the same way) rather
  than assumed clean.
- **13u coordination:** this batch built **13u2 item 1's member row 16** and hands that phase the
  measured fact above — the high-water mark is a CLASS, and anything in the app sizing from
  `usageBoundsForTextContainer` inherits it. The full-document EDITOR is the other surface kind and was
  deliberately NOT examined (out of this batch's one surface); 13u2's pane-stability trace should start
  there. Row 32 stays 13u2's. Nothing else in the 13u fence was touched.

**Batch c CLOSED at this commit. Everything is committed and pushed; no revert is in flight; the tree is
clean.**
